# Tirunedunthandakam

## 1. 1

*minnuruvāy munnuruvil vēdam nāngāy   viḷakkoḷiyāy muḷaitteḻunda tingaḷ tānāy pinnuruvāy munnuruvil piṇi mūppillāp   piṟappiliyāy iṟappadaṟkē eṇṇādu eṇṇum ponnuruvāy maṇiyuruvil bhūtam aindāy   punaluruvāy analuruvil tigaḻum sōdi tannuruvāy ennuruvil ninṟa endai   taḷir puraiyum tiruvadi en talai mēlavē.                            1*

**Пословный перевод:**

mun uruvil – Things that can be seen by eyes (non-sentient); minnuruvāy – He is being one who enlightens about the nature of them (like the temporary nature of) lightning; vēdam nāngāy – He is being talked about by all the four vēdas; viḷakkoḷiyāy – Like how light would remove darkness, He removes the inner darkness that is ignorance (of true nature of ātmā, etc.), and enlightens about His true nature/form; muḷaittu eḻunda – appearing in the mountain, and rising up in the sky; tingaḷ tānāy – like the moon, He is being of delight; pinnuruvāy – being behind that non-sentient, that tatvam (truth/existence) (which is jeevātmā); munnuruvil – unlike the aforementioned shining lamp and moon; piṇi mūppillāp piṟappiliyāy – He is being the matter of (parama)ātmā which does not have ignorance, weakness, the six changes of body, or birth; iṟappadaṟkē eṇṇādu – and without thinking about death (kaivalyam (eternal self enjoyment) which is considered as death of ātmā); eṇṇum – and He is eligible for always to meditate upon; ponnuruvāy – having the nature of gold (brightness, desirable, etc); maṇiyuruvil – in the form (body) having the nature of gem; bhūtam aindāy – and being made of five primary elements (panca bhūtam) (different from the five elements of this world); punal uruvāy – and being very easy to approach like the water that everyone could fetch and drink; analuruvil tigaḻum – and having the form (body) of not able to approach like fire; sōdi tannuruvāy – and being one who illuminates those things that are having light; en uruvil ninṟa – He being set in my body; tiruvadi – divine feet (soft, etc.); endai – of the sarvēśvaran who is my lord; en talai mēlavē – are being set on my head; taḷir puraiyum – due to which His feet freshens (sprouts)

**Перевод:**

**munnuruvil – pinnuruvāy –  In the things that are seen around before the eyes (this is about non-sentient things since they are seen by eyes), and in those that are learned, He showed them to be having the nature of lightning. That is, He showed the unstable nature of material realm and related things (‘minnin nilaiyila \[tiruvāimoḻi 1.2.2\]’);**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In this pāsuram āḻvār is divining about the series of help of emperumān, starting from removing dēhatmābhimānam (giving importance to body than ātmā; thinking of body itself as ātmā that is to be taken care of) (minnuruvāy), up to His giving association towards emperumān’s divine feet (tiruvadi en talai mēlavē).*

When the reasons for bondage of ātmā with *prakruti* (material realm / *acit* / non-sentient) are *different*, (that is, either due to (a) the good and bad deeds of earlier births, or due to (b) kindness of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); the latter being the cause of incarnation of [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)s and ācāryas); (this is by *jāyamāna kāla katākśam* (giving His grace at the time of birth, like he gave to prahlādan, druvan, [tiruppāṇāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thiruppANAzhvAr/)) to those of good deeds; and by giving ‘*mayaṟvaṟa mati nalam* \[[tiruvāimoḻi 1.1.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/)\]’ to [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)s),

then the understanding of nature of ātmā *is also different* (for different sentient)*.*

That is, when ātmā is bound to a body due to karmas, the knowledge of nature of ātmā and paramātmā would stay hidden from the person; and such bondage would hide the nature of the body which is impermanent and incapable, and make it appear to have permanence and enjoyability;

but, when bound to the body based on [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s grace, it would enlighten about the impermanence and lack of enjoyability in one’s own body and own things, and would enlighten about the true nature of ātmā and paramātmā (that ātmā is subservient to paramātmā ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), etc).

So, here (for [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)), since it is based on grace of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), it shined light on the impermanence of *prakruti* and things based on *prakruti,* and [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) got the understanding that such non-sentient things are to be shunned.

But, the *prakruti* (body, and related things) are said as ‘*vicitrā dēha sampatti: eeśvarāya nivēditum \| pūrvamēvakrutā’* ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) gave the wealth that is the body of multi-faceted capabilities, to worship [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), and,  as ‘*śareeramādyam kalu dharma sādanam*’ (first instrument that is the means for doing dharma is the body), and as ‘*puruśārta sādanam’* (is an instrument to attain the destiny); so is it correct to say that prakruti etc., are to be shunned?

Like the vessel/plantain leaf that is used for holding food becomes untouchable after eating of that food, and, like after completing the rites, things like pole where animals used for the rites were tied, that pole etc., are not to be touched be the performer,  this body etc., are also to be shunned after the birth of knowledge about ātmā and paramātmā.

The *sāmānādikaraṇyam* (condition of a property in same object (example: ‘that man is *happiness’* implies that he always exhibits happiness, so, that man himself could be called as *happiness*)) that is said in ***minnuruvāy***  is *upakārya upakāra bhāva sambhanda nibhandanam* – that is, one who helped us understand the nature of lightning is [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); the nature He showed us of is the lightning. By this connection, [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is talked about as *minnuruvāy* ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) helped by *showing* the transient nature of prakruti as similar to lightning).

In these ways as these other things are impermanent, the knowledge shall be directed towards the ātmā which is of permanent nature.

*aham* – (me) – (is the thought that happens by perceiving self) – which is ātmā – some would say that ātmā is body, or senses (can’t be because for example, if eyes are lost, one still continues activities/knowledge etc.), or mind, or breath, or intellect – for such mis-assertions to be removed, and for true knowledge to be born, it is required to have śāstram (*vēdam*), so He *gave* śāstram – so says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) now –

***vēdam nāngāy –*** vēdas which are four, the aim of which is to show the essence of knowledge about ātmā (not just about intellect, but showing up to the level that ātmā is subservient and obedient to paramātmā);

(If there is only knowledge in someone (*jñātrutvam*), then one would tend to do actions on their own (*kartrutvam)*, and would seek enjoyment (*bhōktrutvam*);  (these three in such form are to be carefully shunned); when there is subservience and obedience (*pāratantriyam*) then one would depend on others (devotees/ācāryan) and do according to such people’s acts/commands).

Is vēdam just only about explaining ātmā (and not non-sentient, or *paramātmā*)?  The other two are talked about only for ātmā to know what is to be shunned (*non-sentient)* and what is to be accepted (*paramātmā)*, (that is, *vivēkam*) so yes, vēdam is only mainly about explaining the nature of ātmā.

If so, is it not enough to only talk about what is to be rejected and accepted, why talk about true nature of ātmā?  Since it is the true nature (being subservient/obedient to devotees/ācāryan) that determines what are to be shunned or accepted, (so) when learning about what is to be shunned as appropriate for the true nature, and what is to be accepted as appropriate for the true nature, one would have to learn about true nature (of ātmā), isn’t it?

When not having knowledge about true nature of ātmā, one would think that only the body is to be shunned, but would think that sons, cows (wealth), food, etc., are to be accepted/enjoyed; or, if understanding is only that ātmā is different from body, then one would think that sons, cows (wealth), food, etc., are to be shunned but the enjoyment of heaven is to be pursued (for ātmā’s enjoyment);

Since śāstram is given for the sentient, it is about the sentient; Since eeśvaran (paramātmā) by Himself is all-knowing, and since the reality of non-sentience is unintelligent, śāstram is not required for these two. Thus, *śāstram* shall appear only for the sentient as one is expectant of and obedient to *śāstram* and, as he applies the knowledge gained (*jñātā*).

(*vēdayati iti vēda:* ) Since it explains clearly about itself it is called vēdam.  vēdam shows its meanings to those who believe it and wish to learn it; (*bhōddum icccu: – bubutsu:* -\> one having interest in understanding vēdam);  it would hide its meanings to those who do not follow it, as said in ‘*maṟaiyāya nāl vēdam* \[[tiruvāimoḻi 3.1.10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thiruvaimozhi-3-1-10-maraiyaya/)\]’.  The nature of *pramēyam* (who is talked about; [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) who is said as ‘*pattudai adiyavarkku eḷiyavan; piṟargaḷukku ariya vittagan* \[[tiruvāimoḻi 1.3.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-1-paththudai-adiyavar/)\]’  – such nature would be present for *pramāṇam* too.  (*pramāṇam -\>* what talks about (Him)).

Here the *sāmānādikaraṇyam* (equivalence) said in ***vēdan nāngāy***,  equating vēdam to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), is Not based on association of what talks (vēdam) and what is talked about ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) that is said in ‘*vēdaiśca sarvairahamēva vēdya:* \[[śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/) – 15.15\]*’* (it is I who is known by all the vēdas (sacred texts)).

It is based on what is said in ‘*mānam pradeepamiva kāruṇikō dadāti’*  ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is of utmost mercy gave the lamp (that shows things that are to be shunned or accepted/followed)) – this association mentioned as ***vēdam nāngāy*** is based on giver/what is given (vēdam), that is, helper/helped through vēdam (*pradātru  pradēya nibandanam)*.

Next, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) says that He created in me the knowledge born from hearing śāstram (vēdam) from ācāryan.

**viḷākkoḷiyāy –** Since it is having the nature of light of lamp ***viḷākkōḷi***  implies knowledge born out of śāstram. That is, lamp is, one that removes darkness and shows things (as is), śāstram removes ignorance and shows true nature;

As said in ‘*andam tamaiva ajñānam deepavacca indriyōtbhavam*’ (having no knowledge is like darkness that renders one blind/ignorant; knowledge obtained out of senses (the five cognitive senses – smelling, tasting, seeing, touching, and hearing) (*jñānēndriyam*) are like the lamp (that remove darkness (lack of knowledge)), knowledge based on hearing (from ācāryan) is said as a lamp (here, the sense of hearing is talked about because of previous phrase ***vēdam nāngāy*****,** vēdam which is learned by hearing).  (The knowledge is obtained  through *karmēndriyams* also but it does not get understood; it requires *jñānēndriyams* for one to gain the knowledge).

*Also, ‘hartum tama: sat asat eeca vivēktumeeśō mānam pradeepamiva kāruṇikō dadāti’* (to remove the darkness, and to show what is true and what is false, [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) with kindness comes with eagerness like the light from a lamp, and gives śāstram).

By this, ‘***viḷakkoḷiyāy*****’** is about ‘*śrōtavya:’* (listen to (about ātmā)), that is *śravaṇam* (hearing (from ācāryan)). (Knowledge gained by hearing – this level of knowledge is like a moon just about rising – a small part of it is visible/known).

By *‘****mūḷaittu eḻunda tingaḷ tānāy’***  ***–*** it says about  *mantavya:* (reflect up on it (*mananam*) so that what is heard becomes clearer),

and about *nididyāsitavya:*  (meditate upon its meanings so that its true delicate meanings and implications are understood well),

and about *draśtavya:* (get visualization in mind about  nature of *jeevātmā* (*sākṣātkāram*)).

***muḷaittu*** **–** Like the moon seen at the tip of a mountain, the knowledge that comes from stage of *mananam* is brighter than the lamp shown above as example for *śravaṇam* (hearing).

***eḻundu –*** Like the moon which has risen above the tip of a mountain into the sky would be even brighter, compared to the stage of *mananam* this talks about the next stage of *nidityāśam*.

***tingaḷ tānāy –*** This is talking about *sākṣātkāram*.  How so is – by ‘*tānāna tingaḷ’* (moon by itself) – is about moon considered by itself and not considering the blemish that came due to association with earth (earth’s shadow on the moon is considered the blemish on the moon, but by itself moon is considered pure), so it is about the matter of *ātmā* when considered without direct association with *prakruti* (material realm), and which is distinct from such *prakruti*.  *sākśātkāram* can occur only when we see the pure nature of *ātmā*, isn’t it? (so this is about *sākṣātkāram*). (He who gave such sākśātkāram) (sākśātkārām – also about *paramātma sākśātkāram*).

By giving example of moon as example – it shows the *svayam prakāśatvam* of ātmā (being self-illuminous), and, as moon (*candra*) is having the root wording as ‘*cadi āhlādē’* (where *cadi* means happiness, *cadi* becomes *candra* by śamskruta grammar rules),  and so implies one that gives/having happiness, which is applicable for ātmā;

and since the knowledge gained (by [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)s) is by the grace of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), that is, gained easily without difficulty, he ([tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/)) is taking moon as example. In ‘*yatā sūryas tatā jñānam’*  where knowledge is compared to Sun, is about the knowledge gained with great difficulty by own efforts by *upāsakan* (one who tries by own efforts).

***pin uṟuvāy –*** the tatvam (reality) that comes *after* that (after the non-sentient / material realm (*prakruti)* talked about earlier).

As said in ‘*panca vimśōyam puruśa:’* (jeevātmā is twenty fifth *tatvam (after* the twenty four tatvams related to material non-sentient realm), ‘*pancavimśa ātmā bhavati’* (After the twenty four tatvams comes the twenty fifth which is ātmā), such ātmā which is twenty fifth *tatvam* (reality) is clearly seen by (mind’s) eyes, it is beyond and different from the twenty four *tatvams* of material realm.

It is said too ‘*dēhēndriya mana: prāṇa deebhya: anya: ananya sādana:* \| *nitya: vyāpee prati kṣētram ātmā bhinna: svatassukhee’*  (ātmā is different from body, senses, mind, breath, intellect, and it does not have any other means (than [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/));  ātmā is eternal,  ātmā is pervasive in each body, separate ātmā is present in individual bodies (*kṣētram* is land/field; it is used for growing grains; this body is also called *kṣētram* which is used for growing the effects of good and bad deeds); ātmā is happy by itself.  (But ātmā is a holder of intellect; that is why it is affected by good and bad that happens through the body. When ātmā leaves the body, the body would not have intellect and so would not react to pain, etc.).

[āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) says next that such ātmā would not be affected by the blemishes of body that is pervaded by ātmā  (blemishes are change in shape, death, sorrow, happiness, etc).

***mun uruvil piṇi mūppillāp piṟappiliyāy –*** *mun uru* is reference to the aforementioned *prakruti prākrutam* (material realm/element, and items derived from it (like body, house, etc.)) that was explained as those which are seen *before* the eyes or perceived.  The entity that is ātmā (by nature) is not related to sadness/sickness, aging, birth, etc.  By this, it talks about ignorance (which leads to sadness), etc., as well. By ageing and birth, it talks about all the six changes that occur to a body (*garbha* (in womb), *janma* (birth), *bālya* (toddler), *yauvana* (youth), *vārddakya* (old age), *maraṇa* (death)).

Ascertaining the nature of ātmā in ‘*nirvāṇamayaēvāyam ātmā jñānamaya: amala:’* (ātmā is full of happiness, full of intellect, and does not have any blemishes), and it is said in ‘*du:ka ajñāna malādarmā: prakrutēśtē: na ca ātmana:* \[śrīviṣṇu purāṇam\]*’* (sadness, ignorance, blemishes, etc. are due to material realm only and these are not for ātmā), that is sadness etc., are the nature of material realm, and not have it inherent in ātmā.

Like how for ātmā which is by itself is in the state of happiness by its nature would not be having sadness, ignorance etc.,  it is for material realm which is unintelligent also, there would not be sadness, ignorance, etc. But, like how with consumption of *ūmattai* fruit (medicinal herb – *datura*) the mind would become insane (though insanity is not present in the fruit itself), since ātmā gets sadness etc., due to association of material realm, these sadness etc., are the properties of material realm (*prakruti)* only.

(Knowledge that something seems as per our wish, is happiness; knowledge that something seems against our wish, is sadness).

(till now it was about *ātma svarūpam;* now it is about *kaivalyam*).

***iṟappaadṟkē eṇṇādu –*** *iṟappu* is dying. ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) not thinking about (my) dying. Earlier – it talked about death that is one of six states related to body; here, it talks about death related to nature of ātmā. That, is, *kaivalyam* (being in the state of enjoying one’s own ātmā after getting liberated from the cycle of births in this world).

Is it correct to say the state of *kaivalyam* which does not have rebirth and which is a separate destiny, and which has the nature of experiencing by (own) ātmā that is happy by itself, as death?

Death is – seen in the world as removal of ātmā from body. This other death is also in the same way, since ātmā is removing the effect of paramātmā from it for enjoying on own, and so this also shall be considered as death at ātmā level.

But unlike how a dead body deteriorates, ātmā does not deteriorate due to this; so is it correct to call it as its death for ātmā? Since it does not have the enjoyment according to its true nature (being for enjoyment of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), in the mind of *nityar/mukhtar* this *kaivalyam* also shall be death (for ātmā).

In ‘*mrutōdaridra: puruśa:’*, the wealthy one who enjoys everything  would consider poor as a dead person, this *kaivalyam* state of ātmā appears as death for them (for *annāda* (the nityasūris for whom [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is food), *kaivailayam* is said as death for ātmā.

***eṇṇādu –*** [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) avoided thinking about giving this kind of death (kaivalyam) to me.

(Will He think of giving that?) As said in ‘*jarā maraṇa mōkṣāyamāmācritya yadanti yē*  \[[śrī bhagavat gītā\]](http://githa.koyil.org/)*’* (those wishing (only) to liberate from body, which is the cause of birth and death, and wishing to enjoy just the ātmā, I shall give them that *kaivalyam*), He who could give this as my destiny, now, did not think about giving it to me, and avoided my falling into abyss.

(Why not say ‘*tarādu’* (not giving) instead of ‘***eṇṇādu’*** (not *thinking* of giving)?)

Since his being is based on [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s thinking, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying ***eṇṇādu*** (Him not thinking in such ways).

(Place where ātmās are present in state of *kaivalyam* is in between *virajā* river and *paramapadam* (authoritative references for this include *śrīviṣṇu purāṇam*, and many places in vēdam). This means one cannot return to this world after going there. In *tātparya candrikai* [vēdāntācārya](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/vedhanthacharyar/) discusses that it is possible to return from there to this world for another birth).

After doing this help of avoiding such danger for me, He enlightened about His true nature (paratvam (supremacy), etc.), form (divine body), and qualities (intellect, strength, wealth, valor, simplicity, and so on), says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) next, in ‘***eṇṇum pon uruvāy’ –***  (*pon*-\> gold) –  He enlightened (me) about true nature of divine (parama)ātmā, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

***eṇṇum  –***  (could keep thinking about [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) –  Due to eminence of enjoyment, as said in ‘*nēti nēti’* (not like this, not like this – [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) cannot be understood in any such specific ways), other than saying ‘*adu, adu’* (that, that, …), [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) cannot be set in a boundary by saying ‘*idam’* (this; (which is seen by eyes)).

As said in ‘*ēkaika guṇāvadeebhsayā sadāstitā:’* (being with the wish to know the bounds of each and every quality of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)*,* did not leave from the attempt to do that), since each and every quality of His is boundless, does it even require mentioning that it is not possible to determine fully the true nature in which such sort of countless auspicious qualities are present?

***pon uruvāy –*** Since [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is having the nature (*uru*) of gold (*pon*), this talks about true nature. That is, He and gold are similar: in being desirable, when said that as being existing then life would be sustained, using it can achieve all the wishes, and all of the world would fall in the feet of one having this.

***maṇi uruvil būtam aindāy –*** (this is Not about the five core elements (earth, water, fire, air, and ether)  related to material realm (*prākrutam*)).  The core cause elements (by which [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s form is made of), that are five (*paramēśti, pumān, viśvam,  nivrutta:, sarva:*, which are specified in *śrī pāncarātram*).  (maṇi -\> gem).  ***maṇi uruvil –*** Having the nature (*uru)* of gem (*maṇi*),  being lustrous in form (*uru* stands for this also);

like gold that is set in a ruby container (*māṇikkam*, which implies divine body)  (that is, ruby would clearly show the brightness of gold), it shows the nature of ātmā that is formed from the five core elements (*bhūtam*).  That is, the elements would show the nature of (parama)ātmā that supports it, and it would show the qualities of that (parama)ātmā since that form is *sudda satvam* (purely tranquil), and His divine form (body) is more enjoyable than His nature and qualities.

The core elements that are cause of such divine form (body) are five *upaniśat.*

In that special place (śrīvaikuṇṭam), the divine bodies of all present are made of non-material realm, and of five core elements that are *panca upaniśat.*

These five elements are said in ‘*paramēśtee pumān viśvōnivrutta: sarva ēvasa:’.*

As **‘maṇi uruvil būtam aindāy’*****,***  it differentiates the five core elements of material realm that make up our body, from the ones which hide the true nature of our ātmā and that of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) inside it.

That is, [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is capable of being present in such form like quality of gem (in auspicious divine body, in śrīvaikuṇṭam), and also capable of being present in the body that are made of core elements of this material realm.

By this, like the extraordinary divine body, these material realm based ones are also dependent (*vidēyan*) on [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).   (*vidēyan* – who acts according to His wishes)

Without any difference between devotees (in this world) and the material realm that which binds them in this world, they are under the control of the liberator ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), isn’t it?

It is said too, ‘*pradāna puruśa eeśvara:’* (For the core elements of material realm, and for the ātmās, He is the controller).

Now, would such a distinguished One be hard to access? [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) answers:

***punal uruvāy –*** (*punal* -\> water);  Like the water in which any one without differentiation among any ranging from sages like vaśiśtar to a person of very lowly stature could bathe in the same area,

[emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would be easiest for everyone to access. (*sarva sulabhan*).

Even though He is very easy to approach, one does not have to be scared as to what danger might happen to Him – this would not be necessary because like how He is easy for His devotees,  He is like fire for the opposing ones; that is He cannot be overcome/affected – says āḻvār now:

***anal uruvil tigaḻum –*** *(anal -\> fire)* As said in ‘*abhita: bhāvakōpamam* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – āraṇya kāṇḍam – 99.26\]’ (perumāḷ appeared) like fire on all the sides (for bharatāḻvān when he approached perumāḷ to request Him to return to ayōdyā immediately), when He cannot be attained even for the close ones when He does not wish to honor their request, it goes without saying that He cannot be overcome for those who oppose Him, isn’t it?

***sōdi than uruvāy –***  (*sōdi* -\> *jyōti:* -\> light) Earlier, by ***pon uruvāy*****,** it said that by His nature He is bright by himself based on Him being desirable.   (Here it talks about the behavior of His nature). In the world, for the things that emit light, that light is based on their association with Him; this is as said in ‘*tasyabāsā sarvam idam vibhāti’* (By the light of that sarvēśvaran, these things of light are being bright).

***en uruvil ninṟa***  **–** (Here *uru* stands for body (of [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/))) – Whereas when He comes and be present even in ātmā it would be a blemish for Him, now He came and made Himself present standing in my body which is the form that is to be shunned (body that is shunned by Himself and by others);

***ninṟa –*** (stood) **–**  As he ([āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)) took *madal* (going around town and complaining that his lover ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) is not showing up for him, which is to say there is no [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s perturbance (where [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) might establish) that He may be considered not present in the world –  such perturbance got removed and He came standing and His existence (*sattai*) prevailed.

***endai –***  (father/master) – Like his existence (*sattai*), His *sēṣitvam* (lordship) also prevailed.

***taḷir puraiyum tiruvadi en talai mēlavē –*** read as – ***en talai mēlavē – taḷir puraiyum tiruvadi***  per formation of phrase in tamiḻ ***–*** After keeping His divine feet on his head, His feet sprouted (That is, got the life back – became happy).

When not doing that, His divine feet was like dry leaves (His mind was unhappy);

Like how [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)’s *svarūpam* (true nature of self) was despondent when for a long time he was after *viśaya prāvaṇyam* (inclined towards material aspects of world), His divine feet was like so too due to not having association with [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) (*cētana lābham*  (when a sentient and [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) come together, it is mainly a gain and happiness for [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (as opposed to for the sentient), when a sentient finally agrees to accept the ever-present grace of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/))).

(*tiru* also implies *pirātti*, which represents [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s divine chest (where She resides), to say that the nityasūris serve to such [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s divine feet).

***en talai mēlavē –*** The divine feet that is a decoration in the head of *nityasūris* (*amarar cennip pū* \[\]’ ())*,* is the one that has become ornament in my head, you see!

Whose feet were in this head earlier, which is holding His divine feet now..

***ninṟa endai tiruvadi –***  That is, after keeping  the divine feet in his head, He got His existence (***ninṟa***), lordship (***endai***), and body (***tiruvadi*** (which identifies His body)).

So, (in summary), [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) thinks – is it not enough that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) highlighted the impermanence of material realm and related things? is it not enough that He highlighted the true nature (*svasvarūpam)*? is it not enough that he ensured (my) not going into *kaivalyam?* After that, He showed His true nature and the bright nature of His divine body, is that not enough? Oh! beyond that, He came and stood in my body that is to be shunned by (even) me! Even beyond that, Oh!, He kept His divine feet well set on my head! – and so tirumangai [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) becomes very happy thinking about all such Help that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) has done for him.

So, by this pāsuram, it said about the true nature of the three *tatvams* (truth;  viz. sentient, non-sentient, and [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), and that all the three may be destiny (for different types of people), and that only one among them is the true destiny based on true nature (of *jeevātmā*) ,  and that the others are lowly destinies,  and about distinguished characteristics of that important destiny, that is, [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s nature, form, and qualities,  and that when [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) shows them to the sentient He does so with the view that it is He who is honored by that.

– – – – –

Translation by raghuram śrīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 2. 2

*pāruruvi neererikāl visumbumāgip    palvēṟu samayamumāyp parandu ninṟa ēruruvil mūvarumēyenna ninṟa    imaiyavar tam tiruvuruvēṟeṇṇum pōdu ōruruvam ponnuruvam onṟu sentee    onṟumā kadaluruvam ottu ninṟa mūvuruvum kaṇḍa pōdu onṟām sōdi   mugiluruvam emmadigaḷ uruvam tānē                          2*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēr uruvil – Having beautiful bodies; mūvarumē enna ninṟa – being as if all the three are equal; imaiyavar tam tiruvuru – of the form of brahmā, viṣṇu, and Sivan; vēṟeṇṇumpōdu – when seeing separately; ōruruvam – brahmā’s form is; ponnuruvam – of the nature of gold; onṟu – rudran’s (Sivan) form is; sem thee – of the nature of reddish fire; onṟu – form of nārāyaṇan, the husband of lakṣmī; mā kadal uruvam – is invigorating, like (when seeing) the big ocean; mūvuruvum – all the three forms; ottu ninṟa – standing together (with their such qualities); kaṇḍa pōdu – (but) when seeing through pramāṇam; pār uruvi neer erikāl visumbumāgi – creating the five core elements like the earth made of hard material, etc; palvēṟu samayamumāy – creating the world that is of many different paths/categories; parandu ninṟa – and pervading everywhere, inside the ātmās (as antaryāmi); Am sōdi onṟu – being said by ‘param jyōti’ , and being the One; mugil uruvam – the form that is like the dark rainy clouds; em adigaḷ uruvam – īś the form of sarvēśvaran who is the lord of everyone

**Перевод:**

**pār uruvi – Earth that is of hard make up (pār -\> pāṟai -\> hard rock).  urvi  is in the language used by the noble learned people (āryas), that is, samskruta word which means earth. It got transformed into tamiḻ word uruvi here.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the first pāsuram – āḻvār divined about the series of help of emperumān to him, starting from removal of dēhātmābhimānam through giving him the destiny (divine feet); in this pāsuram – āḻvār divines about the removal of his mistaken thought of the three lords as equals. Is it not implied when it was said in previous pāsuram itself as ‘eṇṇum ponnuruvāy’, that is, about distinguished supreme quality of His nature? Based on ‘simha avalōkana nyāyam’ (like a lion after eating its prey and walking away would turn back to look at the prey just to reconfirm), looking at all His help, even if His other help are digested, is it possible to digest the great help of breaking the doubt of equality among the three lords? – thinking of that, āḻvār is again thinking about this particular help of emperumān. (How great is this particular help?)  Is this help like the (relatively less significant) help of saving me from viśayāntara prāvaṇyam, or is this help like the (relatively less significant) help of the danger of kaivalyam – says āḻvār? āḻvār says that emperumān showed me the ways of the One as the lord and the other two as subservient,  and, the other two as created and the One as creator.*

The word ***pār*** is Not to mean earth here – its meaning is from *pāṟai*  (hard rock) that it is a hard material like a hard rock.  By this, – compared to other core elements (like air, fire, etc.), this is different and having the hardness – the earth.

When recited as **pār** ‘***uruvil****’* –  (in *pār uru*) – it means – From the (twenty one) elements from which *aṇḍam*  (world sphere) is created, the following, that is, *pār*-\> earth,  etc., that are *neer* -\> water,  *eri* -\> fire , *kāl* -\> air, *visumbu –* space –the five elements are created – He is being these elements;

If asked – *avyakta* (name of world when it was in the unmanifested state), *akśara*  (the material realm’s source which has not changed), *mahat* (the great element),  *ahankāram*  (ego indicating root material realm) – are these also not the sources for creating of *(aṇḍam)* spherical world? (Why are they not included in the list of creating elements, but only the five elements, by [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)?)

It is since *mahān* etc., are in *sūkṣma* (subtle) form (not visible to people’s eyes), and so would be difficult for people to understand; so [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) talks here only about those that are *stūlam* (gross, can be perceived by the senses of the body) (they are *pār, neer, eri, kāl*, etc. mentioned above), and and since these are the (immediate) direct reason for creation of the *aṇḍam;* and so this is about *nitya sruśti* (more frequent continuous creation activities of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/))*.*

*riśi* (*parāsarar*) too said ‘*bhūtēbhyōṇḍam’* about the direct/immediate source.

(why is it saying earth etc., in reverse order of creation?)  
Saying ***pār mudalā***  is based on order in which they go through annihilation.

***āgi –*** would this word indicate that creation?  (āgi is ‘becoming’, *ākki* would mean ‘creation (but it would also mean creator)) – Since [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) are vēdāntis (followers of vēdantas), [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is divining based on the śruti of ‘*bahusyām’* (I shall **become** many things (That is, being *sareeree* of them (they being part of His body) and his being *nirvāhakar* (controller)))*.*

But, considering ‘*ākāśadvāyu:, vāyōragni:, agnērāpa:, adbhya:prutivee’*,  where it says one is the reason/source for the next one’s creation, is it correct to say that He is the reason/source for the creation of All of them?

As said in, ‘*tēja: aikṣanta, āpa aikṣanta:’,* (desire of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to create the elements) the act of creation based on intent/desire (*sankalpam*) cannot be by [non-sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) (like space, etc.), it is clear that it is [*emperumān*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to whom these state/entities are His body and He in the form of those respective elements creates the next ones, and so He would have to be the creator.

If this pāsuram is about removal of doubt about equivalence of the three lords, why is the meaning given above as creation of material realm? It is to show that for the One who created the core elements that are the source for creation of spherical world (*aṇḍam*), then there is no prospect of Him being considered equal to those other two lords who are present/created within that spherical world.

***pal vēṟu samayamumāy –*** What it talked about above is *samaśti sruśti* (creations before formation of *aṇḍam*;  creation of and without mixing of core elements (*prutvi* (earth)*, appu (*water*),* etc.); creation of ingredients for further creation; without division of dēvas, human, etc.)*.*

Now by the current phrase, it is talking about *vyaśti sruśti* (creation of spherical worlds, their enclosures (*āvaraṇam*), and fourteen layers in them, in clearly diversified forms by mixing of the five core elements (*panca būtams*) , twenty four tatvams (non-sentient elements); with explicit divisions as dēvas, humans, animals, plants).

Since brahmā (*badda cētanar – bound soul*) is present without difference in the five core elements in singular (*samaśti*) form, it is called *samaśti sruśti.*

From such brahmā, subsequently, the other [sentients](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) are born within the spherical world in variegated forms – it is called *vyaśti sruśti.*

(In the śrībhāśyam book published in tirunārāyaṇapuram, elaborate explanation is given about samaśti sruśti and vyaśti srusti).

Creating the world of many and of different system/limitations (*samayam*), and of innumerable ātmās, that is *vyaśti srusti,* is said as ‘***pal***‘ (many);  it is unlike *samasti sruśti* where it is countable (five elements in separate forms).

***vēṟu*** is, being separate such that they are distinct and non-mixed, and so by their bodies they are being completely separate.  ***samayam*** is system/limitation; being with the nature as per limitation of their category of creation (man being of specific set of behaviours, dēvas being of some other set of behaviours, etc.).

dēvas are having the nature of being vorśipped and consuming nectar; humans are having the nature of vorśipping and consuming food;  nature of animals and plants are to be accessories to such worship, and being the reason for the upliftment/life of humans;

and the behavior of those other than the aforementioned categories, is to be under the loving shadow of noble men. It is said too as ‘*dāsya sukha ēka sanginām bhavanēṣvastvapi keetajanma mē* \[[stōtra ratnam – 55](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/sthothra-rathnam-55/)\]’ ((noble ones) are involved in your joy of servitude to you; it should occur that I be born even as a worm in such divine place of their residence).

***parandu ninṟa –***  Doing ***parandu*** is permeating by following the jeevātmā into (*anupravēśam*) all the created bodies and being ātmā for everything (that is, without Him nobody would be able to do anything – here body includes brahmā, Siva et al).

*anupravēśam* is – setting Himself amicable to be inside the tangible material entity (*stūlam*). Similar to how He is amicably present *along with* (*viśiśta*) intangible/subtle forms (*sūkśmam*) of [sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and [non-sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/), He is also amicably present *along with* (*viśiśta*) tangible/concrete forms (*sūkśmam*) of [sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and [non-sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/).

***ēr uruvil –*** in beautiful bodies;  Since by ***parandu ninṟa*** it says – [*eeśvaran*](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) who is present as [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) for everything,  for such [eeśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) everything in the visible world are bodies for Him as said in ‘*jagat sarvam śareeramtē’,* by ***uru*** it refers to world. (that is, all the things (like earth, what and who are inside earth, other worldly bodies, and so on, are all bodies for Him).

Is it appropriate to refer to *samsāris* (people with interest in worldly things) who are to be shunned (by *mumukśus)*, as ***ēr uru*** (beautiful bodies)?

For a *mumukśu* (liberation seeker)*, samsāram* would be a place to be afraid of, and also would be acceptable;  when looking at the visible body/world as attained due to being tied by karmas (and generate more karmas), mumukśus would consider it to be shunned; when looking at the world/people as being body of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as said in ‘*jagat sarvam śareeratē’*,  then it would be considered acceptable.

In this beautiful world (beautiful people of the world) –

***mūvarumē enna ninṟa –*** Avoiding the notion that the world is of numerous chiefs, some *vēdāntis* narrowed it down (to three).

This helped us to not have to reject individually the (numerous) chiefs who seem like ones to be vorśipped, and gave us only a couple of them to be shunned (brahmā and Siva).

In *pūrva kāṇḍam* (first part of vēdam), it says ‘*trimśacca dēva: nava cāsaparyan treeṇi śatā three śahasrāṇyagnim’* (three thousand three hundred thirty nine dēvatas served me, the agni) – it says three thousand three hundred thirty nine dēvatas as to be vorśipped. vēdāntis say that only the One who is the reason for creation of the world is to be vorśipped. From this, some would say that since there are three who are the reason for creation, sustenance, and annihilation, all the three are to be vorśipped.

Since saying ***enna ninṟa –*** (be of stand as if (all three are equal)) – at a cursory look it appears as if all the three are equal,  they would look like ones to be vorśipped; but if we analyse it, there is not even a possibility of the two being equal (to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)).

As said in ‘*brahma viṣṇu śivātmikām* \| *sa* *samjñām yāti bhagavānēka Eva janārdana: \|\| \[śrīviṣṇu purāṇam – 1.2.23\]*’ (the distinct [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) janārdanan assumes 3 forms with the names of brahmā, viṣṇu and śivā to perform creation, sustenance, and annihilation), and so at cursory look it appears to be about equivalence, but with saying ‘*janārdana:’,* as it refers to the substitute word of viṣṇu, it (the thought of their equivalence) is to be shunned.

***imaiyavar –*** (*dēvas*) This is also a reason for the misunderstanding.  Since they (brahmā and Siva) also do not blink their eyes, and since they are referred to with the word ‘dēvas’, this term is being part of the misunderstanding.

Even though it is common for all the (three) dēvas, there is no possibility of equivalence, since it is said as ‘*sākśāddēva: purāṇōsau’* (this [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is of all ages, is the Only dēva), the quality of dēva is present only in Him at all times (unlike for others when it is present only from some point).

***than tiruvuru vēṟu eṇṇumpōdu –*** When thinking about their individual nature/activity (creation, sustenance, annihilation), it is capable of creating such misunderstanding.

Why praise the ones who are shunned, as ‘*tiru uru’* ?  [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is talking so this way because like their nature/acts, their body is that of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (*śareera śareeri bhāvam*).

***vēṟeṇṇumpōdu –*** When thinking about the three of them together, we would consider it as śareera śareeri bhāvam (one (emperumān) having the body, and the body (brahmā, Siva)), and it would be clear that the two are subservient, and One ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) is the lord. This can be seen in ‘*ari ayan aran ennnum ivarai onṟa num manattu vaittu* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.3.6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-6-unarnthu-unarnthu/) & [1.3.7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/07/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-7-onrenap-palavena/)\]’ (those who are popularly known as [hari](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), aja (brahmā) and rudra (Siva), these personalities who are explained in various pramāṇams to reveal their nature/activities, being neutral instead of taking sides of any one (to identify the chief person), contemplating in your heart/mind, analysing their svarūpa (true nature) and svabhāva (qualities) through pramāṇams (śāstram));

***ōr uruvam*** etc. – This is talking about the individual nature/characteristics of them that are a reason for the misunderstand that they are equivalent.

***ōr uruvam pon uruvam –*** brahmā’s nature is that of gold;  gold is such that – all the ornaments could be made from it; in the same way (brahmā’s nature) would appear to be eligible for creating all the fourteen worlds; since he is the one from whose body all the things are created as he is the one for creation of all the bhaddātmās ([sentient – bound souls](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)).

***onṟu sentee –*** Another *tatvam*’s nature is that of fire, that is to destroy the world; nature of agni (fire) is that of burning everything; in the same way, if we see the nature of rudran, it would be suitable for annihilating the world;

***onṟu mā kadal uruvam –*** being remover of sadness for those who see, the ocean’s nature is to keep within itself and save, everything from  bad things, good things, gems and such, etc., isn’t it? in the same way, if we see the nature of [eeśvaran (](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)[emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)[)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/), He is the remover of sadness to those who surrendered to Him, and keeping them under His love and protecting them would be seen as the nature of Him.

The reason/source of the world is to be found only in the one where all these qualities are present isn’t it? Since these three nature are in these three, it is such that one could mistake that all the three are responsible for the reason/source of world (*jagatkāraṇam*).

***ottu ninṟa –*** being together.  That is – each of their nature is together with their acts of creation, sustenance, and annihilation.

Next it talks about removal of the misunderstanding.

***mu uruvum kaṇḍa pōdu –*** When knowing these three tatvams by using authoritative reference.

***onṟām sōdi –*** Among them, the light (which is having lustre by itself) is only One, is the point;

(āren’t the other ātmās also having lustre by themselves?) – the *tējas* (lustre/light) that is of the creator, and being the constituent (*śareeree)* of bodies,  and being lord (*śēśi*), is only of the One isn’t it?

The other two would be of the nature of being created, of being a body, and of subservience;  If asking where have we seen this – ‘*ēkōhavai nārāyaṇa āseet’* (it is well known that only [nārāyaṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) was present (in a form)), that is, it is saying about the One who is the reason for the world (because He was the only one present in a form), and, ‘*na brahmā nēśāna: nēmē dyāvā prutivee’,*  similar to earth, etc., that are [non-sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/), brahmā and rudran are also said as effected to go through annihilation, and, by ‘*sa brahma sa Siva:*’, (they are jeevātmas only), et al, it talks about them as jeevātmās, and as having subservience, and, ‘*ēśa sarvabhutāntarātmā’* – by the world *sarva* (Everyone), it refers to everyone in which He is permeating; so, the fact that these two are among everyone, their being created (*kāryatvam*), they being [jeevātmas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (*kṣetratvam*), they being subservient, and being part of body (of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) – are all well accepted by everyone (*sampratipannam*).

Even though jeevātmās also have the lustre by themselves (*svayam prakāśam*), but by saying ‘***Am sōdi’*****,** it differentiates Him from the other ātmās, due to their being controlled (and Him being controller). That is why, ‘*nārāyaṇaparō jyōti:’*, (light means [nārāyaṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) and, *‘jyōtiśām jyōti:’* (He is light for all the lights) had to be said.

(Instead of splitting as **onṟu Am sōdi,**  why not take the meaning of ***onṟānjōti***  as – all the three would together become one (advaitam)) ?

Saying ‘***mūvurum kaṇḍapōdu onṟānjōti’***  ***–*** would look to be according to those who argue there is brahmam only (and no jeevātmās etc). But since that is not [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)’s philosophy, this is not according to their point of view. Moreover,  [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) in [previous pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thirunedunthandakam-1/) had with clear separation sang *minnuruvāy,* and, *pinnuruvāy,* and, *ponnuruvāy,* about the three separate *tatvams* ([sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), [non-sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/), and [eeśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)), and in this pāsuram, saying ‘***ēr uru’,*** (beautiful form) to mean *śareera ātma bhāvam* (others being bodies, and Him being ātmā for everyone),  and later (in 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram) he is saying ‘*indirarkkum piramaṟkum mudalvan tannai*‘ (lord for indra and brahmā), there is contradiction to their point by way of other pāsurams also.

***em adigaḷ uruvam – mugil uruvam –*** my lord’s form is black like that of rainy clouds; earlier where he said *‘****onṟu mā kadal uruvam*****’*****,*** as similar to the nature of ocean, it was meaning that He is protector;  Now – it is talking about His divine body; (Because) It is this form that identifies His *sarvādikatvam* (being the first of all things; being lord of/distinguished from every one).

***tānē –*** (But vasn’t that said as the colour of gem/diamond in pāsuram 1?) The body which was said as ‘*maṇi uruvil būtam aindāy* \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thirunedunthandakam-1/)\]’  īś the one that is like that of dark rainy clouds, you see!

There it said ‘*maṇi uru’* because He is being pleasant and favourable like how one could keep a gem easily tied in the tip of their cloth, and it is bright, and extremely priceless,  and enjoyable.

In tamiḻ, the sequence of pāsuram words that would form the sentence for the whole meaning is as follows: *ēr uruvil mūvarumē enna ninṟa imaiyavar tam tiru uru vēṟeṇṇumpōdu, or uruvam pon uruvam, onṟu sentee, onṟu mākadal uruvam; ottu ninṟa muvurum kaṇḍa pōdu – pār uruvi neer eri kāl visumbumāgip palvēṟu samayamumāyp parandu ninṟa – onṟām sōdi mugil uruvam em adigaḷ uruvam tānē’*

**{**In the beautiful (ātmās of the) world, the three stood as those to be vorśipped as dēvas, when considering their activities separately, one is like gold, one is like world-ending fire, and one is like the mighty ocean; (but) when seeing such matching nature (through the eyes of śāstram) –  creating the hard-surfaced earth, and other core elements (separately), and permeating in various categories of things/lives (created by combining the elements) – (are due to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) being the reason); such [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who was the only self-illuminating light present, such [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) of mine is of the colour of rainy clouds, you see. **}**

– – – – –

Translation by raghuram śrīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 3. 3

*tiruvadivil karunedumāl sēyan enṟum   tirētaikkaṇ vaḷaiyuruvāyt tigaḻndānenṟum peruvadivil kadal amudam koṇḍa kālam    perumānaik karuneela vaṇṇan tannai oruvadivattōruruvenṟu uṇaralāgā(du)    ūḻi tōṟuḻi ninṟēttal allāl karuvadiviṟ cenkaṇṇa vaṇṇan tannaik   katturaiyē yāroruvar kāṇkiṟpārē                         3*

**Пословный перевод:**

karu neela vaṇṇan tannai – One naturally having blue colour among the colours; perumānai – that is sarvēśvaran; tiruvadivil – in the matter of his divine body; karunedumāl – (like rainy clouds) emperumān naturally having black colour, having a lot of love towards devotees; sēyan enṟum – that He is having reddish colour; tirētaikkaṇ – in the trētā yugam; peru vadivil kadal amudam koṇḍa kālam – in the kruta yugam when having many forms, and took nectar from the divine milky ocean; vaḷai uruvāyt tigaḻndān enṟum – He was having white colour like a conch; ūḻi tōṟu ūḻi ninṟu ēttalallāl – other than being in each kalpam and praise Him (like this); uṇaral āgādu – It is not possible to know (Him); oru vadivattu ōr uru enṟu – as having one specific form of divine body, or as having one type of colour; katturaiyē – other than (everyone) be (only) talking about; karu vadivil sem kaṇṇa vaṇṇan tannai – that emperumān having bluish divine body, and reddish divine eyes; yār oruvar kāṇkiṟpārē – who can see Him (like I did as He showed to me)?

**Перевод:**

**tiruvadivil –  In the matter of divine form/body.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the first pāsuram – āḻvār was astonished about the series of help of emperumān to him, starting from removal of viśayāntara prāvaṇyam (interest in other things of material world) through giving him the destiny (divine feet) which is the gain for true nature of ātmā,  and He showed him all this while considering it as an honour for Himself; (Why the astonishment of āḻvār when emperumān is doing these for His own benefit?) – If āḻvār requests for it and emperumān performs help on that request, then that would be understandable/valid; but while I am averse/turned away from Him, He Himself is eagerly performing such help, so it is astonishing). In the second pāsuram – āḻvār said that emperumān cut his subservience towards others.  Mistaking due to the misunderstanding that the three deities are equivalent would be subservience to others, isn’t it? In this pāsuram –  As said as ‘maṇi uruvil būtam aindāy \[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 1\]’,  and ‘mugil uruvam \[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 2\]’, that which is illuminating the svarūpam (for (parama) ātma vastu (entity)),  and for the qualities present in such vastu (entity), and which is of unsurpassed enjoyment, and which is having the colour like that of rainy clouds – the way in which emperumān helped by showing such divine body/form of His, to those having even a tiny affection, based on their taste, even by affecting His stature/original form (aḻiya māṟi), and the way in which emperumān showed him due to His voluntary kindness His natural body/form – āḻvār, seeing these as He showed them, says who would be able to see this like he did. (Why enjoy His divine body instead of focusing on working for emperumān’s excellence (atisayam) as dictated by subservience?) Like how a virtuous woman would be focused on being in unison with her husband, if one gets subservience, and also get the state of not yielding  to others (ananyārhatai), then he would just be involved in enjoyment of Him isn’t it? His qualities are such that one would enjoy them more than His svarūpam; but one would enjoy His form more than both His svarūpam and His qualities – such is the enjoyability of His body/form, isn’t it? By this pāsuram, āḻvār is enjoying that form/body/beauty.*

Like how in the first pāsuram it was about (His help in) *śēśatvam* (subservience), and in the second pāsuram it was about *anyaśēśatva nivrutti* (removal of subservience to others), now in this pāsuram it is about His (His help about) divine form, you see! says āḻvār.

***tiru vadivu –*** Distinguished form. [periya pirāttiyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s form (form enjoyed by Her).

When it could have been said as (just) ***vadivu*****,** saying ***tiru vadivu*** is – Like said in *‘agalakillēn iṟaiyum* \[[tiruvāimoḻi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/) – 6.10.10\]’ (will not leave you even a fraction of a moment), the form is such that [pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s state is like (a thirsty one saying) ‘water! water!’, it is such difficult to attain and get Him in such form, and it is such status He lowered Himself from, and showed it to the world – it is for this, that [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying *‘****tiru’ vadivu.***

Now it talks about how [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) helped by giving such divine from.

***karu nedu māl –*** Having the form of colour like black rainy cloud **(*****karu*****)**, having the great glory that is beyond both the worlds **(*****nedu*****)**. By this – One who is having distinguished divine body, not needing/expecting from anyone else (having everything by Himself) (***māl*****)**, and superior to everything, is the one, you see, become like these (as shown by phrases that follow)! – says āḻvār.

ōr, by ***māl –*** with maddening love, He is of deep (***nedu***) love. By this, the One who is of greatness as aforementioned, is lowering/changing himself and showing himself due to His being of deep love (*vyāmōghata*).

***karu*** **-\>** *kāḷa,* in samskrutam, both meaning black. *kāḷa mēgham* -\> black cloud.

***sēyan enṟum tirētaikkaṇ –*** When considering *trētā yugam,* He would be having reddish ***(sēy)*** form. People of that time were having more quality of *rajas* (action/anger), so their interest would be in red colour only isn’t it? (rajas is represented as red). Based on that interest of those people, He would present himself to them in reddish colour.

*rajas* (anger/active) is reddish, *satvam* (tranquillity) is whitish, and *tamas* (ignorance/laziness) is blackish.

For *prakruti* (material realm) specific properties are these three qualities as said in ‘*lōhita śukla* kriṣṇām \[śvētasvatara ūpaniśad – 4.5\]’ (red, white, and black) (samsāri (worldly person) with the mind of enjoyment follows the woman that is *prakruti* having red (*rajas),* white (*satvam)*, black (*tamas*) colours (qualities) and which can create various things equal to it; the other one that is *mukta jeevan* (liberated), even though enjoys the woman that is *prakruti* for some period, then hates it and leaves her \[gets liberated\]. This *prakruti* and these beings are eternal (meaning is taken from *pramāṇat tirattu* (collection of authoritative references) given along with vyākhyānam).

Reason for talking about red first is – to show that it is in the order of word of śruti in ‘*lōhita śukla kruṣṇam’.*

While it is for the subservient to set himself for the happiness of the lord, it is the lord who is changing Himself against His superiority according to the taste of His subjects.

(This can be seen in), As said in ‘*tamar ugandadu evvuruvam* \[mudal tiruvantādi – 44\]’ (whatever form the devotees enjoy), the ‘***enṟum enṟum****’* ((praise him) as, as) seen in multiple lines of this pāsuram is to be read with ‘***ūḻi tōṟu ūḻi ninṟu ēttalallāl’*** (other than praising him in each such yugam)***.***

***peruvadivil kadal amudam koṇḍa kālam –*** When considering *kruta* *yugam*;  not saying (explicitly) *kruta yugam* in the pāsuram, like saying ‘***tirētaik kaṇ****’* (in previous line) , but saying about it as ‘***kadal amudam koṇḍa kālam****’* (time when He churned milky ocean) *–* Saying *‘****tirētaik kaṇ’*** appears as barren time for [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) (As śrī rāman with anger bridged on the ocean just for killing the *asura* and so [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is not considering that as great, whereas here He with kindness churned the ocean (and gave nectar)), and so [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is talking about the specifics of emperumān’s glory of *kruta yugam* since the activities of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) of this time is loved by āḻvār*;*

This is similar to how those (in śrīrangam) having love towards *periya tirunāḷ* (*panguni uttram* day when [perumāḷ and pirāttiyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) grace together once a year) would say ‘I will do it (some activity) after *periya tirunāḷ’* instead of saying based on the month, as, ‘I will do after the month of panguni’, thus basing it on the activity of emperumān. (In the same way [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is talking about the time of churning of milky ocean).

But, is it not enough to say ‘***kadal amudam koṇḍa kālam’*** why say ‘***peru vadivil*** **kadal amudam koṇḍa kālam**’? (huge form)

*kṣeerābdi* (milky ocean) is big such that it covers fourteen hundred thousand miles (14 lakh miles (***kādam*** -\> around a mile);

īsn’t He reclining in that ocean as said in ‘*tāḷum tōḷum mudigaḷum samanilāda pala parappi* \[[tiruvāimoḻi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/) – 8.10.8\]’, such that the whole area of the ocean swells and splashes.

With that form itself as He gets up and churns, it would look like an ocean churning an ocean, so [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is divining so (as ***peru vadivil*** (huge form));

Ocean is such that it relieves our burdens, and it is boundless; likewise, (emperumān’s form) also relieves our burden, is boundless, and enjoyable.

ōr, as ***peru vadivu –*** by the form that cannot be measured; for the *mantara* mountain not to drown He took the form of *kūrmam* (tortoise), and to control the turbulence above, the form of *bruhatrūpi* (*brahma rūpi* (as said in *’uparyākrāntavān śailam bruhadrūpēṇa kēśava:* \[śrīviṣṇu purāṇam – 1.9.90\]’) (kēśavan)), thus being in a form among the dēvas and in a form among asuras, and pulling / churning amidst them, and being as strength for vāsuki the snake (used as rope for churning), and as said in ‘*yanna druśtam surāsurai:’*, thus permeating everywhere whether mountain, dēvas, asuras, and gave strength – thus taking many forms in these ways.

***kadal amudam koṇḍa kālam –*** All the *dēvas* without anyone missing, who due to the curse of *durvāsa muni* became losers of wealth, losing nectar and becoming poor, and so as said in ‘*śaraṇam tvāmanuprāptā: samastā dēvatāgaṇā:’ –* surrendered, and the way He churned the ocean and gave nectar to them.

***koṇḍa -\>*** having (as against ‘gave’ the nectar))-  He was thinking that it is his gain/blessing to give them nectar.

***kadal amudam koṇḍa kālam –*** The time when He created nectar for the dēvas, for Himself, and for me ([āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)).

dēvas got the goods of nectar and went away; He took the nectar that is [periya pirāttiyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (who came from the ocean and settled on His divine chest);  These two, as said in ‘*viṇṇavar amuduṇa amudil varum peṇ amuduṇḍa* \[periya tirumoḻi – 6.1.2\]*’* (dēvas got the nectar, [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) got the nectar that is tāyār) are not enjoyment  for āḻvār. It is as said in ‘*nāl tōḷ amudu* \[[tiruvāimoḻi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/) – 6.10.9\]*’* (emperumān the divine nectar), with his hands arms and divine conch, the One who churned the ocean is the One that is the enjoyment for him ([āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)).

dēvas got what they loved; He also got His love; I also got what I love (says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)).

As said in ‘*paśyatām śarvadēvānām yayau vakśa:stalam harē:’,* as all dēvas where witnessing it, stepping her feet on His divine flower of navel, climbed into His divine chest. Why climb so without giving importance to those witnessing? It is only when One gets Her love would She be able to shower love on others.

ōr, thinking – only due to His grace I would be able show my grace, and looked at His face (for His grace/approval).

**(*****aitihyam) ­-*** (nan)jeeyar asked (parāśara) bhattar, ‘Is it appropriate for womanhood to climb on His chest when so many dēvatās are present watching?’;  bhattar divined his reply, ‘While being with husband, is it required to be shy in front of those who are doing personal services to them?’.

Even though all the dēvatās were thinking that they were all powerful as ‘*eeśvarōham’* , the true nature of subservience is to serve towards the togetherness of [emperumān and pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

***amudam koṇḍa kālam –*** Oh what a wonderful time it was! – says āḻvār.

{similar to ‘*madi niṟainda nannāḷāl* \[[tiruppāvai – 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppavai-01-margazhi-thingal/)\]’}.

Not only showing Himself to *ananya prayōjanar* (devotees not expecting anything else), even for *prayōjanāntaraparar* (those seeking some other thing from Him) it is a time of good life as he was available for them too.

Time when His supremacy, simplicity, and enjoyability shined!  Since all the deities came and surrendered, supremacy shined;   since not enforcing His supremacy against the *prayōjanāntaraparar* who are arrogant and think ‘*eeśvarōham’* (I am the controller/supreme), and as He performs what is required to help them, His simplicity is highlighted; due to the togetherness of [Him and pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) enjoyability shined.

(at the time when He incarnated and churned the ocean..)

***vaḷai uruvāyt tigaḻndān enṟum –*** (.. He was white in colour) **–** Since the ones in *kruta yugam* are high in *satva guṇam* (tranquillity), it is white that they are joyous about. So as per their taste, He was in the form having white colour. *vaḷai uruvāy –* in the colour of conch. (*uru* -\> colour, as a facet of beauty). It is said too, ‘*śaśi varṇam catur bhujam’.*

If asking what the authoritative reference is for the time of churning ocean was *kruta yugam*;- Since it is said as He was ‘*śvēta varṇan’* (white in colour), – it shows that it is *kruta yugam.* The great one ([*tirumaḻisai*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/16/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) [*āḻvār*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/16/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)) divined ‘*pālineermai – niṟainda kālam nāngumāy* \[tiruccanda viruttam – 44\]’ (among the four yugams, He having simplicity like milk (white))*,* showing as four colours for the four yugams.

***tigaḻndān enṟum –*** Lowering His state and showing to them, instead of them becoming bright/satiated, He becomes bright/satiated; since He shows/attends even by lowering His state, His simplicity is obviously highlighted.

***perumānai –*** (supreme / lord)**;** Not having any count of the forms He takes in these ways for devotees. If *yugams* are four, would His forms also be just four? Since there is no count for each of the yugams (since they repeat), obviously His forms also would be countless.

Since those in *dvāpara yugam* people were with the mix of *rajas* and *tamas,* He was having the form having colour as per their taste thus not having just red or blue (respectively) – since this is not said explicitly in the pāsuram, this word includes that form/colour as well (bluish black). It is said too as ‘*pāsiyin pasum puṟam pōlum neermai*  \[tiruccanda viruttam – 44\]’ (~like the outside blackish colour of moss outside, He being with simplicity).

***karu neela vaṇṇan tannai –*** In the time of *kali,* since people shun him and are not having any taste about the colour of his form – He would be having his natural form (of colour) (bluish black). From ‘*kalau jagatpatim* \[śrīviṣṇu purāṇam\]*’*– etc., (*parāśara muni* saying to *maitrēya* – people of kali yugam would not worship viṣṇu the supreme) – “Oh maitrēya! See the trouble [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) went through! You are the only one who is not affected by this period! (since parāśara in *dvāpara yugam* can see the future *kali yugam,* he is talking as ‘this period’)”

*karumai –* is the name for black; it is also the general name for colours; when it comes to His colour it would be black.

***oru vadivattu ōr uru enṟu uṇaral āgā –*** Not just lowers himself in colour, but also lowers himself in his form; since in this way for the devotees he lowers himself in his form and colour, it is not possible for anyone to measure it and say that such and such is His form, such and such is His colour; as said in ‘*sura nara tiraścām* \[varadarāja stavam – 17\]*’,* (when incarnating as dēva, human, or animal, He hid His original nature and took respective characteristics of those births), (ōr,  *sura nara tiraścām* \[śrīviṣṇu purāṇam\] (even though you are of no birth and do not go through any change, you are incarnating with the characteristics of dēvas, human, and animals, for your *leelai* (play/enjoyment)), since He comes and incarnates in different types of births, He lowered His original form too;

The adolescent age as said in ‘*yuvākumāra:’*  (emperumān is of such young age), and His colour of dark clouds would be His natural state  – could we not limit/determine His form and colour in this way? Since we also see Him as being a son (growing to different ages), it is not possible to determine the limit of Him based on age.  And, it is not possible to determine the limit of colour since He takes upon the colour based on the taste of devotees.

But what is wrong in determining/limiting by saying, how He is in śrivaikuṇṭam is the natural colour and form, and the colour and form in these incarnations as incidental (temporary)? When looking at the forms and colours that He took for the devotees, the one in śrivaikuṇṭam would look incidental/temporary, and the ones He took up for His incarnations are the ones that would be well set for Him (as actual nature); It is ‘*iccāgruheeta’* (He does that out of love for those forms/colours (due to the interest in such forms/colours by devotees), isn’t it?

***uṇaral āgā –*** Not only one cannot talk about it with words/songs; it cannot even be thought by the mind (about His many forms/colours).

***ūḻi tōṟu ūḻi ninṟu ēttal allāl –*** (other than praising Him in each eon);  we can only do this – says āḻvār. If we see His ways of many forms and many colours, other than praising Him till there is time (eternal), it is not possible to determine/limit them.

***karu vadivil sem kaṇṇa vaṇṇan tannai –*** ([āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)) divines about the form [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) showed to him. Only if there is any interest towards Him would He show as per his interest in any specific form or colour.  Since he is not interested in [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) and interested in material affairs, He showed, based on His own interest, He showed me His natural form; He showed Himself of dark cloud-like colour and as its accompaniment, the divine (red) eyes – the ones which shower the nectar that is the love (represented as red) which  is inside His mind; (like the love (rain) that is inside rainy clouds);  these too define his superiority similar to His being the [husband of periya pirāttiyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

***katturaiyē yār oruvar kāṇkiṟpārē –*** by voluntary blessing of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) I saw Him and talked to Him – is it possible for anyone else to see and talk to Him? (***katturaiyē*** – who can say? ōr, they can only talk based on imagination).

Is there anyone who can see Him *like* I saw Him? Is there anyone who can talk to Him like I talked to Him?

ōr, ***katturaiyē –*** everyone can only stay far and talk about it; is there anyone who can see Him?

(āren’t there vēdams, and āḻvārs who have done that?)

vēdams too talked about the form of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as ‘*neelatōyadamadyastā’* (nārāyaṇan is like the dark rainy cloud with lightning in the middle), thus seeing Him from afar and said approximately so, and went away.

Even those (other āḻvārs) who said ‘*neelamuṇḍa min anna mēnip perumān* \[tiruviruttam – 29\]’ (emperumān of form like black clouds with lightning) – did they see Him *in the way* I saw Him? They saw Him only by their innate indifference (to worldly matters), and innate love (towards Him);  Is there any equal to the ways in which I, who is having innate indifference to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) and love towards material matters, saw Him, in compromising ways? Since such is the case, has the vēdam or have the āḻvārs seen the scene that I saw?

***Ar oruvar kāṇkiṟpārē –*** who can see by their own accord? Like the vorśippers (*sādakar* (riśis)), did I see Him by my own efforts? Like the *prapannas* (*ārtta prapannas* (those who wish to be liberated immediately and reach śrīvaikuṇṭam), did I see Him by being fearful of this material world? I saw while I was roaming around with interest in worldly aspects, isn’t it? (so I am the main one eligible for *paragata sveekāram* (emperumān voluntarily pursuing me)).

– – – – –

Translation by raghuram śrīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)
