## 4. 4

*emperumān revealing tirumantram assuming te forms of nara (human) and nārāyaṇa (superhuman)*

**Пословный перевод:**

mada nenjamē – Oh mind which is amicable (to me)!; indirarkkum piramarkkum mudalvan tannai – Being the reason/source for indra and brahmā; iru nilam kāl thee neer viṇ būtam aindāy – being antaryāmi (inside resident) to the five core elements – the big earth, air, fire, water, space; sentiṟatta tamiḻōsai vada sollāgi – His showing the tamiḻ vēdam (divya prabandams) (which show Him clearly), and samskruta vēdams; tisai nāngumāy – being antarātmā for all the things in all the four directions; tingaḷ nāyirāgi – being antaryāmi for the moon and the sun; antarattil – when being antaryāmi in that way; dēvarkkum aṟiyalāgā antaṇanai – being purest who cannot be known by even dēvas; antaṇar māttu anti vaitta mantirattai – sarvēśvaran the secret entity who is kept safely in the knots of vēdams (anti -\> end/tail -\> vēdāntam) which are the wealth of brāhmaṇas (safe like in the knots of clothes worn); mantirattāl – by tirumantram; maṟavādu vāḻudiyēl – if you enjoy without fail/forgetting; enṟum vāḻalām – you can get good life always (in paramapadam)

**Перевод:**

**indirarkkum piramarkkum mudalvan tannai – In ‘mū uruvum kaṇḍa pōdu onṟām sōdi \[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 2\]’,  it was by squeezing the words we get the true meaning; āḻvār is showing that it is what the meaning he means, by showing it explicitly here. You see, āḻvār is saying ‘that is what I said earlier that He is the only one who is the lord!’.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*By the three pāsurams, ‘arttam aṟṟadirē’ (tirumantra arttam (meaning) has been explained). In the first pāsuram – śēśaśēśitvam (lordship, subservience) was clearly seen (as He put His divine feet on āḻvār’s divine head (tiruvadi en talai mēlavē)); In the second pāsuram – anyaśēśatvam (subservience to others) was removed. (since the misunderstanding that the three dēvas are equal, was cleared); In the third pāsuram – talked about enjoyableness (of emperumān, enjoying His divine form). ==\> (So how do these three pāsurams talk about the meaning of tirumantram?) 1st pāsuram -\> Om  (śēśa śēśitvam) 2rd pāsuram -\> nama:   (ananyaśēśatvam) 3nd pāsuram -\> nārāyāṇāya  (nāra -\> emperumān’s wealth & divine form; these are enjoyable) Then, it is a matter of finding companions for enjoying Him, together.  In that, since nityasūris are far off, and samsāris are people indulged in worldly matters, neither of them would be of companionship. So, it is the inevitable mind of his is the one that would be a companion. So, Oh mind! Let us enjoy this together, says āḻvār. Please set to enjoy through tirumantram that enlightens His true nature (mantirattai mantirattāl), about the glory of His having distinguished divine form/body talked about in the previous pāsuram, the One who is the source of creation of the world (kāraṇatvam) – please see about doing that, says āḻvār (to his mind). If he is enjoying based on tirumantram (whose main word is ‘nārāyaṇa’) , isn’t the meaning of this (word ‘nārāyaṇa’) mainly about his lordship over the two world realms, and about His nature of having qualities? (How come āḻvār is using this word/tirumantram to enjoy Him as being the source of creation of the world?). That tirumantram (word nārāyaṇa), due to many authoritative references (on the word nārāyaṇa), is about Him being the source of creation of the world. They are: ‘ēkō havai nārāyaṇa āseet \[mahōpaniśat – 1\]’ (Only He was present (it means He is the reason for creation)), and, ‘narājjātāni tatvāni \[mahābhāratam -anuśāsana – 13.12\]’ (the twenty five truths (tatvams), which can undergo change/annihilation) got created from emperumān) ,  and, ‘āpō nārā iti prōktā: \[manu smruti – 1.10\]’ (water was created by emperumān who is called naran; so the nāram indicates water; since emperumān held water (milky ocean) as his abode during the time of creation, He is called nārāyaṇan); by these and other references, His creating the world shall be said by the word nārāyaṇan. Why is āḻvār taking up mentioning His jagat kāraṇatvam? Since residents of this world is having it upside down (where some in this world think of others as lords and as reason for creation of worlds, and emperumān trying to prove His lordship), it is in this world where it is needed to use authoritative references.  It is only when someone else is using our land that we would have to show proof of our ownership; that is why he is saying so; So, Oh mind! Let us enjoy the janakan (creator of births). indirarkkum piramarkkum mudalvan tannai   iru nilam kāl thee neer viṇ būtam aindāy sentiṟatta tamiḻōsai vada sollāgit   tisai nāngumāyt tingaḷ nāyirāgi antarattil dēvarkkum aṟiyalāgā   antaṇanai, antaṇar māttu andi vaitta mantirattai mantirattāl maṟavādenṟum   vāḻudiyēl vāḻalām mada nenjamē.                            3*

Creator of indra who is accepted by all as a [jeevātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), and for brahmā who could be misunderstood as eeśvaran (controller).

While it would have been appropriate to talk first about *brahmā* who could be doubted as *eeśvaran*,  why say *indra* first – it is to show that like *indra*, *brahmā* is also one included in all that are created by [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

But, rudran also could be doubted as eeśvaran. Why has [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) not mentioned about him? As said in ‘*brahmaṇaścāpi sambhūta: Siva ityavadāryatām* \[mahābhāratam – anuśāsana\]’ ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) talking to garudāḻvār: Can say about four types of creation in another way too – *hiraṇya garbha* that is said as lotus is first creation; brahmā who was created from it is the second creation; Siva was created from brahmā – conclude this in your mind;  this was the third creation;  from him skanda was created; this was fourth creation), and in other such references, it is famous that he is the son of brahmā, and so when having said about the main of all, that is brahmā and the ending dēva of all that is indra, by *pratyāhara nyāyam* (when first and last letters of alphabet are mentioned together, it includes the ones in between as well), it is to be concluded that rudra is also included amidst this list.

Instead of saying *indira****n****ukkum pirama****n****ukkum*, why use ‘r’ (*rēphāntam)* – it is not due to having any mind of venerating them; [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is showing contempt (*kṣēpam*);  it is even while brahmā is creatable by eeśvaran ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), he is thinking ‘I am the controller of the three worlds’, (indran thinking)  ‘all the fourteen worlds were created by me and I am the controller of them’, and making everyone prostrate/worship him;

***mudalvan –*** (reason/source) – that is [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who does not have a source for Himself;  Himself who is the reason/source (*kāraṇam)* for *vyaśti sruśti*  (variegated creation of world by combining elements);

***iru nilam,*** etc. (earth, etc.)  **–** The aforementioned (about brahmā, et al) phrase said about *vyaśti* *sruśti;* by the current phrase it says about *samaśti sruśti*  (initial creation of independent five great elements (space, air, etc.), (before vyaśti sruśti);

([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) first created ***śabda*** *tanmātrai* – (*tanmātrai* -\> like the stage between milk and curd, where it is neither milk nor curd); from śabda tanmātrai, *ākāśam* (space) is created. Then from it, ***sparśa*** *tanmātrai* is created (stage in-between *ākāśa* and *vāyu*); from that *vāyu* (air) is created;  then ***rūpa*** tanmātrai is created (state in-between vāyu and tējas); then from it *tējas* is created; from it ***rasa*** *tanmātrai*; from it water; from it ***ganda*** *tanmātrai*; from that *prutvi* (earth); So each creation includes cumulative qualities of earlier stages of creation; for example, *prutvi* includes all the qualities of *śabda*, *sparśa, rūpa, rasa, and ganda*; this is also explained in ‘*pūnilāya aindumāy* \[tiruccanda viruttam – 1\]’.

Reason for saying *vyaśti sruśti* (which is a latter creation) first and then *samasti sruśti* (which is a prior creation) in this pāsuram is – since it is with brahmā et al where there may be doubt whether they are eeśvaran; there is no such doubt in core elements.

Reason for saying earth etc.,(***iru nilam kāl thee neer viṇ būtam aindāy***)**,** in a different order instead of in order of annihilation in to its immediate prior source stage, or in the order of creation, is due to some other specific thinking (of [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)):

Being a support for everything, being immobile, and not troublesome, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) first talked about such earth;  other  three core elements being mobile, being of both protection and disturbances, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) told about them in different order after earth; considering that space gives room for other elements, he mentioned it in the end;  if these are not considered, then there would not be an use for [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) saying in this order;

earlier he said ‘***mudalvan tannai’*****;** here he says ‘***aindāy’*** (literal: [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as the five elements  – which means: [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) being creator of five elements*)****.***  *So, the association in this sāmānādikaraṇayam* is – *utpādya utpādaka bhāvam (*the created – the creator)*;*

***sentiṟatta ,*** etc. – For saying later in the prabandam about creation of those who are to be obedient to śāstram (*śāstra vaśyar*; humans), here he is talking about creation of *śāstram*. Creating *tamiḻ* (*drāvida*), and *samskrutam*;

While it would be valid to say *samskrutam*  and then tamiḻ which is its changed/derived form, instead, he enumerated tamiḻ first because – it is eligible to be perused by everyone, being very good at conveying clear meaning, and it is his area of language usage, and also like samskruta vēdam, tamiḻ vēdam (divya prabandams) is also authoritative reference – hence [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) mentions tamiḻ first.

[āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)s’ words are not like that of vēdas and itihāsas and purāṇas. These words (in tamiḻ) is that of *anuśtātā* (who executes the words as per śrīvaiṣṇavam), you see! [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)s say about shunning *aiśvaryam*, *kaivalyam* and not consider them as required (whereas vēdas talk about those things also as means/goals).

***sen tiṟatta tamiḻ ōsai –*** *tiṟam -\>* its feature/capability and ways;  tamiḻ words enlighten in the best way the meanings they carry.  Not having need for an *upabrahmaṇam* to explain it more.  (There āṟē tirumangai [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)’s six prabandams that are called ‘*āru angam*’ for [*tiruvāimoḻi*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/). But [*tiruvāimoḻi*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/) does not *need* them for explaining itself); vēdas *require* *upabrahmaṇam* (to explain it well).

***ōsai***  ***(āgi)*** ***– ōsai*** is the name for poem, and sound. But here it is talking about the words of tamiḻ (*lakṣaṇai –* like: ‘house is in the ganges’ means it is in the *shores* of the ganges); this can also be seen in the next phrase ‘*vada sol’* (*words* of samskrutam);

***vada sol –*** is – talk about samskrutam. tamiḻians call tirumalai as the north border for the places (in south) where tamiḻ is in use. *samskrutam* is in vogue in north of tirumalai. tamiḻ would not be in vogue in those places;  the greatness of this place (south) is that both the languages are in vogue here;

***āgi –*** His creating śāstram is about His revealing/spreading it.

***tisai nāngumāy –*** creating all the *things/people* in all the four directions;  (It is by *lakṣaṇai*, it talks about the things- that is,) like saying ‘*mancā: krōśanti* \[mammadar’s kāvya prakāśam 2<sup>nd</sup> ullāsam\]*’* (bed is crying – means – babies on the bed are crying)*.* By this it says about creation of those who are to be obedient to śāstram (*śāstra vaśyar*; humans);

***tingaḷ nāyirāgi –*** It is as said in ‘*agnau prāstāhutis samyag ādityamupatiśtatē \| ādityājjāyatē vruśti: vruśtērannam tata: prajā:* \|\| \[manu śāstram – 3.76\]’ (offering that is submitted in the fire for *homam* (vēdic ritual) reached the Sun; from Sun, rain is obtained; by rain, food items are grown; using food items, humans live and are created (through father eating the food)); (āgi -\> becoming) shows the completion of creation.

In previous phrases it showed the meaning of ‘*nāra’* (indran, brahmā, five core elements, etc.). Rest of the phrases of the pāsuram show the meaning of ‘*ayanam’.*

***antarattil –*** (*anupravēśam* as part of creation) when being inside; when permeating within everything created; by ‘*prutivyā antara: āntanē*<sup>S</sup>*ntara:* \[bruhadāranyaka upaniśat – mādyandina – 5.7.72\]*’* (One who is present outside and inside of land/[jeevātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), One who is not known by the land/[ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), One who is being inside the land/[ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and letting it act, such [paramātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) is the ātmā that is never destroyed), so by the word *antara* it talks about the permeating quality of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

***dēvarkkum aṟiyalāgā –*** being not known/aware about Him by all that are created (even dēvas);  as said in ‘*yamātmā na vēda* \[bruhadāranyaka upaniśat – mādyandina – 5.7.72\]’ (One who is not known by ātmā), the One whom the ātmā would not know, He is being inside and called as *antaryāmi.* To indicate that [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying that He is *dur-jñēyan* (hard to learn about (antaryāmi));

But, by ‘***dēvarkkum***’, why take up about them specially? even for those who are most knowledgeable, it would not possible to know about Him, that is why [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is considering them in the phrase.

***antaṇanai*** **–** ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) being a) pure one (in mind; (won’t tolerate others’ suffering; won’t think trouble for others, etc.);  there is a greatness in [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) using this word for implying pure one. [*nammāḻvār*](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said ‘*aṟavanai āḻip padai antaṇanai* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.7.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/09/thiruvaimozhi-1-7-1-piravith-thuyar/)\]’ ( most benevolent (due to him bestowing the results); one having the sudarśana cakram (which purifies the heart); the purest eeśvarān (supreme lord)).

(Why not give meaning to that as ‘brāhmaṇa’ (in [tiruvāimoḻi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/))?) there is no brāhmaṇa holding conch and disc, isn’t it? (*kānci* swami said something along the lines of ‘there īś one, and that is ācāryan’; so we could qualify as, One using them as weapons, there is no one other than [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/));  for *kēvalan* (one who is after *kaivalyam* (enjoying one’s own [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), and not [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/))) [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is shown as the One to meditate upon as the pure one, for getting pure experience of *kaivalyam,* so namm[āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is divining in that way, in that context.

And, one who wishes to get to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) shall meditate upon His having all auspicious qualities, so that he would get those qualities;  The one who wishes to enjoy own [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (*kaivalyam)*, shall meditate upon Him as having purity; It is because ‘*jarā maraṇa mōkśā’* (liberation from aging, death) that he considers;

But, here (for tirumangai [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)), is purity required? [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) first talked about creation (*indirar, piraman,* etc.,), and then about that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) permeating in all things created (being inside as antaryāmi is required for completing the creation, and for sustenance of the created ones);  then he says that the  blemishes of those things in which He is present, would not affect Him (He would stay pure (*antaṇan*)) – this is the point wished to be said by tirumangai [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) here.

By ‘*ēśa sarvabhūtāntarātmā – apahatapāpmā* \[subāla upaniśat -1\]*’,* wherever it is talking about Him permeating, it is also talking that the blemishes of those things would not affect Him (He would remain pure); this is what is in the divine mind of this [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)? The most *vaidikas* are called *āḻvārs*, isn’t it? (*parimēlaḻagar* has shown that noble ones have a name as āḻvār, and meaning for āḻvār is noble one)

***antaṇar māttu anti vaitta mantirattai –*** The matter ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) mentioned earlier is on the head of vēdas (vēdāntam) in mystic ways.

***antaṇar mādu –*** (brahmaṇa’s wealth) – As said in ‘*brāhmaṇānām danam vēda’* (wealth for a brāhmin is vēdam), wealth for brahmins is vēdam isn’t it? wealth is not [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is said in ‘*danam madeeyam tava pādapankajam* \[[stōtra ratnam – 30](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-30/)\]*’* (your divine feet is my wealth); it is vēdam – that told about [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); that is the greatness of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) that He is identified by vēdam; only by authoritative reference (*pramāṇam*) that one can know that the wealth is [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (*pramēyam*) who is told by vēdam;

***anti vaitta mantirattai –*** the secret that is kept in vēdāntam (*anti* -\> end/tail of vēdam); ***māttu anti*** – at the end of vēdam; that is, in vēdāntam;

***mantiram*** and *sarvasmātparan*  (supreme to everyone) seems *paryāyam* (equivalent/alias/synonym); saying [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as secret is – like the one who closes the front door and back door and then opens to see a bundle of valuables, this [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is valuable so that, *mantram* is to be advised and implemented only among the four ears (ācāryan and disciple);

ōr, ‘*mantram’* is said as His divine name in śrī sahasranāmam – *mantra: candrāmśu: bhāskaradyuti*: \[viṣṇu sahasranāmam 281, 282, 283\]’ (protector of one who thinks about Him; One having comfortable light like moon; One who is too bright like the sun (these are some of His divine names)); by the root of the word *mantram,* ‘*mantāram trāyata iti mantra:*’  (since it protects the one who recites it, it is called *mantram*), so the word *mantram* is the divine name of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

***antaṇar mādu –*** (why say it is the wealth of brahmaṇas (and not him)) – saying so to show that he is not of such birth; even though all of it is bright in his mind, saying it out would be a damage to the respect for his eligibility was his state. Even while he is being an at most follower of vēdas, like saying “others’ wealth”, he is saying in this way in unconnected way in order to establish the respect of the ways.

***antaṇar mātttu andi vaitta mantirattai mantirattāl – vāḻudiyēl –*** Who is being said ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), and the one that is saying it (*tirumantram* given by perumān) are both being there ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/));

***mantirattai – vāḻudiyēl –*** if you experience/enjoy the One who is the reason/creator of the worlds (*jagat kāraṇa bhutan)*, One who is present inside all (*sarva antaryāmi*), not knowable (*durjñēyan*), and not affected by the blemishes of the body He is present in;  (these qualities are mentioned in the earlier phrases of this pāsuram);

***mantirattāl – vāḻudiyēl –*** When experiencing/enjoying that One, instead of trying to do that through vēdāntam, or through itihāsa purānams, try to do that through in the way how I enjoyed:- try to do that through *tirumantram.*  Other ways would not be delightful like enjoying through tirumantram; As said in ‘*sarvam aśtākṣarāntastatam* \[hāreeta smruti – 3.45\]’ (the meanings in the four vēdas and in other references are all present inside the *mantram* of eight letters/syllables), this *mantram* tells all the meanings collected together;

āren’t there innumerable *mantrams.* Does the word *mantram* indicate *periya tirumantram?* As said by ‘*yāni nāmāni gauṇāni* \[mahābhāratam – anusā 135.13\]’ (names based on His qualities), other *mantrams* are about his qualities only; for the aforementioned meanings (creator, antaryāmi, etc.) there is no other mantram. So that is why mantram here means periya tirumantram.

Can’t the other mantrams that indicate his permeating everywhere be considered as one said here? No, since the other two mantrams (vāsudēvan, vishnu), need tirumantram to complete the full meaning.

The mantram which he said with surety as ‘*nān kaṇḍu koṇḍēn* \[periya tirumoḻi – 1.1\]*’* is this periya tirumantram only isn’t it.

***maṟavādu vāḻudiyēl –*** if you enjoy it without forgetting;

Reason for forgetting about experiencing [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would be interest in material matters, so is [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) saying – enjoy without interest in material matters? No, that would not suit [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/); he is enjoying in his final state after *sākṣātkāram* (enlightenment / seeing [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (*kaṇḍu koṇḍēn* \[periya tirumoḻi – 1.1\])).

So, what then would be for him the reason for forgetting?  Since having connection with the body which is having the three types of sorrows (*tāpams*) (*ādyātimika, ādi baudika, ādi daivika*), seeing having association with the body, seeing his old history, and seeing the greatness of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), there is the thought of him having [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as the means for removal of such connection of body without seeing his prior history, and for getting the loved matter that is [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/);  that thought (of thinking Him as the means) would be the time of/reason for – forgetting to enjoy [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)!

So try to enjoy Him without getting into the *pūrvārdam*  (first line of dvayam – which uses Him as means, (that our thought that He is the means, even such thought is not the means; it is His thought that would be the means) (and second line of dvayam is about enjoying and serving Him);  [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is follower of dvayam isn’t it?

Why explain the meaning in this way? Earlier when we have submitted our living to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), and after that now thinking about our living/upliftment  would be like interfering in the business of others (that is, of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)); there is no difference in ineligibility between addressing one’s own well-being and thinking of creating/addressing well-being of the world (which we cannot not do);

***vāḻudiyēl –*** without involving in His purifying nature (*antaṇanai*), and in His being the means, if you would be involved in his enjoyableness (only),

***vāḻudiyēl –*** prosperity would be yours; I too am your colleague, isn’t it?

As ***vāḻudiyēl –*** talking about his mind considering it as a sentient doer, which is his faculty, is due to seeing the predominance of mind. Till the end of life, it is the mind that is the helper for involving towards [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), which is what a *mumukśu* (one wishing to be liberated from material world) expects from his mind, isn’t it?

***enṟum – vāḻalām –*** can live amongst *nityasūris* (in paramapadam) till the time [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) exists (eternal). Everywhere, it is the experience/enjoyment that is there for him. vaiṣṇavan is – a prince, isn’t it? ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is the king, and vaiṣṇavan can live with many qualities that are equal in [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), after reaching śrīvaikuṇṭam);

***mada nenjamē –*** Oh mind who is obedient to me! Without any difference in the time when getting rid of *viśaya prāvaṇyam* (interest in worldly affairs), and in the time of upliftment (experiencing [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), and during the time of enjoyment, I got the mind that is following me obediently – saying so he is celebrating/appreciating his mind. It is like ‘*nenjamē nallai nallai* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.10.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-10-4-nenjame-nallai/)\]’ ((what you have done is) good, good.).

– – – – – –

Translation by raghuram śrīnivāsa dāsan

## 5. 5

*oṇmidiyil punaluruvi orukāl niṟpa   orukālum kāmaruseer avuṇan uḷḷat(tu) eṇmadiyum kadandu aṇḍameedu pōgi   iruvisumbin ūdu pōy eḻundu mēlait taṇmadiyum katiravanum tavira ōdit   tārakaiyin puṟam tadavi appāl mikku maṇmuḻudum agappaduttu ninṟa endai   malar puraiyum tiruvadi en talai mēlavē              5*

**Пословный перевод:**

oṇ midiyil – after making the soft step; oru kāl – with one divine foot; punal uruvi niṟpa – it went all the way to āvaraṇa jalam (water surrounding the aṇḍam (oval shaped world)); oru kālum – another divine foot also; kāmaru seer avuṇan uḷḷattu eṇmadiyum kadandu – crossing even the extent of thought of mahābali who is having greatness that is liked; eḻundu – it started; aṇḍam meedu pōgi – to go even above the aṇḍam; iru visumbin ūdu pōy – permeating the big space (ākāśam); tavira ōdi – going further by crossing; taṇ madiyum – the cool zone of moon; mēlai – that is above, and; kadiravanum – the zone of sun (that is below); tārakaiyin puṟam tadavi – permeating the zone of stars (above); appāl mikku – permeating even beyond (that); agappaduttu ninṟa – and stood spanning; maṇ muḻudum – all the places; vaṇanginēnē – I got to experience/enjoy; malar puraiyum tiruvadiyē – the lotus flower like divine foot only; entai – of my lord (of such glory)

**Перевод:**

**oṇmidiyil … – With one divine foot He spanned all the earth, lower worlds; and with one divine foot , He spanned all the worlds above, says āḻvār .**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In previous pāsuram āḻvār enjoyed how emperumān is being the root of creation of world (jagatkāraṇabhūtan), and having the world as His divine body, i.e., he is the antaryāmi (jagaccareeree). Next, instead of just enjoying in the writings of vēdam (ōlaippuṟattē) in nārayanāṇuvākam about nārāyaṇan, āḻvār is enjoying how He could be seen by eyes and enjoyed, as He incarnated as vāmanan and spanned the worlds. The word nārāyaṇan is about the relationship of lord and subservient, isn’t it? By His being the reason for creation of world, it also talks about the relationship of ātmā to that emperumān, isn’t it? Saying ‘kāṇmingaḷ ulageer enṟu kaṇ mugappē nimirnda \[tiruvāimoḻi – 6.3.11\]’ (trivikraman growing and as if saying – Oh world! see this wonder), – He spanned all the worlds, for all in the world to see this meaning of relationship. So āḻvār is enjoying the meaning of word nārāyaṇan through the word of trivikraman. (In vāmana avatāram it shows His śeelam only (mingling with simpletons), why say that it is about the relationship?) āḻvār is Not saying about how vāmanan went to the yāgasālai (place of sacred rites) asking for land, etc., but saying about the part of how He kept his divine foot on everyone’s head – this is how we see that āḻvār is enjoying about Lord-servant relationship (as a lord, He kept his divine foot on servants’ heads).*

***oṇmidiyil –*** If He who is the support for everything keeps His divine foot on the heads of sentient, would the world be able to bear it? Did kamsan not get squashed when His divine foot touched him? – He keeping His divine foot, that itself, is the reason for their being able to live.  Even if He / His divine foot that is the supporting/saving becomes one supported, His quality of supporting/saving them would not be lost.

(Where have we seen this?) – Like how for His *tiruvadi****s*** (His divine feet / divine body), His *paramātma svarūpam* (entity) is the support (even though He stands on His divine feet; in the same way), for the people’s heads also He is the support.

**{Translator’s note:** Another meaning: Taking *tiruvadi*s as *garudāḻār* and *hanuman*, as they are called so,  as, Like how even though He sets His feet on their palms/head (during *garuda/hanumanta* vāhanam), He still is the support for them**}.**

Supporter cannot be one supported; One supported cannot be supporter ([jeevātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) cannot become [paramātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) and vice versa). Like how even though [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is having a place inside the body, the body is not the supporter and [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is not the one supported. That the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is the supporter and body is the one supported, is explained when talking about the identity of body and [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (*śareerātma lakṣaṇam* in śrībāśyam) (This is said in ‘*yasya cētanasya yaddravyam sarvātmanā svārtē niyantum dārayitum ca śakyam taccēśadaika svarūpanca tat tasya śareeram*’ (One that is object for the sentient ātmā, in all the ways be commanded by ātmā, be supported by ātmā, be only for that [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) – then that is śareeram.)).

(If [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) leaves the body, the body falls off. So it shows that it is [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) that is supporting the body).

***midiyil –*** (in step/trampling) (why say in this way instead of saying that His divine feet is kept as a jewel on their heads?) – Saying ‘*madeeyamūrdnānam alankariśyati* \[[stōtra ratnam – 31](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-31/)\]*’* (~oh trivikrama! .. when is your divine feet going to decorate my head (again)?), and ‘*padikkaḷavāga nimirtta nin pāda pangayamē talaikku aṇiyāy* \[tiruvāimoḻi – 9.2.2\] (your divine feet only would be the decoration for my head), that is, saying divine feet as decoration for head is only when one understands its glory and also having interest;  since the people are *dēhātmābhimāni* (giving importance to body and not ātmā), they would only think that some feet had pushed their head. So, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying this way (***midiyil***) from their point of view.

***oṇ midiyil –*** Like the breeze under moonlight He kept the divine feet amicably;  if that is not the case, they would have commanded as ‘*tiruvāṇai* \[tiruvāimoḻi – 10.10.2\]’ (by the name of tāyār), ((do not) keep your feet on me);

ōr, the stepping on them is such that if in future one becomes desirous of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), they don’t have to back out thinking about their previous state and greatness of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), so it is ***‘oṇ midi’*** (such a great stepping on his head);  One could say “What is there to not surrender to your divine feet now when we got the desire, while the divine feet which were present in my head when I was not caring for you?”.  When one could argue,  ‘*lōkavikrānta caraṇau* \[vihagēśvara samhitai, padalam (part) 22*’* ((please keep on my head) your divine feet which spanned the worlds), [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would not have any opposing reply (but have to keep His feet on the head), you see!

***punal aruvi –*** His divine feet grew downwards and extended not just till the earth, or seven seas, or the seven continents (*dveepams*), or the mountain of *cakravāḷa* (mountain that surrounds the previous layers)*,* or the great water (*mahā jalam*), or the covering wall of the aṇḍam (*aṇḍa kadākam*), but till the water covering the aṇḍams (*āvaraṇa jalam*);

Why go till that extent, when the ones to be protected are within the aṇḍam? The protector’s eagerness is not just till the extent of the ones to be protected; As said in ‘*adanil periya en avā* \[tiruvāimoḻi – 10.10.10\]’ (my desire which is bigger than that) – His desire is like [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) ’s desire, you see!

What this one’s love (prēmam) did, would His affection (*vātsalyam*) not do that?

If asked – if the divine feet intruded the covering wall of the aṇḍam and go till the water covering aṇḍams, would the *aṇḍa kadākam* (covering wall) not be destructed? It does not have to be so, when the subtle one (*sūkśmam*) permeates the tangible/gross ones (*stūlam*). Space (*ākāśam*) (already) permeates that *aṇḍa kadākam;* the *mūla prakruti* (source of material realm) is subtler than that *ākāśam;* the divine auspicious body of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is subtler (*sūkśmam*) than that *mūla prakruti.*

***oru kāl niṟpa –*** one divine feet standing;

later in this pāsuram he says ‘*tiruvadiyē vaṇanginēnē’*  (vorśipped only the divine feet); earlier – ‘*tiruvadi en talai mēlavē* \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thirunedunthandakam-1/)\]’ (divine feet on my head); given that, instead of saying *tiruvadigaḷ* (divine feet), his saying *kāl* (foot) here is from the point of view of *samsāris* (worldly people); other than thinking – someone’s leg is in my head – they don’t know the way of considering it as one to be attained and which is enjoyed by the nityasūris, such lord’s divine feet.

***niṟpa –*** as He took up to use the divine feet to span (just) the earth, it showed the beauty of breaking (as it went up to the water layer of aṇḍam (*āvaraṇa jalam*) and stopped (*niṟpa*) as if by pulling the reins. His greatness is like supporting like the reins to stop the divine feet!

***oru kālum –*** earlier, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) enjoyed the divine feet that spanned the earth; by this phrase [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is enjoying the divine feet that spanned the upper worlds;

***oru kālum –*** also the other divine foot; saying ‘*oru* *kālum* (one leg also)’ instead of ‘maṟṟa kālum’ (other leg also) is – “Oh how wonderful *this* *one* is”. When [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) was enjoying that divine foot, ‘There cannot be anything more enjoyable than this!’ was his state; as he saw this other divine foot, he is saying ‘There is nothing more enjoyable than this one!’ (***oru -\>*** unique/wonder)

***kāmaruseer*****,** etc. –  going beyond (the knowledge that is extent of mind of *mahābali*)*.*

***kāmaruseer avuṇan –*** *mahābali* having attractive nature;  instead of censuring him as ‘one having haughtiness’, ‘thief of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s places’**,** [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying ‘greatly fortunate’ about him. What is the reason for this? Unlike us who are here at a later time, he got to see the beauty of the form and simplicity (*śeelam*) of śrī vāmanan. Are there any fortunate people like him? – is the thought of [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) . Oh! instead of being as ‘*adiyēn’* (servant), I too should have stolen the places of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), and should have artificially taken up generosity and do *yajñam* (worship by sacred rites).   ***kāmaru*** -\> liking.

***eṇ madiyum kadandu –*** (crossing the limit of *mahābali*’s thought in mind (*eṇ -\> eṇṇam* -\> thought)) – As He said ‘*mūvadi* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 3.8.9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-8-9-kolvan-nan/)\]*’,* mahābali was like ‘rest of the places are mine only’.  After He spanned everything including the earth, he was like – there are those other worlds above for me’. As He spanned the space above, he realized he lost even the worlds above and became loser. So, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying *‘kadandu’* (going beyond the limit of thoughts of *mahābali*).

***aṇḍa meedu pōgi –*** One divine foot itself going beyond the wall like limit of the aṇḍam (*aṇḍa bhitti*). Instead of saying in the order in which He spanned, why first say about ***aṇḍa meedu pōgi***? Due to his great love of the victory of śrī vāmanan, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is setting a marker (*koṇḍaik kōl*) at the limit of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s extent of spanning.

Like how śrī jāmbavān mahārājar said due to his great love for His victory, ‘*jitam bhagavatā jagat* \[harivamśam – pratamapari – 42\]*’* (jāmbavān saying to dēvas: all the worlds have been won by [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (by incarnating as trivikraman), (so please come back to your places)) – by going around the *cakravāḷagiri* and announcing by drum, about His victory to everyone.

Saying ***meedu pōgi –*** shall be considered as ***pōga*** (to go), to mean, to go to it. This is similar to ‘*tiruvudai adigaḷ tam nalam kaḻal vaṇangi* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.3.8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/07/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-8-nalum-ninradu/)\] (vorśipping the good feet of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is having tāyār in His divine chest), where it is taken as ‘*vaṇanga’* (if you worship); There also [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) saying that first is to set the marker (*koṇḍaik kōl*) of the full extent.

It is to be read as ‘***eḻundu – iru visumbin ūdu pōy****’* (started and went through the space).

***eḻundu –*** The moment water drops fell on his palm (water poured into the palm of vāmanan by mahābali, to complete the pledge to give three steps of land), Him raising from the place of *yajñam* of mahābali;

***iru visumbu –*** the big space; since it is big when considering earth, and due to the great love in [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s growth, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is referring to *space* as ***iru visumbu.*** (BīG space);

***ūdu pōy –*** (went through) He raised his divine foot such that the space that is above the earth and below heaven that is of hundred thousand *kādam* (*kādam is* approximately a mile or two and a half) got fully filled plump by His divine foot; creation of space became fruitful; when the divine feet had not permeated there, it was like a waste, you see.

***mēlai …*** – Sun’s place is the upper border of space, and lower border of heaven; above that, for one hundred thousand *kādam* is the place of moon. But why not [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) say about the place of the Sun and then about that of the Moon (***mēlait taṇ madiyum*** (moon) **kadiravanum** (sun) **…**)? Since He kept His divine and completely delicate feet on people having self-interest and not interested in [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is attending to service for that divine feet by trying to make it cool down (through moon’s place); to show that, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying ‘***taṇ madi’*** (cool moon);  (Since it is already known that moon is cool, he still specified that attribute **taṇ,** for this purpose only. He did not specify any attribute of Sun in this pāsuram, so he does not want to think about its heat)  He did not say ‘*vem* *kadirōn’* **,** but just ***kadiravan*** only.  ***mēlait*** (above) ***taṇ madiyum*****,** implies ‘*keeḻai’* (below) ***kadiravanum.***

***tavira ōdi –*** going above that. By ***ōdi*** – (running)  – having to stop it with difficulty as the divine foot is running due to haste (looking for devotees); As said in ‘*tāy nādu kanṟē pōl* \[[mudal tiruvantādi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi/) – 30\]’ (like the calf seeking its mother), like how a devotee would show haste in reaching the lord, the lord would have the haste to get His devotee. For everyone, there would be haste for getting what they love, isn’t it?

***tārakaiyin puṟam tadavi –*** permeating the place of stars, and going above that; above the place of moon, there is the place of stars for one hundred thousand *kādam.* Above the upper limit of the place of stars, is the place of *śimśumāra prajāpati.* Like the planets, he is one of the stars; he is of twelve hundred thousand *kādam*.

***tadavi –*** lord (caressing) trying to see if any devotee would be found, like how one would caress around in the floor (in the dark) searching for something that fell from self.  When going and trying to find someone and not finding anyone, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) says, ***kadandu*** (crossed that).

As said in ‘*pudait tān peridē puvi* \[periya tiruvantādi – 75\]’ (Is earth like an elevated place that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s flood of kindness not flow through it?), what a loss this is to be not having the divine feet of trivikraman on our head, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) .

***tadavi –*** (softly caressing) – since He would keep the divine feet in positive ways, that touch would be of delight.

***appāl mikku –*** going up till the brahma lōkam (abode of brahmā); since mahābali stole only the three worlds, why go above beyond that?  Like how the three worlds were ruined due to *anyaśēśatvam* (subservience to others such as *indra*), the other worlds were also ruined due to feeling themselves as independent (*svasvātantriyam*); because their mind is having the state of ‘*eeśvarōham’* (I am the lord); disaster of nature of [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) due to *anyaśēśatvam* is not (that worse) compared to disaster due to *svasvātantriyam.* The former has (at least) accepted subservience.

**{Translator’s note:** This is also succinctly put by [piḷḷai lōkācāryar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/pillai-lokacharyar/) in *mumukśuppadi* as ‘*piṟarkkuriyan Ana anṟu than vailakṣaṇyattaik kātti meetkalām; tanakku ennum anṟu yōjñataiyum kūda aḻiyum* \[mumukśuppadi – 81\]’ (~When one agrees to be subservient to others, they can be retrieved back by Him showing them His greatness compared to those of others; but if being self-independent, even eligibility to retrieve would be lost)**}**

(The goodness for Him is based on the goodness He does for His devotees).

***maṇ muḻudum agappaduttu*** **–** Spanning the fourteen worlds; saying about earth by the word **‘*****maṇ’*** **,** he says ‘*muḻudum’*, thinking about the worlds above it as well;

ōr, by ***‘maṇ’*** to mean place, it is talking about the places above.

Instead of saying ‘***aḷandu*****’** (spanning), [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying ‘***agappaduttu’***  (capturing), which shows His falling on them with love *(mēl* *viḻum*)*,* and their sliding away.

***ninṟa –*** stopping satisfied having done His wishes (of capturing His devotees); like how a devotee would stand satisfied, here the lord is satisfied after getting (reforming) the devotees.

***entai*** *(*my *svāmi -\>* my *lord* / father*)* ***–*** when seeing śrī vāmanan’s beauty and simplicity, proper thing to do would be to ignore the kingdom and pierce into the chest and writing that he is subservient to Him – instead indra got his kingdom and went away; mahābali kept him (his generosity) for himself (to consider himself great), and gave (only) his kingdom to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); (But) I gave myself to Him, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) .

***malar puraiyum,*** etc.  (divine feet that is like lotus flower) – I got to enjoy the boundless enjoyment that is His divine feet.  Earlier, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) said ‘*taḷir puraiyum tiruvadi* \[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 1\]’ (divine feet that is like a flower bud blooming); here he says ‘***malar puraiyum tiruvadi’*****.**  Reason for this, is – there, it was about [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) withering due to separation from this [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) , and then as He kept His divine feet on [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) ’s head, it started becoming fresh and blooming; here – as the ones who enjoy, as they started to enjoy, it is talking about the enjoyableness of the divine feet (like a lotus flower).

***malar puraiyum –*** due to presence of devotees the divine feet is blossoming. (the other lotus would blossom in the present of Sun).

***tiruvadi –*** the divine feet is the place the devotees go to for enjoyment (compared to other parts of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)).

by saying ‘**E**’ (*avadāraṇam*) in ***tiruvadiyE****,* (*only* the divine feet), it shows the specialty/difference from/compared to other parts of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

This divine feet is the one not having any dearth of greatness (*mēnmai)*, kindness (*neermai*), and beauty of shape (*vadivaḻagu*).  There is no dearth of greatness to the divine feet as He kept His divine feet on the heads of those who were like ‘*eeśvarōham’* (I am the lord); for getting His property (devotees), He rushed eagerly, which shows there is no dearth of His kindness *(neermai);* attracting even the bad one like mahābali, there is no dearth of his beauty of form. So there is no need (for devotees) to go to any part other than His divine feet, isn’t it?

***vaṇanginēn –*** I got to enjoy; this meaning is as said too in ‘*nama ityēva vādina:* \[mahābhāratam – śānti parvam – 344-45\]*’* (identity of those who got liberated into śrīvaikuṇṭam would be to be doing salutation (*anjali*)) (so) they would be happy; they would be of nature of saying ‘*nama:’* in their mouth)*,* (saluting is) about enjoying Him by the liberated (*muktas*);

for [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) , the divine feet are the ones to surrender to (*upāyam*); and also ones to enjoy (*upēyam*);  during the time of surrendering it was said as ‘*ulagam aḷanda pon adiyē adaindu uyndēn* \[periya tirumoḻi – 5.8.9\]’ (reached and survived by the divine feet that spanned the worlds);

Is there anyone to surrender to other than the beautiful One who creates interest even in *mahābali* who was disinterested?

Is there anyone to surrender to other than the one who is eager to go and get His own property ([ātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), by pleading for it)?

Is there anyone to surrender to other than this simple One who made His divine body which cannot be seen by any senses, to be seen even by the *asuras*?

Is there anyone to surrender to other than the lord who spanned the fourteen worlds?

(even other incarnations of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) are shunned here)

Being lord of everyone, simple, easy, and being one who creates desire in Him, is the one who is also the One we could surrender to and experience the enjoyableness in Him.

[āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) said, ‘*trivikrama tvaccaraṇāmbuja dvayam madeeyamūrdānam alankariśyati* \[[stōtra ratnam – 31](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-31/)\]*’* (Oh [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who performed incarnation as trivikraman! When would your divine lotus feet decorate my head?).

[udaiyavar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/) said, “*lōkavikrānta caraṇau śaraṇam tē’vrajam vibhō* \[[śaraṇāgati gadyam 6th cūrṇai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/12/saranagathi-gadhyam-6th-churnai/) – gadya trayam (quoting from vihagēśvara samhitai – part 22)\]’ (I surrender only to the divine feet of yours that spanned the worlds).

– – – – – –

Translation by raghuram śrīnivāsa dāsan

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

## 6. Part 1

*alampurinda nedum tadakkai amarar vēndan   anjiraippuḷ tanippāgan avuṇarkkenṟum salam purindu angu aruḷ illāt tanmaiyāḷan   tānuganda ūrellām tan tāḷ pādi nilam parandu varum kaluḻip peṇṇai eertta   neduvēygaḷ padu muttam unda undip pulam parandu pon viḷaikkum poigai vēlip   pūnkōvalūr toḻudum pōdu nenjē.                        6*

**Пословный перевод:**

manamē – Oh mind!; alam purinda nedum tadakkai amarar vēndan – One having long and huge divine hand that gives until (the one asking) saying enough, head of the nitya sūris (eternal residents of śrīvaikuṇṭam ); am siṟai puḷ tani pāgan – being the unmatched rider of garudāḻvān having beautiful wings; enṟum salam purindu – (and emperumān) always creating problems; avuṇarkku – for the asuras; angu aruḷ illā tanmaiyāḷan tān – not having any kindness towards them (angu) as His nature, such emperumān who is sarvēśvaran (lord of all); uganda – presiding in; Ur ellām – all the divya dēśams; than tāḷ pādi – praising His divine feet; nilam parandu vaum kaluḻi peṇṇai – river then-peṇṇai is growing spanning all of the world, and having muddled (water); eertta nedu vēygaḷ padu muttam unda – and pushing (into the fields) the pearls that are in the bamboo sticks that it pulls along with it; undi – and (such pearls) are pushed aside (as weeds, by the farmers); pulam parandu – spreading into the fields; pon viḷaikkum – and growing gold; vēli – having wall on four sides; poigai – the water tanks; pūnkovalūr – the beautiful tirukkōvalūr; toḻudum – let us enjoy it; pōdu – you shall come (Oh mind!)

**Перевод:**

**alam purinda nedum tadakkai – Arm that is long due to giving, says āḻvār. Giving such that the receivers say ‘enough’ (alam);  giving such that one does not have to go to another person’s house seeking. By this it says – one who has taken the shelter of emperumān, is not interested in other matters.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(sangati -\> review of/connection with previous pāsurams) Not just seeing tirumantram (that is, sarvēśvaran) through authoritative references as said in ‘mantirattai mantirattāl maṟavādenṟum vāḻudiyēl’, but to be seen by eyes and enjoyed He incarnated and kept His divine feet on everyone’s head and suggested about the relationship; thinking of that, and, of His blossoming divine feet at that time, āḻvār became sad for not being able to enjoy that incarnation because of living later than that time; So, emperumān said, “It is only for getting those to experience me, those who are living at a later time than my incarnation, have I come and stood with elegance in tirukkōvalūr”, and so showed āḻvār the place that He is fondly present (ugandu aruḷina nilam); and so āḻvār is saying ‘Oh mind! go there to enjoy that opportunity of us’. These ugandaruḷina nilam (divya dēśams) are the frontier of the meaning of tirumantram (that He is of at most saulabhyam (easy to attain / kind). When mentioning tirumantram first (in the very first pāsuram of this āḻvār), and when in the stage of experiencing its meaning, āḻvār enjoyed that as only ‘sūḻ punal kudantaiyē toḻudu \[periya tirumoḻi – 1.1.2\]’ (~worship the divya dēśam of tirukkudantai) (So the meaning of tirumantram is divya dēśam). But, would it not be sufficient to say (about the divya dēśam, as in) ‘pulam parandu pon viḷaikkum poigai vēlip pūnkōvalūr toḻudum?’ Why (describe emperumān by) saying other prior words? It is saying the history of the place due to emperumān being present with eagerness. Both supremacy (so, talks about this) and kindness are in the meaning of the word nārāyaṇan.*

ōr, ***alam purinda –***  As He engages into generosity, He would take back His hand only when the receiver says ‘enough’, and not due to any limit in His generosity. He stops only because it is not possible to pour from ‘*padakku’* in to ‘*uḻakku’* (these are measuring containers of different sizes; this says that He would stop only due to the limit of the receiver). When seeing His generosity, the receiver’s expectation is like the water in the sand of a foot step compared to water in the sea (*kadalil kuḷappadi*).

*puri* -\> giving and withdrawing the hand (after them saying enough).

Giving till saying enough is – giving such that one does not have to go to anybody else seeking more/anything.

{From śrī. u.vē. bhūvarācāryar swami’s kālakśēpam} – Poet kambar describes daśaratan’s generosity, in kamba rāmāyaṇam – a person would ask for some wealth from daśaratan; daśaratan would give so much of it that have to be loaded in to several carts. When that person would return with these gifts, he would see another person who is on his way to the king asking for help. The first receiver would say that there was no need to go to the king since he himself could share from the carts. Then the 2<sup>nd</sup> receiver would do similarly to a 3<sup>rd</sup> person and so on; so ultimately, there was only one person who would have gone to daśaratan seeking wealth. daśaratan gave so much that the receiver not only would not have to go to another person’s house seeking, but he himself would be able to give enough to other people. Since rāmān is such daśaratan’s son, He too got this quality of generosity – ‘*piṟandu padaitta audāryam}*.

For one who is *ananya prayōjanan* (one who does not ask Him for anything else), He has given Himself and His belongings, and there is no need for giving anything else. Such recipient also would not ask Him anything. Such recipient will be immersed in experience of Him, and He would stay with the eagerness of – what more shall we do for this devotee (‘*en seyvan enṟē* \[periya tiruvantādi – 53\]’ (what more shall I do (for my devotees)).

***nedum tadakkai –*** arm/hand that grew watered by that generosity;  growth of protector is due to getting time/opportunity to engage in the path of protecting.  (long and wide) arm such that it is not possible to limit the generosity;  also, arm that extends up to the presence of all those seeking (other things / Himself);

***tadak kai –*** As said in ‘*bāhuccāyām avaśtabda:’* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – sundara kāṇḍam 34-31\] (world is under the shadow of the shoulders of supreme rāmā (says hanuman to seetā)),  shoulders having more space even if whole world is sheltered under them. Arm/hand that is for everyone,  apt to be a shelter, and being in itself the purpose;  ***tadak*** ***kai –*** hand that is wide.

***amarar vēndan –*** is talking about those (nityasūris) who live based on this generosity. ***amarar –*** those who do not have death. If asking whether *nityasūris* have the applicability of death: since they would lose their breath if they don’t get the experience of togetherness with emperumān; but since there is no actual death, it says ‘***amarar’*****.** As their experience of Him is eternal, they also are being eternal.

‘***vēndan’*** **–** He who is having heads down focused on getting their subservience to Him. ***vēndan –***  king. (Do they not also have the crown, as said in ‘*mudi udai vānavar* \[[tiruvāimoḻi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/) – 10.9.8\]’ (nityasūris wearing crown)) – when He is getting them to serve Him He would be having such crown; they, as said in ‘*aham sarvam kariśyāmi* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – ayōdyā kāṇḍam – 31.27\]’ ((iḷaiyāḻvār to perumāḷ) – I would do all services to you (while You are awake or asleep)), they would be having crown of subservience. Coronation is applicable for both.  It is said too, as ‘*arasamarndān adi sūdum arasu’* \[perumāḷ tirumoḻi – 10.7\]’ (~the kingdom of wearing the divine feet of perumāḷ in his head (for bharatan)).

***anjiraippuḷ tanip pāgan –*** ([āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)) is talking about one nityasūri as an example of how emperumān accepts the subservience of all nityasūris – unmatched rider / protector of periya tiruvadi (garudan) having beautiful wings.  īsn’t there a need for a ride/transport only when there is some war (வினை) to do? (there is no war in paramapadam) – even when there is no war, kings would carry flower bouquet in their hands and go around in a ride during festivals. It is said too, as ‘*ōdum puḷḷēṟi* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.8.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/09/thiruvaimozhi-1-8-1-odum-pulleri/)\]’ (climbing on garudāḻvār wander around (fulfilling his desire to serve him));  since periya tiruvadi cannot sustain himself and emperumān also cannot sustain Himself if there is no such ride, and also to give divine experience to the nityasūris, there would be this riding (‘*garuda sēvai’*) in śrīvaikuṇṭam also.  It is said too, as ‘*seḻum paṟavait tān ēṟit tirivān* \[[tiruvāimoḻi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/) – 10.6.5\]’ (~would climb on the beautiful bird (garudāḻvān) and roam around).

***am siṟai –*** beautiful wings. When He is flying on periya tiruvadi, the wings would be a decoration like a beautifully folded 2<sup>nd</sup> cloth (*angavastram*) on Him. ōr, when going around with flower bouquet, for removing the tiredness, like fanning (*ālavattam*), he is the one having wings as fan for emperumān. It is said too, as ‘*vyajanam trayeemaya:* \[[stōtra ratnam – 41](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-41/)\]’ (periya tiruvadi performs services also as a fan). ōr, we can consider him as having wings that are having the beauty of helping to bring emperumān to His devotees.

***puḷ –*** (bird) – Do nityasūris have bad karmas due to which they could be born as a bird \[It is said that when one commits an offense by speech, they are born as a bird/animal\]? When [pirātti and sarvēśvaran](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) are inside the divine screen (privacy), their togetherness would be such that moon, and breeze would also have to back off in their movement;  like when emperor and queen are alone, old people and eunuchs would be allowed inside for serving them, the nityasūris would take on appropriate form of body for performing personal services. They would take on different forms whether it is of snake, bird, or plant (ādi śēśan, garudāḻvān, tiruttuḻāy);  while a worldly person would take on body based on karmas, they would take on these forms for the states of services to emperumān and pirāttiyār.

***tanip pāgan –*** (*pāgan* -\> mahout) – by this it suggests that He is being under the guard of him, and he being under the guard of Him; mahout would control the elephant through his mind, and he would be serving the elephant as well; identity of [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) is to be present well in the back of periya tiruvadi and conduct him; it is ‘***tani’*** (matchless), because there is nowhere else this is seen (with other demi-gods, etc).

ōr, ***tanip pāgan –*** unique/matchless conductor for *all* the nityasūris. It is said too, as, ‘*ayarvaṟum amarargaḷ adipati* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.1.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/)\]’ (lord of nityasūris).

***avuṇarkku,*** *etc. –* by this – since there is no question of enemies in that abode (śrīvaikuṇṭam), it talks about Him incarnating as rāma, kriṣṇa, etc., for saving the devotees, and to remove their enemies. Removing the enemies is also one use of His incarnations (as said in [śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/), etc).  Why does it talk here only about removal of enemies (and not about grace to devotees, etc)? Since removal of enemies is done first (for getting  the services of devotees), it talks about that.  It is said too, as ‘*sarva pāpēbhyō mōkṣayiśyāmi* \[[śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/) – 18-66\]’ (~would liberate (you) from all sins) (where it talks about removal of the unwanted, to imply goodness that would go with it); for [jeevātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) which by nature there is sense of service, if sins are removed, then servitude would automatically flourish; removal of enemy/hurdle (sins) is the one to be achieved. That is why this is said as a representative of the subsequent result as well.

***avuṇar –*** by this – it is not talking about *asuras.* it is talking about those having *ahankāram (*ego, I) and *mamakāram* (possessiveness, mine) and being as said in ‘*na namēyām* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudda kāṇḍam – 36-11\]’ (would not prostrate in front of anyone; would rather be split into two and fall (sideways), said rāvaṇan); since, while śrīprahlādan being in that birth (of asuras), he is praised as ‘*upamānamaśēśāṇam sādūnām* \[śrī viṣṇu purāṇam – 1-15-156\]’ (prahlādan is always being an example for noble people), and since while being a rākṣasa by birth, śrīvibheeśaṇāḻvān is praised as ‘*vibheeśaṇastu darmātmā* \[śrīrāmāyaṇam – āraṇya kāṇḍam – 17-23\]’ (vibheeṣṇanan is of noble character (sūrapaṇaka to śrī rāman about her family)), and while being in the birth of dēvas, jayantan (*kākāsuran*) became the target of *brahmāstram*, this is not based on caste/descent. It is based on nature of them as having asura character.  It is said too, as, “*viṣṇu bhakti parō dēvō vipareetastatā’’ura:* \[viṣṇu darmam – 109-74\]” (In this world there are two types of births, as dēvas and asuras. One who is having devotion towards śrīman nārāyaṇan is said as dēva; one who is in other ways is said as asura).

***enṟum salam purindu –*** like how it is a constant for them to be as ‘*na namēyam’,*  for Him also causing trouble to them out of anger, is a constant.   ***purindu –*** those who go to emperumān do not have to return empty handed, you see. It would not be void of benefit but rather they would get His grace or His anger. Is it a benefit for them if they are getting destroyed? Even though they may not consider it so, it would end up being the good thing for them – like how when someone tries to cut his head with a sword, it is beneficial to remove that sword,  it is beneficial for emperumān to remove the body that is trouble for emperumān and His devotees. Since all the acts of emperumān would be regarding protection, doing those things also would be of protection. It is said too, as, ‘*rāma bāṇā sanakṣiptamāvahat paramam gatim* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – kiśkintā kāṇḍam 17:8\]’ (The arrow that is shot by the valiant rāmapirān, and which would put one into paramapadam, gave to valiant *vāli* that paramapadam), ‘*talaiyai āngē aṟuppadē karumam kaṇḍāy* \[[tirumālai – 8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thirumalai-8/)\]’ ((for such asura characters) such things are required (because that would be good for them));

***salam purindu –***  is – doing the destruction due to anger. tamiḻ pundits would give meaning for ‘*purindu’* as ‘doing’.  Doing (getting) ‘***salam’*** (anger) – is the meaning.  Meaning of ‘*purindu’* is about doing or giving.

***angu aruḷ illāt tanmaiyāḷan –*** Like how it is a constant for Him to show grace to His devotees, it is a constant for Him to not show grace towards those others. Anger and kindness are opposite qualities, but it can be present in one place due the difference of where they are applied. We see how the same stone (called candrakāntam), which is a non-sentient, is being as water in the presence of moon, and is solid in its absence (so it is not a wonder for such quality to exist in [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)). ***tanmaiyāḷan –***  having His nature as being so. It is always His nature to be generous and kind towards devotees, and be angry and strict towards their enemies.

(to be continued in pāsuram 6 – part 2).

– – – – – –

Translation by raghuram śrīnivāsa dāsan

pramātā (preceptors) – [http://acaryas.koyil.org](http://acharyas.koyil.org/)
