## 7. 7

*vaṟpudaiya varai nedum tōḷ mannar māḷa   vadivāya maḻuvēndi ulagam āṇḍu veṟpudaiya nedum kadaluḷ tani vēl uytta   vēḷmudalā venṟānūr vindai mēya kaṟpudaiya madakkanni kāval pūṇḍa     kadipoḻil sūḻ nedu maṟugil kamala vēli poṟpudaiya malai araiyan paṇiya ninṟa   pūngōvalūr toḻudum pōdu nenjē               7*

**Пословный перевод:**

vadivāya maḻu ēndi – carrying beautiful axe; vaṟpudaiya varai nedum tōḷ mannar māḷa – (When incarnating as paraśurāman), having strength and mountain-like tall shoulders, such that the kings (like kārtaveeryārjunan) die; ulagam āṇḍu – (incarnating as śrī rāman), ruling the word for a very long time; veṟpu udaiya nedum kadalūḷ tani vēḷ uytta vēḷ mudal venṟān – (when incarnating as kaṇṇan) of emperumān who won those like murugan (subrahmaṇya) who threw his spear into the sea that is having mountain inside it; Ur – such emperumān‘s divine place; vindai mēya kaṟpu udaiya madam kanni kāval pūṇḍa – (place which is) guarded by one who had done penance in vindyā hills, who is having great knowledge, who does not turn away from a task undertaken, and who is subservient to emperumān only, that is durgā; kadi poḻil sūḻ – (place which is) surrounded by fragrant gardens; nedu maṟugil – having wide/long divine streets; kamalam vēli – having ponds with blossoming lotuses; poṟpudaiya malai araiyan paṇiya ninṟa – and the place where emperumān is standing such that the king of the abundant people of hills would come and surrender to Him; pūm kōvalūr – such beautiful tirukkōvalūr; toḻudum – shall enjoy that place; nenjē – Oh mind!; pōdu – Ye come!

**Перевод:**

**vaṟpudaiya, etc. – āḻvār‘s excess of fear is such that emperumān has to show His strength and make āḻvār feel better.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As āḻvār set out to enjoy by taking along his mind also, that emperumān who is enjoyed was the one being in the divine pose of trivikraman who without differentiating between noble and otherwise had kept His divine feet on their heads and gave Himself to them. Thinking about this, āḻvār forgot that it is to be enjoyed, and started becoming afraid. Why be afraid, is His standing in the pose not par for the enjoyment?  By the state of one like himself who are the enjoyers, and by the state of Him who is enjoyed, fear bubbled up. āḻvār‘s love for Him – is such that fear and doubt is shown in the wrong place (where emperumān is the almighty). āḻvār‘s love blinds him such that instead of devotion being an instrument for enjoyment, it becomes an instrument for protecting Him. āḻvār‘s enjoyment is emperumān‘s beauty, etc., and as he thought about emperumān‘s tenderness, he would have to be scared. When vibheeśaṇāḻvān came amicably and stood for surrendering, mahārājar (sugreevan) due to being blinded by love, had said ‘vadyatām badyatām \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudda kāṇḍam – 17-29\]’ (This vibheeśaṇan along with his ministers shall be killed through severe punishment). āḻvār too thought that since this place is full of asuras and rākśasas (by character), as he sees this he would have to be afraid. (Are there enemies here? Yes, there are asuras as said in ‘vipareetastata asura:’ (Those who do not have devotion towards viṣṇu are asuras)). (ācāryas always followed emperumān as He would go in procession. As emperumān would return to temple, embār and mudaliyāndān would prostrate to each other and hug each other and jump happily with fear removed as emperumān has returned safely. piḷḷai uṟangāvilli dāsar would carry a knife with him during procession of emperumān, so that if there would be any small discomfort for emperumān then he would kill himself with the knife). When āḻvār loses (the experience) due to fear, it is on the protector to remove such fear and give the experience of Himself to āḻvār. So too, like how perumāḷ decided that it would not be possible to convince mahārājar by showing about the ways of kings like about His promise that He would not reject those who surrender to Him, as ‘na tyajēyam \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudda kāṇḍam – 18-3\]’ (it is not possible to reject him due to any case), had instead showed the strength of his shoulders, and removed the fear of sugreevan,  (tirukkōvalūr emperumān) showed his strength/pride (incarnations), the guardian of His place (durgā), and the protection present in the place (description of the place), thus giving āḻvār such knowledge, and so āḻvār being relieved of his fear, tells, ‘Oh mind! Come! let us go and enjoy!’.  Shall fear thinking about delicateness of Him; shall be without any fear by thinking about His bravery and virility.*

***vaṟpudaiya nedum tōḷ mannar –*** Divining about the strength of these kings like talking about the strength of hiraṇyan, rāvaṇan, and such, is to show His strength.  They are having strength and having shoulders like mountains. If anything hits a mountain those would get decimated, and no damage would be done to the mountain. Likewise, enemies would get destroyed and these kings’ shoulders would not be affected due to that.

***mannar māḷa –*** (āren’t the kings saviours, is it appropriate to destroy them?) – If they have decorated themselves as per kings’ form, but are having excess of *asura* (demoniac) qualities, then [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would destroy them, is the point. (*uḷam tottu iraṇiyanai –* emperumān checked within the chest whether there was any bit of good quality in hiraṇyan)).  In the case of such kings, He would not think as – oh they are kings who are there to protect their people. He would only see their demoniac qualities.  For those kings like *kārtaveeryārjunan* of thousand hands to be destroyed without any bit left.

What is it that He did that they got destroyed –

***vadivāya maḻuvēndi –*** (He *held* the axe in His hands) – As He held the axe in His hand, they simply got destroyed. They got destroyed seeing the sharpness and how His hand held it. (bhattar nirvāham is similar for (*karan*), ‘*ven narakam sērā vagaiyē silai kunittān* (siriya tirumadal) – seeing the way perumāḷ held the bow, karan did not have to go to hell; that posture itself was like hell for him).

Like hiraṇyan became dead seeing the form as narasimhan;

or, the state  of (paraśurāmar) holding the axe in his hand is a beauty to behold;

Whereas vaiṣṇavas think that this form of holding axe with anger is not to be supported (vorśipped), this [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying ‘***vadivāya maḻu’*** (beautiful axe);  even if [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) holds some and any weapon, it would look beautiful; the divine conch is also beautiful to look at as and when He holds it.

***ulagam āṇḍu –*** Since there is no kingdom for śrī paraśurāmāḻvan, this is about rāmāvatāram. paraśurāman, due to the anger that the kings had affected His father, had cut the heads of all the kings and filled the pond with their blood and performed the karmas for His father, is all that He had done. But then, like it is said in ‘*snātvā bhunjeeta* \[dharma śāstram\]’ (you have to eat only after having taken bath (‘tvā’ being ‘lyap’ pratyaya in samskrutam)), isn’t the phrase ‘***ēndi (ulagam āṇḍa)’*** (samskruta ‘*lyap*’ pratyaya) set to be performed by one person only? Due to same [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) doing both, it is acceptable to say in this way. Since in one incarnation both actions were not done, it is acceptable also to consider this as were done in different incarnations. It is for the same reason that further up in the pāsuram, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is going to talk about the actions of incarnation as kriṣṇan, since it is the same emperumān. (Since paraśurāman gave his power to śrī rāman, and so since paraśurāman’s powers were included in that of śrī rāman, it is acceptable to say that both of them had ruled the world, so ruling the world is talking about śrī rāman only). When paraśurāman saw perumāḷ, He dropped the axe and took up the bow; perumāḷ got paraśurāman’s bow and His powers and used that bow for winning rāvaṇan and other evil persons, and saved the world such that the strong do not trouble the weak. Thus it talks about perumāḷ (śrī rāman) ruling the world in this way.

***veṟpu*** etc. – *subrahmaṇyan* who is like a young calf who threw his spear into the big ocean that is having a mountain (***veṟpu***); That is, when mountains were able to fly and were destroying the worlds, *indiran* used his *vajra* weapon and cut the wings of mountains. One of the mountains escaped and hid inside the ocean. Since he (*subrahmaṇyan*) is the head of the army of dēvas, he learned its hiding place and destroyed it – this phrase talks about this incident.

***nedum kadal –*** *very* vast ocean. Talking about this to show that it was so vast that it would be very hard for anyone else to find where that mountain was hiding.

***tani vēḷ uytta –*** Throwing the spear that is unparalleled – if he throws his spear then it would not require any other weapon to kill the enemy. While ‘***vēḷ***’ refers to *kāman,*  referring to him as ‘***vēḷ***’  is about talking of his winning enemies due to his youth and strength.

***mudalā venṟān Ur –*** place (tirukkōvalūr) where The Man (kriṣṇan) who won such *subrahmaṇyan* and others; in the battle with *bāṇan*, it is he who came first and foremost, so his name is being mentioned first in the list (of family of Sivan who came fighting). It is said too as ‘*nēr sarindān kodik kōḻi koṇḍān* \[[tiruvāimoḻi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/) – 7.4.8\]’ (came to the battle, one who is having rooster in his flag). Since there were innumerable persons who came fighting emperumān, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is talking about it in short, saying ‘***vēḷ mudalā’*****.**  Similar to ‘***muṇḍan neeṟan*** \[tiruccanda viruttam – 71\]’ (a bigger list is present in this pāsuram).

***venṟān Ur –*** (kaṇṇan’s grandson was taken away while sleeping, as arranged by *bāṇan*’s daughter *uśā,* and was kept in her room with love; as her father learned of this, and), as  (*bāṇan*) captured and tied *aniruddāḻvān*, śrī nārada bhagavān came and informed kriṣṇan; He went there riding periya tiruvadi (garudāḻvān);

(*bāṇan* then approached *Sivan* to protect him from this). Since he (*Sivan*) is of ignorant mind to not realize that it is not possible to win if it is kriṣṇan on the other side, he thought about his promise (to *bāṇan*) to protect him, and so came and opposed kriṣṇan. Since they (*dēvas*) are not like those (*rāvaṇan*) who say ‘*na namēyam’* (won’t bow),  but since they came to fight thinking ‘*eeśvarōham*’ (we are supreme),  He made them realise and leave *bāṇan* and run away showing their back.  Not killing *bāṇan* but only destroying his many shoulders/arms (except two) is – to avoid *uśā* crying ‘I am fatherless’;

leaving them so had also helped in another way – ‘Oh the trouble I went through taking you as my protector’, and ‘Oh the trouble I went through imagining me to be your protector’ saying  so they could hug each other with (remaining) two arms and weep.

***venṟānūr*** **–** place (*tirukkōvalūr*) where such a hero (*veeran*) is present.

***vindai mēya,*** *etc –* [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is divining about the strength of protection for this divine place.

***vindai mēya –*** She who was present in the forest of *vindyā* mountains;  while he could have referred to her by just saying ‘*durgai’*, he is saying this (***vindai mēya*****,** ***madak kanni****)* to – talk about the forest where she performed penance to become a protector of emperumān. Even though it is by true nature (of ātmā) one could render service, one would do penance to get the recognition as protector of a divine place, by acceptance of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); (why sing about her?) – her greatness is resulting in protection of the place, so [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is divining about her.

***kaṟpudaiya –*** *kaṟpu –* (result of *kaṟṟal* (learning) – knowledge).  She having the richness of knowledge/ability of tying the eyes of an enemy of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s divine place, and to release it when he becomes amicable.  ***kaṟpu –*** as female virtue; like how  a female would try to save her virtue even by forgoing her life if required, this demigod is protecting the place even forgoing her life;

***madak kanni –*** *madam* – is a quality where one does not forgo an association with someone. Here, even if there would be any strong enemies to this divya dēśam, she would not shy away, but would protect it. ***kanni –*** implies not be subservient to any one other than emperumān.

***kāval pūṇḍa –*** not doing the protection based on someone’s instigation, but due to her own desire.

***kāval pūṇḍa –*** due to overflowing affection, she involved herself in protecting the place. If she marries someone then there would be a break in her protecting emperumān, ***kanni*** (virgin) implies she is focussed only on protecting emperumān.

***kanni –*** is the name for something that does not disintegrate (similar to ‘*kannit tamiḻ*’), so since she does not have annihilation, there is no annihilation (for the protection) of the place. (During the incarnation of kriṣṇan), she had undertaken His command and had gone from tiru āyppādi to maturā, and she always holds affection towards Him. (Since she was born before Him, to yaśōdā, and as an elder sister she was protective towards kaṇṇan, so) [*nanjeeyar*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/) says ‘she is *tambi vaḷaikkāri isn’t it’* (so says [periyavāccān piḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/periyavachan-pillai-english/) in this commentary. *vaḷai* – protection). Even though one is a servant , due to one’s overflowing affection would take up the role of a protector. periyāḻvār too said ‘*nin sevvadi sevvi tirukkāppu* \[[tiruppallāṇḍu – 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-1-pallandu/)\]’ (divine protection to your beautiful divine feet).

***kadi poḻil*****,** etc. – arcade having fragrance as its identity. Place which is of spring season eternal. **Now, the intention is not to talk about the enjoyability of the place; since this is the occasion of talking about the things that aid in removal of** [**āḻvār**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)**‘s fear of safety of emperumān,  our ācāryas divine the meaning of this phrase as aid of removal of fear**. There is no other destiny than enjoyment (of things in this world), and liberation (to śrīvaikuṇṭam);  the beauty of this place would attract and tie those who are after material pleasures of this world, and thus would not let them go inside the divya dēśam; it is such that ‘do you have to go inside and bring trouble to the One? Is it not enough for you to enjoy the beauty of the place?’.

***nedu maṟugil –*** (***maṟugu –*** divine streets) The divine streets that are beautiful to see by length and breadth, and so would tie those who escaped the beauty of the arcades, and thus prevent them from going inside the temple.

***kamala vēli –*** Place having waterways where lotus blooms through out the breadth of them;  since the streams would be full of flowers (like a fence), it would attract and keep such people; by this, it says that that the place is having such protection as if there is no other need of protection by people.

***poṟpudaiya***, etc. – (***poṟpu*** – connection); even if there is no (aforementioned) protection, there is the strength of policing of the divya dēśam preventing them entering it. This is the place of residence of the head of people living in the neighboring mountains, who is fond of the divya dēśam and who is into protecting it. Unlike protecting it after doing penance, he protects it considering it as his duty.  ōr, ***poṟpu*** to imply all the kings around are connected to each other and the group is protecting the divya dēsam; hence mentioning them together in singular (as ***malai aṟaiyan***).

***paṇiya ninṟa –*** place of the One who is surrendered to by everyone;

***ninṟa –*** (emperuman) stood firmly, feeling satisfied and happy about the purpose of His incarnation only after everyone surrendered to Him; those *who* surrender to Him would feel that their purpose is satisfied after getting immersed fully in the enjoyment of Him; in the same way, when people were for other things, He voluntarily showed their relationship with Him and when they surrendered to Him based on that connection, is it required to mention explicitly about the satisfaction He would derive from that?

***pūngōvalūr toḻudum –*** that divine place (the people of that divine place) is enjoyable even without considering Him; this is how this [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is fully attached (*maṇḍinapadi*) to divine abodes.

***pōdu nenjē –*** even after his fears were cleared (based on aforementioned), the divine mind of his was still in fear and still fainted; we don’t have to fear since this place is protector in all the ways, Oh mind! let us go so we can experience this, says āḻvār.

***toḻudum pōdu nenjē –*** He is calling like how someone would call ‘let us go for feasting’ (which is enjoyable to everyone);  it is said too as ‘*toḻudu eḻu* \[[tiruvāimoḻi 1.1.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/)\]’ (worship, be uplifted and be enthusiastic);  there is no existence/life when not vorśipping;  [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying ‘***toḻudumpōdu nenjē’*** (Oh mind! let us worship, come on!).

===========

Translation by raghuram śrīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 8. 8

*neeragattāy nedu varaiyin ucci mēlāy   nilāttingaḷ tuṇḍattāy niṟainda kacci ūragattāy oṇṭhuṟai neer vehkāvuḷḷāy   uḷḷuvār uḷḷattāy ulagamēttum kāragattāy kārvānattuḷḷāy kaḷvā   kāmarupūngāviriyin ten pāl mannu pēragattāy pērāden nenjin uḷḷāy   perumān un tiruvadiyē pēṇinēnē                        8*

**Пословный перевод:**

neeragattāy – Oh One who is giving divine presence in tiruneeragam divya dēsam!; nedu varaiyin ucci mēlāy – Oh One who stood at the top of tall and great tirumalai!; nilāttingaḷ tuṇḍattāy – Oh One who is giving divine presence in the divine place called nilāttingaḷ tuṇḍam!; niṟainda kacci ūragattāy – Oh One who is giving divine presence in the divine place called ūragam by pervading the whole of kacci (by your qualities)!; oṇṭhuṟai neer vekhāvuḷḷāy – Oh One who is in sleeping posture at the beautiful shore of water tank that is in tiruvehkā!; uḷḷuvār uḷḷattāy – Oh One who is present in the hearts of those who think of you (as their leader)! (that is also a temple for Him); ulagam ēttum kāragattāy – Oh One who stood in the divine place called ‘tirukkāragam’ for the whole world to worship!; kār vānattuḷḷāy – Oh One who lives in the divine place called kārvānam!; kaḷvā – Oh the thief (who hid the divine form and not showing it to the devotees)! (there is a divya dēsam called kaḷvanūr); kāmaru pūm kāviriyin then pāl mannu pēragattāy – well set in the town of tiruppēr (of appakkudattān) that is on the south shore of very beautiful kāvēri!; en nenjil pēradu uḷḷāy – Oh One who is showing Himself to my mind without break or going away!; perumān – Oh One having many many divine places!; un tiruvadiyē pēṇinēnē – I am calling for your divine feet (wishing to see it)

**Перевод:**

**tiru neeragam – image courtesy – http://www.divyadesam.com**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As āḻvār became sorrowful and thought – ‘We could not experience at that time when He strode the worlds’, and (as He said), ‘I am there with that beauty in tirukkōvalūr, come and experience it!’, as āḻvār took along his mind to experience Him (there), he forgot about the saurya (not running away in a fight (absence of parāngmukhatvam)), and veerya (not getting confused and agitated during fight (unlike arjuna did in kurukśētra)) of āyan (of tirukkōvalūr divya dēsam), also forgot about experiencing Him, and as he saw Him in front of his eyes he saw the soft delicateness of emperumān, and thought about the nature of the place which is having many asuras, and thought ‘Oh what might happen to this divine One’, thus fearing for Him. It is the nature of ātmā to be loving of and be caring of Him. As He showed His valor etc., and cleared his fear, he took his mind along and was involving in experiencing Him – in the previous pāsuram. In this pāsuram – āḻvār is deciding that we shall go to tirukkōvalūr and experience the emperumān. Since it is he (who is having extreme love toward emperumān), his divine feet are not moving; thinking about the beauty etc., of āyan (of tirukkōvalūr) his senses got weakened as said in ‘kāl āḻum nenjaḻiyum kaṇ suḻalum’ \[periya tiruvantādi – 34\] (~ my feet would get stuck, mind would be destroyed, eyes would not focus, even if I just hear about how He is lying down in the divine milky ocean), and to be according to the true nature of self (to be subservient and not do something on own efforts) – that is, both at the time of surrendering to Him, and at the time of enjoying Him (by doing services) – surrendering to Him when He comes and pushes us to surrender to Him, and enjoying Him when He comes with His own efforts and making us enjoy Him, is our true nature. This is as said in ‘kūvudal varudal seyyāy \[tiruvāimoḻi – 9.2.10\]’ (~You call me and make me serve you, and You come to me), ‘en tiṟam sollār \[tiruvāimoḻi – 8.3.7\]’ (He is not saying a single word about me His ardent devotee). Thus due to decimation of senses, and not having eligibility (as per his nature), he is not able to go there; but he did not have the place he was in to happen to be tirukkōvalūr; he started calling (the place); as he was longing to enjoy tirukkōvalūr, why would he start calling other divine places?  Like how one would pick and collect multiple grains (spilled in the field), as āyan’s nature is shining in divya dēsams, āḻvār is calling them based on that. It is like (praising śrī rāmā through multiple examples as) said in ‘viṣṇunā sadruśō veeryē \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – bāla kāṇḍam – 1-18\]’ ((śrī rāman) is equal to viṣṇu in manliness; He is pleasing to the eyes like the moon; is like the annihilating fire in His anger; is equal to the earth in His patience).*

***neeragattāy –*** Oh one residing in tiruneeragam! Since One who is of *neermai* that is of quality of *neer* (water), is residing here, the place is referred to as *neeragam.* Even though the nature of [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is same for everyone, it is due to karma there are differences of being human, dēvas, etc., and also based on that there are differences in their knowledge and thoughts; but water is the life line for every one of them; likewise, the [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) of tiruneeragam  is the life line for everyone. So the divine feet that stored all the worlds is the life line for everyone; since this nature of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is highlighted in this divya dēsam, Oh One who is enjoyable to everyone without any limits, is not showing yourself to me! wonders [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

***nedu variyin ucci mēlāy –*** Oh one who is divining His presence in tirumalai such that You are enjoyed not only by those in the earth, but also in the higher worlds.  This is as said in ‘*vānavar vānavar kōnodum namanṟeḻum tiruvēngadam* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 3.3.7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thiruvaimozhi-3-3-7-sumandhu-mamalar/)\]’ (nityasūris (eternally free souls of paramapadam), with [sēnai mudaliyār (viśvaksēna](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/senai-mudhaliar/) – who is their leader), bowing (which highlights their total dependence), feeling accomplished (having realized their true nature), divine place having the name tiruvēngadam); the state of tirumalai reminds of the divine feet of nāyan who strode all the worlds; You who would show up even for those who think of themselves as lords, is not showing yourself to me! wonders [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

***nilāt tingaḷ tuṇḍathāy –*** this is also being the name based on the nature of nāyānār ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), the lord). Oh, like the moon who is complete in all the arts, One who helps those who suffer from the three types of *tāpams* by clearing those *tāpams,* and being sweet to those who enjoy You!

As said in ‘*sūryāmśu janitam tāpam ninyē tārāpati: śamam* \[śrī viṣṇu purāṇam – 5-10-3\]*’* (like how a discerning mind would remove the sorrow caused by pride, moon the head of stars removed the heat caused by the rays of sun), for the people who suffer due to the heat of the sun it is the moon that is reason for their calm and comfort;  ***nilāt tingaḷ –*** during waning phase its light would be dimmed, and so it is mentioned as ‘*nilāt tingaḷ’*  (to imply full moon). Since the divine abode is a part (***tuṇḍam***) of the earth, he is saying ‘*nilāt tingaḷ* ***tūṇḍam***’ based on that. Since he mentions about it specifically saying ***tuṇḍam,*** he is saying it to show its uniqueness compared to other divya dēsams. This reminds about [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is present with removal of tiredness he got due to striding the worlds, and being lovely. Oh one who removes the tiredness and gives happiness to everyone, is not showing yourself to me! wonders [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

***niṟainda kacci ūragattāy –*** Oh one who is present and divining in the divya dēsam of tirukkacci ūragam for the whole of kānceepuram to be filled with your beauty and kindness! He is present in tiru ūragam that reminds us of His divine posture in tirukkōvalūr! ***niṟainda kacci –*** As said in ‘*śōbhayan daṇḍakāraṇam* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – āraṇya kāṇḍam – 38-15\]’ (śrī rāman, by His brightness, appeared like a young moon which rose just then, in that forest), the whole of tirukkacci is filled with His brightness; also kacci that is filled with divine abodes (tirukkaci being one having so many divya dēsams).

![⚠ картинки нет на сайте: рис-001.jpg](<https://www.divyadesam.com/hindu/temples/kanchipuram/images/ulagam-1s.jpg>)

ūragam – image courtesy divyadesam.com

***oṇ tuṟai neer vekhāvuḷḷāy –***  Oh one who is in sleeping posture in the shore of beautiful tiruvehkā! Unlike other places, since this is the one where [tirumaḻisaip pirān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/16/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) got down and took holy bath, this is referred to as beautiful shore. He is staying put and lying down in this shore to show that He obeyed [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/16/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)‘s words. Going behind the devotee when he left, and coming back as that devotee came back and lying down back again but in the opposite direction keeping His head where His divine feet were earlier – isn’t this how the ‘*pāratantryam* toward devotees’ is present in there!

[tiruvehkā](http://thiruvekka.koyil.org/) – sonna vaṇṇam seyda perumaḷ – image courtesy divyadesam.com

As said in ‘*irunda ūril irukkum mānidar ettavangaḷ seydār kolō* \[periy[āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) tirumoḻi – 4.4.7\]’ (Oh what penances they might have performed to be rewarded of being in the town where His devotees are present!), for śrīvaiṣṇavas it is the place where a śrīvaiṣṇavan lives is the preferred place – likewise, it is for [eeśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) the place that is preferred is where there is a connection of devotee.

As said in *‘vāsal kadai kaḻiyā uḷ pugā kāmar pūngōval idai kaḻiyē paṟṟi ini* \[[mudal tiruvantādi – 86](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-86/)\]’ (neither going out nor going in, you are staying in the in-between place (because that is where we three mudal āḻvārs had stayed once)!), how He had entered and jammed Himself into the place where the three āḻvārs were present, and even after they left, He did not have the mind to leave that place – this is how it reminds us about the nature of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) at tiruvehkā (being dedicated to devotees).  *soṇṇa vaṇṇam seyda perumān* mingled with the devotees with his divine head in the place of the feet. Oh You who is steadfast in the place that was of the devotees, are not showing Yourself to me who would not bear the separation!

***uḷḷuvār uḷḷattāy –*** Oh one who lives in the hearts of those who live with the thought that there is a lord for me!  It is for reaching a devotee’s heart that He is present in a divya dēsam (this is the reason for [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) to talk about the heart of devotees when in the setting of listing these divya dēsams). ‘*ninṟadu endai* \[tiruccanda viruttam – 64\]’ (He being present in these divya dēsams is doing all those sitting, standing, etc., in my mind too), etc. You who would live in the hearts of those who have got even a little love toward You, are not showing Yourself to me! wonders [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

***ulagam ēttum kāragattāy –***  Oh who is divining his standing presence in *tiruk kāragam* for the whole world to heap praises! As said in ‘*lōka vikrānta! caraṇau* \[vihagēśvara samhitai padalam 22\]’ ((*vikrama*:  = stepping) – Oh [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)! adiyēn am leaving all the relatives, and all the means, and all other destinies including *kaivalyam*, and surrendering unto the divine feet of You who strode the three worlds!), for all the worlds to argue (with Him saying – Your divine feet is common and available for all), and surrender, He having such history of taking the three steps, that āyan being amicable for all to surrender, and this divya dēsam (too) lets us think about that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)! Oh You who is available for all to surrender, are not showing Yourself to me!

***kār vānattu uḷḷāy –*** Oh one who lives in *tiruk kārvānam,* who is having the nature of cloud whose nature is to pour rain! The divine name for divya dēsam is based on the nature of this emperumān. Cloud would pour rain without expecting any benefit, and without any distinction of water or land (to everyone) – this divya dēsam reminds of the generosity of āyan (of tirukkōvalūr), who kept His divine feet on the heads of all the people whether they longed for it or not.

***kaḷvā –*** Oh one who steals! (*apahāram*). We would not call Him ‘*kaḷvā’* since we do not have such love towards Him like this [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/). Stealing is – hiding something that belongs to one by making it not his. His stealing is – not showing His beauty which is to be experienced by His devotees. *ātmāpahāram* is when the sentient does not agree that the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) belongs to Him;  whereas that is how it is regarding [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), we don’t agree to that but say ‘*svatantrōham’* (I am independent) – this is our stealing.  Is your preventing my stealing, is to steal (not showing) Your [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (divine body)? Making the meaning of [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) to flourish, is for destroying the meaning of ‘*bhaktānām* \[jitantē stōtram – 1.5\]’ (everything of You are there not for you; they are there for Your devotees)?

***kāmaru pūm,*** etc. – Oh One who is having presence in *tiruppēr(nagar; appakkudattān*) that is near the south of kāvēri river that is beautiful which is wished to be seen! [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is adding this adjective (to river *kāvēri*) because it is having *kōyil* (tiruvarangam) in the middle. *kōyil* (śrīrangam) is such that it is preferred by the ones who are to be saved and for the One Who saves.  ***then pāl –*** checking whether there are people going by the shore (on the south), He is staying put near the shore (to get them). Like people who depend on the shore for their livelihood – He is also by the shore for His being the protector to come to bear.  ***mannu –*** He is staying put thinking He won’t leave unless everyone one in this world reached Him; ‘Is there anyone who will come and reach Me?’ – thinking so You are waiting for such opportunity; but You are not showing Yourself to me! This divya dēsam also reminds us of āyan (of tirukkōvalūr) who cannot not sustain Himself without having devotees following Him;

***pērādu en nenjil uḷḷāy –*** You are not making Yourself the target of my eyes, but then why are You appearing in my mind? While making Yourself grandly visible in my mind like it is a brahmōtsavam, why are You keeping Yourself away on the outside like without any *kāhaḷam* (instrument that makes sound), or pole/flag,etc. (that are used during celebrations). If You would not show Yourself in my mind, I would live without thinking about You; I did not get to live by seeing You on the outside; did not get to live by Not seeing You on the inside (mind); did not get to live by dying – says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/);

he could not live by seeing – because it is He who has to give that vision; he could not live by forgetting Him in his mind – due to the distinct greatness of what he sees in his mind;  not able to live by dying – due to the wish the he would be able to see Him again;  even if separated, the distinct greatness of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is such that one would keep saying ‘that! that!’ about Him. Only if it is about other matters, that one would be able to forget as time goes by; this matter is such that it would increase the longing as days go by;

he is not able to live by dying – because being present under His control – (sītā) pirātti could not proceed with attempting to hang Herself – because She is His property; [*nammāḻvār*](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)*,* even though was desperate when singing ‘*uyirināl kuṟaivilam* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 4.8.10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/04/thiruvaimozhi-4-8-10-udambinal-kuraivilla/)\]’ (There is no use of my [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (self) which is not liked by [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), he had to beg Him for ending his life as he sang in ‘*sāmāṟē* – *paṇi* (*kaṇḍāy*) \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 4.9.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/04/thiruvaimozhi-4-9-1-nannadhar-muruvalippa/)\]’ (to die (and shed my body), you have to mercifully speak a word (as in the case of śrī bhagavat gītā 18.66 “*mōkśayiśyāmi*” (I will free you)).

***perumān –*** Oh one who does not have any count of such divya dēsams! Since there is not a count of divyadēsams that remind us of āyan (of tirukkōvalūr), and since he ([āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)) does not have the ability to talk about all those divya dēsams, he is collecting them together with this word (***perumān***).

ōr, ***perumān –*** Oh the lord who accepts our services! Oh I am suffering because of knowing the relationship with Him, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/). If I had not known that then I would have happily done whatever came to mind as one interested in worldly matters, like the *samsāris* (people interested in worldly matters)*.* Is it correct for him to feel bad for not being like the *samsāris* who undergo the three types of suffering? – the suffering of *samsāris* due to their *ahankāram* and *mamakāram* are tolerable; the suffering that [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) goes through by not getting [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) even though he wishes for it so much – this suffering is not tolerable, isn’t it? ([āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/) too said) ‘*sarvam sahē* \[[stōtra ratnam – 25](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-25/)\]’ (my) sorrows are tolerable (but not that of your devotees), and as I said in ‘*nān kaṇḍu koṇḍēn nārāyaṇa ennum nāmam* \[periya tirumoḻi – 1.1.1\]’ (I have obtained the divine name nārāyaṇā), I thought it is enjoyable when I got You. But it has ended up being the reason for my sorrows! When one gets the knowledge about their true nature, there would be happiness only when one gets life that is according to that nature; when that is not the case, it is all sorrows only, isn’t it? It is for one who is having indigestion that the hunger is preferable; for one who is very hungry, it is that hunger that is the cause for his suffering. ‘On one hand I am not enjoying in worldly matters like the *samsāris*; on the other hand, I am not enjoying in Your experience like the *muktātmās* (liberated from the cycle of birth); I am not being in the state of ‘*tat tasya sadruśam bhavēt* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – sundara kāṇḍam – 30.30\]’ (it would fit His nature (to get me when He likes)) like *mumukśus* (those wishing to be liberated) who have understood their true nature; like being not in any of these groups, I am having this state of suffering!, wonders [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

[emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) asks – For you who were having affinity towards bodily affairs and earthly interests, I showed you the true goal that is suitable for your true nature, and affected you such that your union with and separation from me would be happiness and sadness for you – while there is all these help of mine for you, why are you calling in this way like an ungrateful?

***un tiruvadiyē pēṇinēnē –*** I am not calling while being ungrateful for things You have done for me; in those matters I am grateful to You; I am calling due to the longing to see Your divine feet – says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/). Is my *puruśārtam* (aim) about getting a few better things compared to worldly people? (No,), it is about seeing You with my eyes that is the *puruśārtam* for me, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/). Because You have not shown Your divine feet, the good things You did for me have become the reason for my sorrows, is the thought here.

***un tiruvadi –*** Your divine feet that is both the means and destiny. Mentioning many of these divine abodes and then saying ‘***un***’ (Your in singular) ***tiruvadi,*** shows that [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is experiencing the ways of āyan (of tirukkōvalūr) only in all these divya dēsams.  ***un tiruvadi –*** Your divine feet that is of such nature.

## 9. Part 1

*vangattāl māmaṇi vandundu munneer   mallaiyāy madiḷ kacci ūrāi pērāi kongattār vaḷangonṟai alangal mārvan   kulavaraiyan madappāvai idappāl koṇḍān pangattāy pāṟkadalāy pārin mēlāy   pani varaiyin ucciyāy pavaḷa vaṇṇā! enguṟṟāy emperumān unnai nādi   ēḻaiyēn inganamē uḻi tarugēnē                         9*

**Пословный перевод:**

munneer mallaiyāy – Oh One who lives in tiruk kadal mallai (divya dēsam, modern day mahābalipuram) by the shore; māmaṇi vandu undu – which brings and pushes the best gems; vangattāḷ – by ships!; madiḷ kacci ūrāy – Oh One who lives in the city of kāncee having divine ramparts / walls!; pērāy – Oh One having divine presence in the city of tiruppēr!; kula varaiyan madappāvai idappāl koṇḍān pangattāy – Oh One having on one side (of His body) the rudran who is having in the left side (of his body) acquiescent/beautiful pārvati, who is the daughter of himavān who is the best of kings; kongu Ar vaḷam konṟai alangal mārvan – and such (rudran is ) having in His chest the garland of koṇṟai flower that is having honey and much beauty; pārkadalāy – Oh One who is resting in the divine milky ocean!; pārin mēlāy – Oh One who incarnated in the earth (for doing good to those living here)!; pani varaiyin ucciyāy – Oh One who stood at the top of cool divine tirumalai (tiruvēnkatam)!; pavaḷa vaṇṇā – Oh One having pleasant divine body like a coral!; engu uṟṟāy – where have You gone in to?; emperumān – On my lord!; unnai nādi – searching for You; ēḻaiyēn – adiyen having the wish in vain, am; uḻitargēnē – roaming; inganamē – in these ways only

**Перевод:**

**vangattāl māmaṇi vandu undu munneer mallaiyāy –  A devotee (named puṇḍareekan) had in this shore put together a garland, and thought ‘we should decorate Him with this before it loses its sheen’ (and since emperumān lives on the shore of the other end of the sea), he started draining the sea water thinking he can not go near Him across the sea unless the sea dries up; seeing his love towards Him, He came to the place where he had made a place for Him and rested there on the ground (without ādi śēśan), the place which then became to be known as tirukkadal mallai.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since the place he is in is not got to be tirukkōvalūr, and since emperumān did not come to the place where he was and showed Himself, he called Him using the true characteristics of emperumān that would be the reasons for Him to come and show Himself – in previous pāsuram. In this pāsuram – ‘For those who wish the see the beauty of my striding the worlds, I am showing that beauty in the same pose by coming to tirukkōvalūr; so āḻvār who wishes to see that should come here and enjoy it, is all there is’ – is this what He is thinking (and so is not showing Himself in the different place where I am now)?, wonders āḻvar, and also fears,  ‘Is He not showing Himself here to me, thinking that I am a rogue?’; Then, he thinks ‘If we consider His ways, He would go by Himself and show His form to those even having a lit bit of interest; He is One who shows up without considering the merit or absence of it in a person; so such blemish would not occur in Him; so my losing His divine vision is due to my sins only; so due to my wish in vain and not able to let go of Him, and so toiling in this body, my state has become like that of being yātanā śareeram (body which one gets into while going to hell) which would not be destroyed nor be tolerable, and so am being put up in this place and be sorrowful’  – āḻvār is sad thinking about his state.*

But, while it would have been apt to just say ‘***munneer mallai****’* why add adjectives (***vangattāl māmaṇi vandu undu*** *…*) – ship is one that would push things from one island to the other; likewise, He who is set in paramapadam got pushed into this world and got Him lying down on the floor – due to the love for His devotee. Here the ship is His love towards His devotees.

***māmaṇi –*** gem of very high value; This love pushed that gem which is as said in ‘*kaiyār cakkarattu en karu māṇikkam* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 5.1.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/05/thiruvaimozhi-5-1-1-kaiyar-chakkaraththu/)\]’ (with the divine hand well fixed with  the divine disc, One who made me enjoy your attractive blue gem like divine form!), and ‘*vānavar ucci vaitta peru māṇi* \[periya tirumoḻi – 9.5.4\]’ (nityasūris keep Him in their head as a valuable gem).

***vandu undu –*** He is lying down in the sea shore with no one to come and have Him! Just as how one who knows the value of ruby gem would keep it safely tied in a valuable cloth, One who is ever enjoyed by *nityasūris* is the One who is here lying down the shore – there is no one who understood this greatness and nurture Him here, that the state in which you see, He is staying here on the floor; even if some people come to Him they would ask for other things and go away, alas, there are none who wish to get Him.

***munneer mallaiyāy –*** One of great valuables of another abode (place; paramapadam), has come to this island (place) that is tirukkadal mallai, but is not sold (wished for), but is still lying down on the ground of this shore without getting tired (of waiting).  It is the *anantasāyī*  (One who rests on anantāḻvān (ādi śēśan)) who for a devotee has come here and is lying down on the ground, you see.  By these adjectives, there is no one who has understood His greatness and nurture Him, and thus talks about the lowness of this place (world);

One who could come to the place of a devotee and show Himself, is the One who is not showing Himself to me who is longing.

***madiḷ kacci ūrāy –*** Even though (in previous pāsuram) he talked about these divya dēśams, there is no blemish of ‘saying same thing again in pāsurams’ (*punar ukti dōśam*) due to difference in application here; (there, it was to say that He is similar in the good characteristics of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) of tirukkōvalūr;  here he is mentioning the divya dēsams for different reasons);

Unlike in *tirukkadal mallai* where He is present without the care of anyone, here some people of high quality have constructed these walls which we got the opportunity to see – says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/). He talked about his sadness of seeing no one to care about [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) there; here he is caring that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) has been saved with the walls raised; even while he is sad due to not seeing [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), his true nature is such that he is caring seeing whether there would be any danger to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). ōr, the walls are such that the enemies cannot get into the place, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

***mallaiyāy madiḷ kacci ūrāy –*** stuff that comes in at a shore would get sold in a city only; likewise, [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) came ashore at tirukkadal mallai and got sold (cared for) at kanceepuram.

Caring for Him considering His tenderness, and, grieving/being angry considering ones’ own state (of not getting the vision of Him) , are both apt for a jeevātmā.

***pērāy –*** in the aforementioned, he talked about how [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is anxious whether He would get the devotes;  here – as said in ‘*tirumāliruncōlai malai enṟēn enna tirumāl vandu en nenju niṟaiyap pukuntān* \[[tiruvāimoḻi](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/thiruvaimozhi/) – 10.8.1\]’ (~(even though I was not  interested in You), just based on the reason of my saying ‘tirumāliruncōlai’ You came and took me as yours), He is present (in the divya dēśam *tiruppēr*) to get the devotees for Himself, and that is He who is not taking me who cannot survive if separated from Him, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).  By this, it says that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) comes to the place of His devotees and shows Himself.

Now it talks about His coming and showing Himself without looking at one’s disqualifications.

![⚠ картинки нет на сайте: рис-002.jpg](<https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Harihara_VA.jpg>)*emperumān having Sivan in His divine body*  
(image coutesy: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Harihara\_V%26A.jpg)

***kongu*** etc – Like a saying in tamiḻ, ‘*nāyē koḷ, pēyē koḷ’*, that refers to sivan who is being with ghosts, got a place in the divine body of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), but that divine body is being hard for me to reach, laments [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/). Oh the One who in a part of His divine body is having sivan who is decorated with ‘*konṟai’* garland that is having honey, fragrance and beauty. ***kongu –*** honey. ***Ar –*** excess of it. ***vaḷam –*** beauty. (***aṟṟu –*** a meaningless affix allowed by tamiḻ grammar). Beauty of a flower – being fresh, and full of fragrance.

***kulavarai*** etc. – One who is having *pārvaty* as is wife;  while it would have been enough to just say,  ‘giving part of his divine body to *rudran*’, this pāsuram is giving so many attributes to him describing so much, which is to make his wrongful pride explicit: trying to be equal to [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) who is decorated with divine basil/*tiruttuḻāy* and who is the [husband of śrīdēvi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), he wears *konṟai* flower garland and thinks ‘I am eeśvaran’ – it is to such sivan of bad disposition that He gave his body.

***kongattār vaḷankonṟai alangal mārvan –*** Being opposite of ‘*taṇḍuḻāy mālai mārban* \[[tirumālai – 5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thirumalai-5/)\]’ (One having chest decorated by tiruttuḻāy garland). Seeing [eeśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)  protecting/sustaining the worlds wearing unique garland of tiruttuḻāy, he is wearing *konṟai* garland thinking ‘I too should need a garland if I should be called eeśvaran and so look for one’.

***kulavaraiyan madappāvai –***   Being a counterpart of ‘*malar magaḷ virumbum nam arumpeṟal adigaḷ* \[[tiruvāimoḻi 1.3.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-1-paththudai-adiyavar/)\]’ (being joyed about the two aspects of being *eḷiyavan* (simple to attain) and *ariyavan* (difficult to attain)) being desired by One who is having the lotus flower as her residence, our master who is difficult to attain). Oh He is being [śriya:pati (husband of lakshmi)](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/),  when we get such opportunity we should also be with a wife – thinking so he is being *umāpaty* (husband of *umā* (*pārvati*)).

***idap pāl koṇḍān –*** As counterpart to ‘*vala mārbinil vāḻginṟa mangai* \[[tiruppallāṇḍu – 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-2-adiyomodum/)\]’ ([tāyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who lives in the right chest of Him); (rudran is having *umā* on his left side).

***pangattāy –*** (*pangu* – a share); As said in ‘*kūr āḷum tani udamban* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 4.8.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/03/thiruvaimozhi-4-8-1-eralum-iraiyonum/)\]’ ((*kūr* – share) having divine body which the consorts individually (part by part) enjoy and consider to be their own abode, which is unique, and spiritually distinguished), emperumān gave a part of His body to rudran as if in competition with [periya pirāttiyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) . It is the divine body which He gave to Her who never separates from Him, which He also shared with rudran who thinks ‘īśvarōham’ (I am the lord). He does not see rudran’s state, or Her state, or His own state – He sees only His relationship with them ([ātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)). The form that He gave to the one having perversity of mind that thinks ‘I am the lord’, is not available to me who does not survive without seeing that form, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

Continued in part – 2

## 10. Part 1

*pon ānāy poḻil ēḻum kāval pūṇḍa    pugaḻ ānāy igaḻvāya toṇḍanēn nān en ānāy en ānāy ennal allāl   en aṟivan ēḻaiyēn – ulagam ēttum ten ānāy vada ānāy kudapāl ānāy   guṇapāla mada yānāy imaiyōrkku enṟum mun ānāy pin ānār vaṇangum sōdi   tirumūḻik kaḷattānāy mudal ānāyē.                     10*

**Пословный перевод:**

then ānāy – Oh One who is like an elephant that stands in the beautiful tirumāliruncōlai mountain; ulagam ēttum – which is worthy of praise by one and all in the world!; vada ānāy – Oh One who is like an elephant that stands in the tiruvēnkatam in the north!; kuda pāl ānāy – Oh One who is like an elephant in the westerly (in sleeping pose in tiruvarangam)!; guṇapāla madam yānāy – Oh One who is like a proud elephant (in tirukkaṇṇapuram)!; imaiyōrkku mun ānāy – Oh One who stands in front of nityasūris (for them to see and enjoy You); enṟum – at all times!; tirumūḻik kaḷattānāy – Oh One living in tirumūḻikkaḷam; pin ānār vaṇangum sōdi – like a luminous entity who could be surrendered to by those living after your incarnations!; mudal ānāy – Oh the cause of the world!; pon ānāy – Oh One who is like the gold!; poḻil ēḻum kāval pūṇḍa – Oh One who is having the fame due to giving divine protection to all the seven worlds!; igaḻvu āya toṇḍanēn ēḻaiyēn nān – I who am a devotee who is lowliness/scorn personified and who am of unsteady mind (capalan); en ānāy en ānāy ennal allāl – Other than saying ‘Oh my elephant! Oh my elephant!’ (calling emperumān so); en aṟivan – what else do I know to say? (ākincanyam)

**Перевод:**

**pon ānāy – Oh emperumān You who are my destiny!  As said in ‘rukmābham svapnadee gamyam \[manu smruti – 12.122\]’ (Should only meditate upon the supreme being who is … and like gold …), and ‘avyayō nidi: \[mahābhāratam – sahasranāmam – 301\]’ ((emperumān) is the ever lasting (fixed) deposit of wealth), and as ‘vaippām \[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.7.2\]’ (emperumān is the wealth (that is the goal)), in smruti, itihāsa, and purāṇas, by the word ‘nidi’ it talks about destiny/end.  Wealth is – one that helps during crisis, one which if known to exist would bring back the life (of its owner),  and one which is enjoyable by everyone.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*By this pāsuram, āḻvār says: He who goes to the place of His devotees, and does so without seeing whether they are qualified or not qualified, has absolutely not shown up for me – āḻvār gets a doubt that “this could be due to my saying earlier ‘mandirattai mandirattāl maṟavādenṟum vāḻudiyēl vāḻalām \[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 4\]’ may have been construed by Him as a means I used (other than Him as the means), and so He must have avoided coming to me till I complete the process of such means”; āḻvār says – in that pāsuram “I said that one could have that tirumantram as companion, which shows us the relationship between us and emperumān, and be enjoying that and such people would be able to enjoy so till there is ātmā (eternally), and I did not say that tirumantram could be used as a means. Only if I think that there is something other than You as prāpyam (destiny) would I think that there is something other than You as prāpakam (means), isn’t it?” Saying ‘mandirattāl’ – could be construed as using it as a means, and saying ‘maṟavādu vāḻudiyēl’ – could be construed as saying that one should perform that means without fail, and saying ‘vāḻalām’ – could be construed as that if one performs such means then there is a benefit/end/destiny for that. This pāsuram is this āḻvār’s ‘nōṟṟa nonbu \[tiruvāimoḻi – 5.7\]’. Like how that was for nammāḻvār, where he said ‘nōṟṟa nōnbilēn nūṇṇaṟivilēn \[tiruvāimoḻi – 5.7.1\]’ (I do not have karma yōgam performed to attain the result/destiny; I do not have subtle knowledge related to jñāna yōgam which is focussed on knowledge about self and god), saying about his ākincanyam (utter destitution), and ‘āgilum unnai vittu onṟṟum āṟṟā kiṟkinṟilēn \[tiruvāimoḻi – 5.7.1\]’ (Not having bhakti yōgam which is impossible without these two, i.e. karma yōgam and jñāna yōgam, but after meditating upon your qualities, I am unable to survive even for a moment without You who are the saviour), saying there is no dearth of interest (toward Him) – this is how nammāḻvār divined. In the same way, in the 4th pāsuram tirumangai āḻvār sang ‘mandirattai mandirattāl’  thus divining that there is no dearth of interest (toward Him), and in this 10th pāsuram he is divining about his utter destitution.*

(Why talk about destiny/end when in the context of means?) – saying this now is to imply – only if I knew that there is some other destiny than You, would l be knowing about some means other than You.

ōr this is also said to show – one would look for a means only when they need to get to the destiny, isn’t it? (since I have already got You I would not be looking for some means). [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) too said ‘*vaippām marundām* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.7.2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/09/thiruvaimozhi-1-7-2-vaippam-maruntham/)\]’ ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is the wealth (that is the goal) and the medicine (that removes the obstacles in attaining the goal) ) (showing that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) Himself is the means as well as the destiny);

ōr, since [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is saying ‘***poḻil ēḻum kāval pūṇḍa****’,* thus saying that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) Himself is the means, and since in ‘*ananyasādyē* \[bharata muni: – viśvaksēna samhitai\]’ (When someone else cannot get you what you need, then with full faith praying to the One who is fully qualified (for getting you that), saying ‘Only You shall be the means please’ is saraṇāgati), and so since He is the suitable means for getting the destiny, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is first saying ‘*pon ānāy’*, thus saying that He is the destiny.

***ānāy –*** While I am loitering being interested in worldly matters, based on Your grandeur You Yourself took me up, creating interest in me toward You, and became my destiny. This is all You, isn’t it? (So is there anyway I would look for some other means?)

***poḻil ēḻum kāval pūṇḍa pugaḻ ānāy  –*** by this, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) says ‘You are also the means’. Oh the protector of *leelā vibhūti*!

***poḻil ēḻum –*** talking about all the seven worlds. By this – it implies the worlds below as well.  It implies all the oval shaped worlds, that is said in ‘*eedṟuśānām* \[śrī viṣṇu purāṇam – 2.7.27\]’ (There are thousands, ten thousands, crore of crores of oval shaped worlds (in this environment of prakruti)). ***poḻil –*** world.

***kāval pūṇḍa –*** You are unlike the kings of the world who while having taken the crown would use others to protect his people, but is like one who with a weapon in his hand looks out for the safety of his town, You are protecting all the worlds with great attention.

***pūṇḍa –*** You have taken this protection as a penance; You protect by all means even in the presence of any hindrance/danger in protecting. That is, even if one of very close relationship like *mahārājar* (sugreevan) say ‘*vadyatām* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudda kāṇḍam – 17.29\]’ (this vibheeśaṇan shall be killed along with his ministers in most harsh way), Your stauncness of protecting is like your reply ‘*na tyajēyam* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudda kāṇḍam – 18.3\]’ (There is no way I would reject him (vibheeśaṇan) who has come in the form of a friend).  As saying in ‘*abhayam sarva bhūtēbhya:* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudda kāṇḍam – 18.33\]’ (I give protection from all things (to those who surrendered to me), (this is my vow)), You would protect me without even leaving that responsibility to me.

By this – You are the one who protected me, removing my ignorance, scent of prior actions, and their taste.

ōr, ***pūṇḍa –*** He is in the form of sweetness itself that is the destiny;  without any other option He is being the means also which He takes it upon Himself;  even though milk may be the medicine to remove the bile (*pitta*), one would not consider it as a decoction (*kaśāyam),* but only as a medicine; so – You Yourself are anxious to be the means, says [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/).

***pugaḻ ānāy –*** Oh You having the splendour due to protecting so! It is said too as ‘*yaśasaśca ēka bhājanam* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – kiśkindā kāṇḍam – 15.19,20\]’ (He is being the holder of fame (due to protecting everyone)), and as ‘*ulappil keertti ammānē* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 6.10.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-10-1-ulagam-unda/)\]’ (lord with endless glories).  As said in ‘*yāni nāmāni gauṇāni*  \[mahā bhāratam – anusā – 153.13\]’ (I shall tell you the divine names of viṣṇu, which are based on His qualities. These were divined by noble ones. I shall say these for the goodness of the world (stating so, bheeśmar says to yudiśtra the thousand divine names of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/))), your divine names are to express the qualities and acts of Yours that are due to Your vow of protecting.

***igaḻvāya toṇḍanēn nān –*** By this it talks about the true nature of ones (*adikāri* svarūpam) who attain the means (*prāpakam*) and destiny (*prāpyam*). Like how for You the whole form is greatness, for me the whole form is my lowliness. For us the qualification is not having any quality etc that qualify us. Does it mean one should keep doing mistakes? No, one does not have to do any bad deeds; it is already there aplenty in us.  All one has to do is humbly convey that to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

In ‘*aham asmyaparādānāmālaya:* \[ahirbudnya samhitā 87.30 and 87.31\]’ (I am the abode of all faults..), and ‘*rāvāṇō nāma durvrutta:* \[śri rāmāyaṇam – yudda kāṇḍam – 17.12\]’ ((I am the brother of) the one who is full of evil qualities named rāvaṇan), and ‘*amaryāda: kśudra:* \[[stōtra ratnam 62](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/03/sthothra-rathnam-62/)\]’ ((I am) one who crossed the limits of vēdas, having great interest in lowly matters…), it is humbly conveying to Him of one’s own faults only. So what we have to do is convey to Him about our faults, and about not having any qualifications.

***toṇḍanēn nān –*** I who am deeply interested toward You. By this, it talks about interest in what is going to be attained (*prāpya ruci*).  Like how for praying Him to be the means, one has to convey to Him that we do not have any qualifications and convey our own faults, to pray that He is the one to be attained, one has to have interest toward Him, isn’t it?  It is about not having any qualifications and not tolerating separation from Him that convey our qualification for involving in *dvaya mantram*.

ōr,

***igaḻvāya toṇḍanēn nān –*** Like how people would scorn a servant who carries water pot if she wishes to marry the emperor, and like how everyone would scorn a beggar who begs for a handful of food if he wishes to be crowned, it is same as me who am a *nitya samsāri* (forever involved in worldly affairs)*,* wishing to attain You.

***en ānāy en ānāy ennal allāl***  **–** (*ānai* – elephant) ***–*** By this – it talks about how the time is spent by him, he who considers [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) only as the means and destiny.  I reminisced your qualities (like in ‘*mantirattai*) for spending the time only, and not by considering such interest of me as a means (to attain You).  He is saying ‘en ānāy’ (~You are like an elephant for me), based on the qualities of elephants.

Elephant is such that (1) one is not satisfied by seeing it just one day – it is such that one would wish to see it always; (2) having huge pride; (3) is such that it could lift someone nearby and place on its neck; (4) it would stoop down if one wishes to climb on it by himself;  (5) if it pushes down some unwanted one from its back it is not possible to find its reason;  (6) would itself give the rope for tying it down;

[emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) also carries these qualities; (1) He having the beautiful form that is wished to be seen always;  (2) He having great pride as there is no one equal to or above Him; (3) like how if a king wishes to make a female servant as his main queen then there would not be anyone to stop it, if [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) wishes to grant Himself to a *nitya samsāri* (one who is forever involved in worldly affairs) based only on his surrendering to Him, then there is no one to stop Him – He is having such independence; (4) like how the elephant would stoop down if someone wishes to climb on it on his own, [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) having the character of simplicity of giving Him even to those who wish to attain Him by using some other means; (5) having the character of not having anyone who could stop Him from finishing off the enemies of His devotees even if they are related to Him; (6) creates interest towards Him by He Himself giving the *bhakti* (devotion) that could be used for enjoying Him – these are the characteristics that are matters for [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)‘s time pass. (How is rope related to *bhakti?*) – In ‘*ettinōdu iraṇḍenum kayiṟu* \[tiruccanda viruttam – 83\]’, bhakti is referred as a rope. It is about tying [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) in the pillar/post that is mind, using bhakti that is rope, so that He would not be able to go away.

By repeating ‘*en ānāy’* (and by comparing the aforementioned respective qualities), [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) implies that these qualities are always in his mind, and are the matter for his time pass. It is not possible to remove this from his mind.  [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) cannot indulge in material pleasures – since he became afraid of those and so joined [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) cannot indulge in other means – since he has surrendered to the means that is [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who does not tolerate other means. So [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) can only be involved in the matters of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) by talking about his many qualities.

***ennal allāl –*** In this way I keep spending my time thinking about Your nature; other than that,

***en aṟivan –*** would I know any other means mentioned by the śāstram? Would I know the qualities like ‘do not disrespect the noble people’ etc. (qualities such as ones listed in [śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/) chapters 7 through 11)? Would I know *karma yōgam?* Would I know *jñāna yōgam*? Would I know *bhakti yōgam* that can be attained through these two *yōgams*? What do I know? Like the dawn that came upon someone sleeping, when I was roaming as one interested in worldly pleasures, I saw, as you showed, the greatness of You. This is all I have got – where did I see other means – is what he is saying.

[emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) asks “If you are staying put thinking that I am your means and destiny, and if you are in the state that you do not have anything good as a qualification, then do you not have to just be as I see fit? Is it right of you to call saying ‘Oh You are not showing your face to me!’ “

***eḻaiyēn –*** What can I do if my love for seeing You is unbounded? During those times when I was interested in worldly affairs only, I did not have any control or limits; then how would I be able to control or limit in the case of wishing to enjoy You?  Without having ability to think that this is good and that is not good because of my my excessive interest, I had immersed in worldly matters; this is all I had done isn’t it? (now also I am showing interest towards You without any control or limit). If you are going to consider this my love for You as my using it as a means and You try to reject me,  then you would have to reject the *nityasūris* first before rejecting me.

As [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) asks, ‘Did you learn through śāśtrams or through an ācāryan that I have got qualities similar to that of elephant?, [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) replies, ‘I found and learned from the divine [abodes](https://koyil.org/index.php/dhivya-dhesams/) where You are happily present (*ugandu aruḷina nilangaḷ*)…

*Continued in part 2*

==========

Translation by raghuram śrīnivāsa dāsan
