## 26. 26

*ninnayandāḷ enṟiṟaiyē iyambik kāṇē 26*

**Пословный перевод:**

tēn maruvu – Having honey flooding fully; poḻil idattu – in the garden; malarnda pōdai – with flowers blossoming; vāy maduttu – drinking; tēnadanai – that honey; un pedaiyum neeyum – your female and you; pūmaruvi – well set in the flower; inidamarndu – and be in union with her; aṟukāla siṟu vaṇḍē – Oh the bee having six legs; poṟiyin ārnda – having lots of dots in the body; unnai toḻudēn – I prostrate and beg you; maruvi mēytta – herded with interest; A niṟai – the groups of cows; amarar kōmān – and who is the head of nityasūris; aṇi aḻundūr ninṟānukku – and who is standing in the beautiful place of tiruvaḻundūr; inṟē nee senṟu – you go now itself and; anjādē – without being afraid; maruvi ninṟu – stand there strong; iyambik kāṇē – try to tell Him; iṟaiyē – a little bit, that; ōr mādu – one female; nin nayandāḷ enṟu – is being interested in You

**Перевод:**

**tēmaruvu poḻilidattu – In the place that is having gardens that are always with honey. By the word maruvu, it says that honey is always present, and it is being plentiful; by poḻil it says that it is a garden that removes tiredness. These three qualities are present in paramapadam also which is the place of enjoyment. Only if you placed us in such a place of happiness, would the place of your enjoyment be enjoyable.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since her longing for Him is the result of His long term efforts, He became happy about that and stayed away from her; since the mother stayed away with appreciation for daughter’s state of not listening to her good words, her friend tried to determine what could calm her down, and asked the story of what happened, since it was the story of what happened with Him she could sustain herself and tell the story in the previous two pāsurams (21 & 22); Then her friend inquired, “As He is under your control, could you not have asked Him when you got a chance, telling Him not to go?”, and she replied “I tried, but since garudāḻvān came and called Him and He flew away from here, my asking Him to stop had not helped” (pāsuram 23). In pāsuram 24, “Could you not stop garudāḻvān who came and took Him away?”, “Was it just one or two to stop? I could not because all of His servants (nityasūris) came and surrounded Him”. In the previous pāsuram (25), she summarized her state that she explained in these previous pāsurams. That is, “He came like one who was going to give everything of Him, showed straight what was there with Him, and stole everything from me and left; as He left, in order for me to sustain my life, He told the name of His place. In this pāsuram (26), since there was no one to ask (more) questions, there was no answer she needed to give, and so her longing reared its head, and she thought, “He is not coming because He doesn’t know about my longing; if that is informed to Him, then He would come somehow”, and so she is sending some bees as messengers. Instead of sending some human being who is sentient, why send bees that are non-sentient? Seeing her state, all her relatives were already in bewildered state even before her. Since it was not known that it gave any fruitful result when Humans, sentients, had gone as messengers, but in rāmāvatāram when hanūman who is not a human had gone as a messenger, it had given the results, she is sending bees in this pāsuram, and crane in the next pāsuram, as messengers. When kaṇṇan who is of utmost wisdom had gone as a messenger His help only created a war. But instead of sending a monkey as messenger like in rāmāvatāram, why send a bee? There, śrī rāma who is a male had to send a messenger to a female, so He had to choose hanūman who was able and detached towards worldly affairs. Here, since it is a longing female sending to emperumān a male, she had to send only those who experience union and suffer due to similar longing. Since they would understand the suffering due to separation, and since she has seen these bees earlier that were in union and happy, she is sending bee as a messenger. But would the bees who are staying together happily agree to go as messengers? She is asking them to go because of understanding her experience she had when together with Him. That is, when she and Her man were together, if some people who suffer due to separation came to her, then she would take care of their problem and only then be together with her lover, as said in “apyaham jeevitam jahyām tvām vā salakṣmaṇām \| na tu pratijñām samśṟutya brāhmaṇēBhyō viṣēśata: \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – āraṇya Khāṇḍam – 10-19\]” (Hey seetā! I might lose my life, and lose you along with lakṣmaṇa, but I will not ignore the promise I made (that too especially ones made to brāhmaṇas)); based on this experience, she is in the state that seeing her suffering, the bees would go as messenger. She is first praising the bees, to request them to take care of her problem, by their gaining mercy towards her. In terms of inner meaning, bees represent ācāryas who get us to reach emperumān. ācaryas are to be praised, as said in – “pratyakṣē gurava: stutyā: parōkṣē mitrabānḍhavā: \| kāryāntē dāsa-Brutāśca na kadācana putrakā: \|\| (One should praise ācaryas right in front of them; praise friends and relatives in their absence; praise servants after they finish their work; should never praise the children). kriṣṇa as a messenger tēmaruvu poḻilidattu malarnda pōdai tēnadanai vāy maduttu un pedaiyum neeyum pūmaruvi inidamarndu poṟiyilārnda aṟukāla siṟu vaṇḍē toḻudēn unnai āmaruvi niṟai mēytta amarar kōmān aṇiyaḻundūr ninṟānukku inṟē senṟu neemaruvi anjādē ninṟōr mādu*

**malarnda pōdait tēnadanai vāy maduttu –** Drinking honey from the flowers as they blossomed. Since it is the honey from the flowers that blossomed just then, it would be fresh and very tasty. If time has passed since a flower blossomed, then its honey would be past time, less, and old. If honey is extracted when the flower is still a bud, then there would be less honey, and since it is taken forcefully, that honey would taste less. So, it is saying here that the honey is from just blossomed flower.

Would the flowers which blossom (only) when wind blows, would blossom when these bees go there? We can consider it as – since those flowers are at the stage of blossoming even if touched, they blossom due to the wind from the wings of bees, and also the air of their breath.

This same aspect is divined by āḻvār in “*pūviriya madu nugarum* \[periya tirumoḻi – 3.6.1\]”. Unlike Him who left me without understanding my freshness and sweetness, you got to understand the freshness and sweetness of flowers, she says. Only if you join me with Him while I am fresh and sweet, would you be known as having complete ablity to enjoy fresh honey, she says.

**vāy maduttu –** Like husband and wife eating from the same vessel, taking the honey in mouth and feeding it to each other. Honey is there for enjoying each other in this way. That is, “Don’t you have to make me enjoy the sweetness by joining me with Him, like how śrī rāma enjoyed together with seetāp pirātti as said in “idam mēḍhyam idam svādu \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – ayōDhyā Kāṇḍam – 96-2\]” (This is pure, This is sweet.. – saying these, śrī rāma was present together with seetāp pirātti in tiruccittirak kūdam).

**un pedaiyum neeyum –** The female bee that is under your control, and you who are under her control; the female bee behaves as per your thoughts, and you who behave as per her thoughts; Oh it is good that you both are able to interact knowing each others’ mind, unlike Him who does not behave with understanding of my wishes.

**un pedaiyum neeyum –** You who are together as male and female, don’t you have to take care of work for me who am separated from my lover?. It is my fortune that you are together and so not need someone to get you together first – she says. śrī rāma had to unite mahārājar (sugreevan) with his wife and kingdom first and then only had to search for His wife. Unlike that, you are already together, and that is my fortune, she says to the bees.

**pūmaruvi –** Oh what a dare this is, like someone hugging the fire!- she says. As said in “*menmalarp paḷḷi vem paḷḷiyālō* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 9.9.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/09/thiruvaimozhi-9-9-4-paviyen-manaththe/)\]”, bed with flowers in it (soft like flowers) is like a bed on fire for her. Since she is separated from her lover, she thinks only those who knows *agni-stamBha-vidyā* would be able to lie down in such a bed. *agni-stamBha-vidyā* is union of man and woman. As I am lying on floor since I cannot tolerate the bed, only if you do my work would it be enjoyable for you to be together in bed; Oh what a great wonder it is that unlike for me, you both as male and female got the garden which removes tiredness as a place of enjoyment, honey as enjoyable, and flower as bed! – she says.

**inidamarndu –** Sitting nicely. Nicely being together as male and female. Since her union with Him ended in separation, that union become one that gave sorrow. She herself said *“enṟālum idu namakkōr pulavi tānē* \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 23](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/10/thirunedunthandakam-23/)\]”. Idea is – only if you removed my sorrow would your union be enjoyable.

**poṟiyilārnda –** By *poṟi* it refers to dots. Having rich number of dots in its body. This phrase is adjective for the bee. It talks about the brightness to its body due to union.

The male bee is in beginning stage of youth like said in “*yuvā kumāra:* \[ruk – ash – 2.2.25\]”, (viṣṇu is in the age of youth, and having a bit of previous stage of adolescence).

The female bee is in the beginning stage of youth as said in “*yuvatiśca kumāriṇee*” (pirātti is in upper stage of adolescence, and beginning of youth).

As said in “*Bhartā nāma param nāryā: Bhūśaṇam BhUśaṇAdapi \| ēśāhi rahitā tēna Bhūśaṇārhā na śōBhatē \|\|* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – sundara Khāṇḍam – 16.26\]” (Husband is like an ornament above the ornaments worn, and so He is the reason for her brightness. Since she is separated from such a husband, seetāp pirātti who could wear ornaments, was not being beautiful), for me who has become lean due to not eating because of separation, if you create beauty for me, only then your beauty would be meaningful, she says.

**aṟukāla siṟu vaṇḍē –** Oh small bee who has got six legs! Since it is natural for bees to have six legs, why mention this as adjective to the bee? Oh! Instead of having just two legs, or just four legs, it got to have six legs that would enable it to go to Him fast! – This was the meaning established by previous ācāryas. But since the means for a bee to travel is its wings, since she is going to say next in this pāsuram – *toḻudēn* (prostrating and praying to you), I got six legs for it to touch my head (when I prostrate / when I happily keep your six legs in my head when you return from Him) – is the establishment of meaning by śrī parāśara Bhattar.

(From *puttūr svāmy’s vivaraṇam –* bee refers to ācāryas who enjoy the honey that is auspicious qualities of Bhagavān. Like towards ācāryan, one should also serve ācārya’s divine son, and ācarya’s divine wife. Combined, it is their six divine feet. There is a phrase matching śrī parāśara Bhattar’s meaning in “*en tūdāy, num kālgaḷ en talai mēl keḻumeerō numarōdē* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 9.7.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/08/thiruvaimozhi-9-7-1-enganal-agangazhivay/)\]”)

**siṟu vaṇḍē –** You who is already small unlike tiruvadi (hanūman) who had to make himself small when entering lankā when doing the messenger work.

**vaṇḍē –** You do not have to take additional efforts like tiruvadi who had to figure out where to sit and how to talk to seetāp pirātti; you could be in the flower ring garland in His head, or in the garland in His shoulders, and talk to Him without anyone else knowing – such is the type of birth yours is!

As she called the bees, they who were experiencing their togetherness heard her requesting voice, and got up with a startle like someone threw fire, and peeked.

**toḻudēn unnai –** I prostrated, fell, on your feet. It shows that one should prostrate, fall on ācāryan’s feet, and praise him.

**unnai –** I am not falling at His feet; even at this state of His having left me, I am falling at your feet, you who are going to join the severed connection between Him and me. I am separated from Him and suffering even while He exists, and I exist; you who are going to join us are the one who are to be praised, and prostrated. This is the way of her *“atra-paratra-cāpi* \[[ālavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/)’s [stōtra ratnam – 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/08/thiruvaimozhi-9-7-1-enganal-agangazhivay/)\]” ([śrīman nātamuni](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/22/nathamunigal/)’s divine feet is my destiny in this world and the other world).

As the bee says, “Let your prostrating me be; what is it to be accomplished by me. You shall tell me that”, and so she replies –

**amarar kōmān –** Please go and announce the following to the leader of nityasūris as said in “*ayarvarum amarargaḷ atipati* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 1.1.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/)\]”.

Is it possible for us to go near Him?

**A-maruvi nirai mēytta** – He is one, though so superior, who had been set together with the cows and protected them. Like how He is the head of nityasūris who could not exist if separated from Him, His existence depended on the cows He protected. Since nityasūris cannot live without Him, He is being approachable to them. Since He cannot sustain Himself if separated from cows, He is well set among them. **maruvi** – well set/involved.

Oh! But that was the earlier time of kriṣṇāvatāram..

**aṇiyaḻundūr ninṟānukku –** Like how He protected the cows during the time of incarnation as kriṣṇa, to protect those who are present after that time and who are with no wisdom similar to cows, He stood in tiruvaḻundūr. Please inform Him about me. When He left, He said “*punal-arangam-Ur* \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 24](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/10/thirunedunthandakam-23/)\]”, and so must have gone to tiruvarangam; why send messenger to tiruvaḻundūr? Sending so is meaningless – it is similar to saying “*karṇē spṟuśtē gatim cālayati* \[saying\]” (He moves his hip if one touches his ear)? “Since He is also a sentient, suffering would be there for Him too due to separating from me; He left me for some work; He would not leave me altogether; He might have stayed in tiruvaḻundūr on the way. Please go and request to Him there” – she says.

ōr, “Since tiruvarangam is *bhōga-maṇḍapam* (meant for enjoyment), He would not have been able to stay there for long because He would not be able to sustain the separation from me. So, as He would have started returning to me, He might have reached up to tiruvaḻundūr. Go there”, could be the other meaning of her saying this.

**ninṟānukku –** In the first meaning, He could not go to His place, so He stood in tiruvaḻundūr like a tree; In the second meaning above – during return, He might be staying in tiruvaḻundūr thinking that He should not voluntarily go to me showing eagerness, and so He may be waiting there hoping that I send someone to Him”.

**inṟē senṟu –** You should not say “We will go tomorrow”. I will not sustain myself till tomorrow – she says. If I won’t be there, then He too won’t be there. If you think both of us should live, then you go today itself. If both He and I are destroyed, then it is like the whole world is destroyed.

**nee maruvi –** Since He was well set among the cows and protected them, He would definitely welcome you who is of animal species. So you approach Him and;

**nee maruvi –** Like how I who had seen the excess of His quality of simplicity but had hesitated thinking that He is supreme god instead of thinking as human being, as said in “*avarai nām dēvarenṟu anjinōmē* \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 21](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/10/thirunedunthandakam-21-part-1/)\]” – you please do not behave like me, but approach Him and be well set with Him.

**anjādē ninṟu –** Stand there without fearing when seeing His supremacy. Instead of being afraid thinking that He is the leader of nityasūris, consider His being well set among the cows and taking them for gracing; and so stand there sustaining yourselves.

ōr, **anjādē ninṟu –** Stand with pride and talk to Him, with the appearance of coming as messengers from the Queen. If you talked with pride, it would be clear to Him that you came from me.

What is it to be conveyed to Him? She responds –

**ōr mādu –** No need to say my name to Him; If you just said “one woman”, that would be enough. He would know automatically. If someone says “There is a deer in a forest which has got arrow-wounds and is suffering from it”, then the one who shot that arrow would be knowing the place of that deer.

ōr,

**ōr mādu –** She says – tell Him that the girl has no equals. If you say so, then He would know who it is. It is because she is not a part of those who are immersed in the material world, not of liberated souls, and not of those people who wish to leave for śrīvaikuṇṭam (mumukśus). She is present like said in “*pinnai kol, nila māmagaḷ kol, tirumagaḷ kol piṟandittāḷ* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 6.5.10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/02/thiruvaimozhi-6-5-10-pinnaikol/)\]” (unique woman like the divine consorts of emperumān).

**ninnayandāḷ** – If you (who is a bee) went to Him as a messenger, then He would know whose messenger you are. There would be no need to say anything. Even if you say any words, if you just said “*ōr mādu*”, then He would definitely know. If even that is not enough, it is enough if you just said a word “*ninnayandāḷ*”- She wished for you – is the meaning. That is, that she is hurt at the part vital to life.

Ask Him, “Did she wish for someone walking in the road? She wished for You who are the greatest of all. She wished for you who are of greatness that one could not sustain themselves if separated”.

**ninnayandāḷ –** Did she wish for you in your state of *para, vyūha, viBhava*, that she could stay without great anxiety? She wished for you who is in *arcāvatāram* (vigraham/statue form).

**enṟu iṟaiyē iyambik kāṇē –** So as you start talking these words to Him He would know, and would not even let you finish; and you will see Him rejoicing about you – she says.

**iṟaiyē iyambi –** (say only a little) – It is enough if you said “*ōr mādu”,* it would be excessive to say even “*ninnayandāḷ”.*

***kāṇē –*** You are definitely going to see all the respects He would pay to you after you just said this. Saying like “*ḍharmata: parirakṣitā:* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudḍha Kāṇḍam – 1-11\]” (We all got saved of our lives because of your deed of beneficence – śrī rāma says to hanūman when he returns after finding seetāp pirātti),

and hugging like “*ēśa sarvasva Bhūtastu pariśvanga:* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudḍha Kāṇḍam – 1-13\]” (This my embracing you which is like giving everything of me.. (said śrī rāma to hanūman)),

you are going see that He would do all these – she says.

**kāṇē –** Oh you are going to see His divine body which I could not see! – she says – This could also be the meaning.

—–

Translation by raghurām śrīnivāsa dāsan.

## 27. 27

*irunilattil inidinbam eydalāmē                                    27*

**Пословный перевод:**

sem kāla mada nārāy – Oh crane having reddish legs!; inṟē senṟu – Going today itself; tiruk kaṇṇapuram pukku – and entering tirukkaṇṇapuram; uraitti āgil – If you would tell; en sem kaṇ mālukku – one with lotus eyes, and who is in love with me; en tuṇaivarkku – and who is my companion, that is sovripperumāḷ; en kādal – about my interest in Him; idu oppadu inbam illai – there would be no other happiness like this; emakku – to me (who is suffering due to separation); taruvan – I will give; eedu – this; paingānam ellām – full area of garden; unadē āga – making it fully yours; nāḷum – for all the time that you are alive, and; kavarndu uṇṇat – for you to pick, and eat; meen – fish; paḻanam – in the water of the land; tandāl – After I give you so; un pedaiyum neeyum – your wife and you; ingē vandu – come to this place and; inidu irundu – be with happiness; iru nilattil – in (this) big land; inidu inbam eydalām – and attain utmost happiness

**Перевод:**

**sem kāla mada nārāy – Since she has seen Him praising her feet when she was with Him, when she is sending message, she is praising the feet of the messenger, and not other parts of it. Oh what a foot and how reddish it is!, she praises.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram she sent bees as messengers. Since she did not have the strength to sustain herself till the time they went and conveyed the message, and brought the reply, she is sending a crane as a messenger. As said in ‘dikśu sarvāsu mārgantē \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – sundara Khāṇḍam – 30-3\]” (seetāp pirātti who searched in all directions) like how He sent monkey warriors in four directions, she is also sending whichever messengers she saw. Like how the clan of monkeys were gratified in śrī rāmāvatāram, after āḻvārs’ incarnation, the clan of birds were gratified. sengāla mada nārāy inṟe senṟu tirukkaṇṇapuram pukken sengaṇmālukku en kādal en tuṇaivarkkuraittiyāgil iduvoppademakku inbamillai; nāḷum paingānameedellām unadēyāgap paḻanameen kavarnduṇṇat taruvan; tandāl ingē vandinidirundun pedaiyum neeyum*

It appears that she has got connections with reddish feet. Oh! this foot is like that of my lover! Like how it would be pleasant if we kept a load of black forest flower in our head, it feels like I could keep looking at your reddish feet only, and not even send you as a messenger. I said it wrongly when comparing your feet as equal to His. Whereas giving His feet is what He does, your feet have the greatness of giving His feet as well as of yourself. You have got the greatness of being easy to obtain, and making Him also easy to obtain.

**mada-nārāy –** Oh crane that does not have intellect! By **madam** it is saying about absence of intellect. Should she talk like this when indulged in praising it? Like an ironical phrase, this also leads to praising only. Ironical phrase (*nindā-stuti*) is – cursory meaning would be a ridicule and inner meaning would be praise. As said in “*na śāstram naiva ca krama:*” ((one having at most love) would not see the rules of SAstram nor the manners of world), you are having great ability of involving in love such that you behave differently during togetherness.

If one interacts with proper etiquette during the time of togetherness, it would just maintain the nature of being a male and female lover, but it would not have the greatness of having love. (ōr, considering the meaning of *madam* as completeness (*madappam* and other qualities of a woman)), I am having you with completeness in you that would help during my loss by going as a messenger.

**nārāy –** As vocative case word, it is like saying “Oh mother!”, asking for help. Like while having lost everything to robbery and then seeing the mother who would console; your appearance is like that when I am suffering due to separation from Him, she says to the crane.

As said in “*pampā teerē hanumata: sangata:* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – bāla Khāṇḍam – 1-58\]” (In the bank of river pampā, śrī rāman joined hanuman), when in the state of suffering in the hands of things that reminded śrī rāma of pirātti, tiruvadi came to Him – for śrī rāma this was like meeting the mother after having lost everything to robbery.

**nārāy –** Calling it in high pitch shows the excess of her sadness. Since I am the lover of emperumān, if you see the greatness of me the wife of king, you should give me payment for doing my work! – she says.

“Do you have to ask me? Won’t you just tell me what is to be done?”.

She replies –

**inṟe senṟu –** It is not a matter that you can postpone to tomorrow. If you see His nature, it could not be done next day. If you see my nature, then also it could not be done next day. As said in “*na-jeevēyam kṣaṇamapi* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudḍha Khāṇḍam – 66-10\]” ( I would not live even for a second, being separated from seetā), His nature is that He cannot live even for a second when separated from me, she says. As said in “*jalān matsyāviva-udḍhrutau* \[śrī rāmāyaṇām – ayōḍhyā Khāṇḍam – 53-31\]” (Like how a fish would live only for short time when out of water, we would also live for only a short time when without you), my nature is like separating fish from water.

**tirukkaṇṇapuram pukku –** When tirumangai āḻvār says tirukkaṇṇapuram, it does not sound like how riśis and other āḻvārs say. Interest in that place for this āḻvār is much more compared to them. Like how it is when anantāḻvān says “tiruvēnkatamudaiyān”, when Bhattar says “aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ”, when sōmāsiyāṇḍān says “emperumānārē śaraṇam”, it is so when tirumangai āḻvār says “tirukkaṇṇapuram” out of desire.

**kaṇṇapuram pukku –** As she had sent message to tiruvaḻundūr, instead of sending there again, why send to tirukkaṇṇapuram? He would not sustain Himself in tiruvaḻundūr after I had sent the bee. He and the bee would have started their journey to here in a big hurry (that the clothes in their waist, and plait in their head would loosen up), and would have reached tirukkaṇṇapuram. So go there and give the message, she says.

**en sem kaṇ mālukku – en mālukku –** Seeing the supreme lord who controls both the worlds is under her control, she is saying, “To the One who is under the control of my love”. **sem kaṇ mālukku –** To the One who showed the beauty of His eyes, and won me over, and kept me under the control of His love;

**senkaṇmālukku –** As said in “*anidras satatam rāma:* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – sundara Khāṇḍam – 36-44\]*”* (śrī rāma is sleepless always), He does not sleep due to separating from me; so His eyes would be watery and reddish – that would be an identification of Him for you – she says this also.

**mālukku –** To the one who is a big mad lover. (Tell) Him who showed His madness and elevated my madness of love. Only gaining madness is there on my part; gaining madness and making me mad is there on His part only. It is said so too in “*mālāyp piṟanda nambiyai, mālē seyyum maṇāḷanai* \[[nācciyār tirumoḻi – 14-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/05/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-14-simple/)\]” (~One who is madly in love, and who makes others be madly in love).

**en kādal –** Tell Him that my love is not like His love. “Your love is such that there won’t be any change in the brightness/make-up of the divine body when you show your love, as said in “*minnilangu tiru uruvum periya tōḷum* \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 25](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/10/thirunedunthandakam-25/)\]”; whereas, as said in “*pongār mel iḷam kongai ponnē pūppa* \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 17](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/08/thirunedunthandakam-17/)\]”, her love is such that the colour of her body would drain away” – is what you go and tell Him.

**en kādal –** As said in “*yā preetir avivēkānām viśayēśvanapāyinee* \[śrī viṣṇu purāṇam – 1-20-19\]” (Like how for those who don’t have discerning knowledge there is desire in the worldly matters, (I should always have interest in you (prayed prahlāda to emperumān)), my love is not like the people of the world who love the worldly aspects like sound, form, taste, etc. Those desires would be abandoned after gaining true knowledge. But this desire of mine would not be abandoned. My love is not like those who got liberated from the material world. That love will give only happiness. My love will give sorrow due to separation.

**en tuṇaivarkku –** To my relative who cannot be avoided. Tell Him who is always my companion. His companionship is such that, when I was not interested in Him He gave me life, then created interest, and after union with me and then separation, He made sure I am able to sustain myself (*tiruvarangam Ur*).

**en tuṇaivarkku –** Tell the One who can remove the trouble I am going through due to separation.

**uraittiyāgil – If** you would say. We can only request and not order someone who is helping to get us together. That is only the reason she is saying “if your divine heart wishes”.

**uraittiyāgil –** It can also mean – **if** you would avoid getting distracted by His beauty but do the work as messenger and tell Him about my state.

**uraitti –** She is saying, “*vācā darmamavāpnuhi* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – sundara Khāṇḍam – 39-10\]” (You gain good karmā (*puṇṇiyam*) by telling such words to śrī rāma).

For you the effort is saying just a word; what I would gain is my being – she says.

**idu oppadu enakku inbam illai –** There is nothing more joyous than this, she says. There is no pleasure for me beyond your telling Him. There is pleasure only after gaining His togetherness; is there pleasure gained just by announcing to Him? Yes since she is of the thought – there is delay only in announcing to Him; His coming and making me happy would not fail.

**nāḷum,** etc – It is not enough that you help me and leave, you should accept the little honour I give you due to my gratitude – she says.

**nāḷum,** etc. – If ācaryan helps even one time, disciple shall be grateful to ācaryan his entire life.

**paingānam eedu ellām –** She is saying that she would give to the crane all the land that she thinks is hers. That is, the area of all the garden which is near the sea shore. In tamiḻ tradition, the place where lover girl spends time is the sea shore (*neydal nilam*) when suffering due to separation from her lover. So she is saying she would give that area to the crane.

**eedu ellām –** She is giving all the land that the eyes could see. “I will give you all my wealth”, she says. With crane representing ācāryan, and she representing disciple, giving this land implies disciple shall give both the worlds to ācaryan. This is said too in “*ponnulagaḷeerō bhuvani muḻudāḷeerō* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 6.8.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/04/thiruvaimozhi-6-8-1-ponnulagaliro/)\]”.

**unadēyāga –** Will not keep it common for both you and me; I will give such that it is all for you – she says.

**paḻana meen –** In the land of water, since there would always be water, the fish would be fat and big like elephant. By this it implies that what a disciple gives to ācāryan should be of great quality.

As said in “*yadanna: puruśō Bhavati tadannās tasya dēvatā:* \[śrī rāmāyaṇām – ayōḍhyā Kaṇḍam – 103-30\]” (Whatever one eats, should be presented to the deities also (said śrī rāma when doing last rites for His father)), one could give to forefathers in *pitṟulōkam* the same food that one takes; but, for ācāryas one should search for and give what is desired by ācāryas.

**kavarndu uṇṇa taruvan –** I would give with hospitality such that it could be eaten with eagerness.

**tandāl ingē vandu –** It is not enough that you accept based on fullness of your qualities what I present to you; you should come here and eat them in front of me.

**vandu –** Unlike my lover who came once and helped me and then not showed up, you should come to the place I am in and eat.

**un pedaiyum neeyum inidu irundu –** Should not come alone. Should come with your lover.

**iru nilattil inidu inbam eydalāmē –** In this world of *samsāram* that is the place for endless sorrows, you could attain endless bliss of liberation. You could get to live with your family as said in “*nalla padattāl manai vāḻvar koṇḍa peṇḍir makkaḷē* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 8.10.11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/06/thiruvaimozhi-8-10-11-nalla-kotpattulagangal/)\]”.

Since śrivaiṣṇavas living here are with body, they could achieve at most happiness of gladly giving the things desired by others, by living in a family – this greatness is not there in śrīvaikuṇṭam.

—–

Translation by raghurām śrīnivāsa dāsan.

## 28. 28

*eppoḻudum ninaindurugi iruppan nānē 28*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḻee! – Oh friend!; senṟu – (He who) went (there and won, as the); araṇ – forts; sidari – got destroyed; tennilakai – in the beautiful lankā; avuṇan māḷā – and also rāvaṇan got destroyed; ulagam mūnṟinaiyum tirundu – and who spanned all the three worlds by His steps (during trivikrama avatār); ōr tērāl ūrnda – He who conducted using a chariot; bāratattai māḷa – for everyone to be destroyed in the mahābhārata war; man ilangu – that was having bright presence of kings; varai uruvin mā kaḷiṟṟai – that is, emperumān who is big like an elephant in form like a mountain; pūttik koṇḍu – (I will) tie (Him in); en than – my; pon ilangu mulaik kuvattil – pillars that are the bosom that are beautiful like gold; pōgāmai vallēnāy – and become able to stop and prevent from going away; pulavi eydi – and go through all the sorrows in front of Him which I went through in His absence; ennil angam ellām vandu inbam eyda – for all the parts of my body to come to me and become happy; nān – I (would); ninaindu – think about Him only; eppoḻudum – at all times; urugi iruppan – and die, so I can survive by escaping from sorrows of separation

**Перевод:**

**then ilangai araṇ sidari – In the next line of this pāsuram she refers to emperumān as elephant, “varai uruvin mā kaḷiṟṟai”; so when talking before that about the deeds of emperumān, she talks about Him using elephant as metaphor. Destroying that beautiful fort; it is the strength of fort that looks beautiful for the brave. When tiruvadi went to lankā in search of seetāp pirātti, seeing the strength of forts, and that of rāvaṇan, he thought “With the way this fort is, only my entering it, or my father vāyu entering it would be possible. Other than that, nobody else can enter it. Even that entry would have to be in the night only and not during the day time. To such a strong fort, I am trying to bring in the princes who are delicate”, and thus felt terrible in his mind. If we look at the strength of the head of this place, he is able to win many in other places all by himself.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**sidari –** Like an elephant destroying a sand castle without any effort. If it is a stone or tree then the word *idari* (tripped) could be used. viBheeśaṇāḻvan, just only with the knowledge of hearing about valour of perumāḷ, had told “*yadā saikatamamBhasi* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudḍha Kāṇḍam – 16-24\], that is, like how a river destroys a sand castle, you and your army are going to be destroyed in the hands of śrī rāma.

**avuṇan māḷac ceṟṟu –** Going there such that rāvaṇan dies. rāvaṇan was dead just on the walk of śrī rāma. Then it was just cutting his dead body, and breaking his sword. That is, Like how when a mad elephant walks, it would destroy everything in its path with its foot and trunk, seeing how He destroyed karan, dūśaṇan, etc., destroying vāli who had tied rāvaṇan in his tail, going and staying in the sea shore and building a bridge across the ocean that was protecting lankā, reaching lankā and surrounding it, rāvaṇan was dead much earlier.

āren’t these His acts for his lover? He did not do this based on love. He did these to get the women who are powerless, so these are just a show only, she says.

**ulagam mūnṟinaiyum tirindu –** Spanning the seven worlds above and seven worlds below; It used the word *tirindu* to imply that like how a mad elephant would roam as per its wish, He had kept His divine foot on anyone and everyone.

Not only He showed up for His lover seetāp pirātti, He kept His divine feet on everyone including those who ignore Him due to subservience to others, or feeling independent; isn’t He of such nature? (asks her friend).

“He is an expert, you see; He would rather keep His divine feet on those who ignore Him, but not show up for those who wish to be near His divine feet as I said about me in “*emperumān tiruvadik keeḻ aṇaiya* \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 22](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/10/thirunedunthandakam-22/)\]”, she replies.

**ōr tērāl mannilangu bāratattai māḷa ūrnda – oru tērāl –** Enemies had said that kriṣṇan shall not use weapon, but they did not say that He should not do such acts as going as messenger, or driving the chariot; So, as a charioteer He destroyed using a chariot. There is no restriction for Him that using certain things He accomplish only certain things. It is said too as “*kollā mākkōl kolai seydu* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 3.2.3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thiruvaimozhi-3-2-3-kolla-makkol/)\]” (~using the stick of chariot driving, He destroyed all the armies).

**mannilangu bāratattai –** The bhārata war that was led by the roaming soldiers like Bheeśma, drōṇa who were experts in chariots, and with expertise as *ati-rata, mahā-rata.*

***māḷavūrnda –*** emperumān directing the chariot such that it is not possible to even see the dead bodies. As said in “*ādiya mā-nedun tēr padai neereḻac ceṟṟa pirān* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 6.8.3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/05/thiruvaimozhi-6-8-3-odi-vandhu/)\]”, He drove the chariot that the enemies got ground under the wheel and got powdered.

He did all these for draupadi who did not consider anyone else as Her protector, isn’t it? – asks her friend.

He did all these for the world to fall for Him by making them think, “Oh He did all these acts that are not a match for His greatness, so that a woman could tie her hair!”. He is the one who would make the hair be spread out for those like me who cannot survive without Him – she replies.

**varai uruvin mā-kaḷiṟṟāi –** Him who is like an elephant having strength and pride like a mountain. Her friend’s thought is – He who is staunch in destroying the enemies of His devotees, and having pride due to happiness of accomplishing the deeds for His devotees.

**tōḻee –** He who is described by you as having all these greatness, is going to suffer in my hands, which you are going to see for sure, she says.

ōr,

as said in “*ēkam du:Kham suKham ca nau* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – kiśkintā Kāṇḍam – 5-17\]” (Our happiness and sorrows are together (said śrī rāma to sugreeva)), Like rāma and sugreeva, you are together with me in happiness and sorrow, if you don’t stop me dear friend, then you will see what He undergoes in my hand – could be the meaning.

**tōḻee –** addressing her so, thereby requesting her not to stop what she is going to do.

**enṟan pon ilangu mulaik kuvattil pūttik koṇḍu –** When there is *brahmāstram* (ultimate weapon) with me, is there anyone who cannot be won by it? She says.

**enṟan ponnilangu mulai –** Is my bosom grown only as much as it is grown for the women of this country? Is my bosom grown only as much as his bosom \[Here bosom represents the counter-part for males – moustace and beard\]?

**ponnilangu mulai –** Is my womanliness only as much His manliness? My womanliness is such that the colour of my body would pale if He separates. His manliness is that, as said in “*minnilangu tiru uruvum* \[[tirunedentāṇḍakam – 25](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/10/thirunedunthandakam-25/)\]”, even when separated, His body stays bright like before. Would He become equal to me, when the coloiur of His body stays the same whether together or separated?

**mulaikkuvattil pūttik koṇḍu –** enjoining Him in the pillar that is the bosom; enjoining Him like that, is – expressing to Him her sorrow of separation.

**pōgāmal-pūttik koṇḍu –** As He tries to leave because of not getting attention from me, I shall fully express my sorrow of separation.

**mulaik kuvattil pūttik koṇḍu –** Enjoining Him in the mountain that is the bosom. He is “*varai-uruvin mā-kaḷiṟu”*(A big elephant like a mountain), so it has to be tied together with a mountain.

**vallēnāy –** This is the only If, she says. If I have the ability to stop Him from going away. If i get this ability, then I would do all these, she says. That is – If my mind would help me do this difficult task, is the meaning. If He comes and shows Himself to me, then my heart will fall for Him. If he does not do that; if you, my friend, would not stop me what I do, and be on my side; she is seeking her friend’s help because her friend is closer to her than her heart is.

**pulavi eydi –** All the sorrows I went through, I am going to go through in front of Him; ōr, all the sorrows I went through, I will make Him go through the same in front of my eyes.

**ennil angam ellām vandu inbam eyda –** At first glance, this might give the meaning of getting happiness. But the topic here is showing anger due to love; so, it means – since there is no happiness for me than dying, “I shall die in front of Him” is the meaning for this phrase. That is, “Without giving room for another separation, I shall die in front of Him”, is the thought.

**ennil angam ellām –** All parts of my body which He liked and also praised when together, as, “Oh! How beautiful these eyes are! How beautiful this smile is! How beautiful this bosom is!”.

**inbam eyda –** die and thus get ultimate joy (because there would not be repeated separations from Him). Like how a man having a big family would drown together with His family when not able to tolerate His family’s and His hunger duringfamine, I will die along with all parts of my body, and be joyful, she says.

**vandu –**(all parts of my body would come to me).Seeing the sweetness of emperumān, various parts of her body would behave like sentient and would wish to enjoy Him, and so compete independently. Like how Bharatāḻvān and kaikēyi had had different opinions, She the mother cow, and the parts of her body which are the calves, would be eager to enjoy Him independently. I will retrieve all of these from Him, and join them in me which is their place, and we (self and the parts of the body) will die together – she says.

**inbam eyda –** She is saying ‘for me to attain happiness’, to imply, ‘I am going to die’. Because her suffering from separation is such, that she thinks that only dying would be paramapadam.

**eppoḻudum –** In all states. That is – As her friend said, “You are saying these because He has not come in person. If He does, then as said in “mye-vaṇṇam \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 21](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/10/thirunedunthandakam-21-part-1/)\]”, you would become involved with Him”, she is replying, “I would die even in that state”.

**eppoḻudum –** He is the owner, I am His property – even if He shows me this relationship of ours, I am still going to die, she says. This is due to her stauncness based on – “I shall not join Him and give room for another separation”.

**ninaindurugi iruppan –** I would be destroyed the moment I think of Him. I will not give room for Him to come to me and say “I would not leave you; I would not survive if I leave”, and such false words like He did before; instead I would die just as I think of Him, she says, and this is due to her delicateness.

Anger of love came to [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in “*minnidai madavār* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 6.2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/10/thiruvaimozhi-6-1-vaigal/)\]; then when emperumān came to her, she together with her friend, stopped Him by putting up the protection wall that is her anger of love (like how tāyār closes the door in tiruvarangam periya kōyil during praṇaya kalaha utsavam). Then emperumān had to show to her step by step His inability to go anywhere else, remove her anger of love, and then only get her. Instead, without giving room for that, I would die the moment I think of Him, says [parakāla nāyaki](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/).

**nānē** – But this kind of delicateness is not seen anywhere else (is it possible)? – Being this delicate is my quality only, she says. “Those like [parānkuśa nāyaki](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) would be having strength to fully execute their anger of love if they decided -‘Let me not get emperumān to come near me if He showed up’. Unlike that, my delicateness is such that I would die the moment I think of Him”, she says.

—–

Translation by raghurām śrīnivāsa dāsan.

## 29. 29

*nediyānai adināyēn ninaindittēnē 29*

**Пословный перевод:**

araiyan tannai – Him who is a leader; āyar kulam magaḷukku – for nappinnai pirātti who incarnated as the best woman for the clan of covherds; anṟu – once upon a time; alai kadalai kadaindu – Him who churned the milky ocean having waves splashing; adaitta ammān tannai – Him, the lord who constructed bridge (in salty ocean); kunṟada vali – having blemiśless strength; arakkar kōnai māḷa – that is, rāvaṇan to die; kodum silai vāy – Him who in the grave bow; saram turandu – set the arrows and shot them; kulam kaḷaindu venṟānai – and destroyed the clan of asuras and won; tōḷinānai – Him who is having shoulders; kunṟu edutta – that lifted the gōvardana mountain as an umbrella; nāḷum ninṟānai – Him who is living forever; viri tirai neer viṇṇagaram maruvi – well set in tiruviṇṇagar that is full of water bodies having waves; kidanda mālai – Him who is in the dear one being in reclined position; taṇ kudandai – in the cool place of tirukkudandai; nediyānai – Him, the perumāḷ who is the most eminent that others; nāy adiyēn – I who am a lowly one like a dog; ninaindittēn – thought about – Him

**Перевод:**

**anṟu etc. – He is talked about as showing up for His lovers. First, āḻvār divines about His helping nappinnaip pirātti.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**anṟu –** At that time also a danger happened like today. Considering the beauty of nappinnaip pirātti, deciding that her groom should be the one who is having the ability to protect her, and hence it was established that winning the bulls as the condition for giving her daughter in marriage; so before kriṣṇan came for that, the shivering of nappinnai is the shivering with the thought that she might become related to someone else.

**āyar kula magaḷukku** – For nappinnai who was born in noble clan; thinking that it would not be possible to get nappinnai unless born in an equally noble clan, is the reason why kriṣṇa had come and took birth as nandagōpar’s son.

**araiyan tannai** – Equivalent to destroying enemies and getting back a kingdom and ruling it, is His winning the bulls that were in His way to get nappinnai, and getting the delightful nappinnai. Like how He removed the hurdles and helped Her get Him, should He not remove the hurdles for me and come to me – [parakāla nāyaki](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) asks.

**alai kadal etc**. – He is divining about the way perumāḷ helped śrīdēvi and seetāp pirāttis. To get periya pirātti He churned the ocean. To solve the loneliness of seetāp pirātti, He bridged across the ocean. Giving nectar to dēvas, giving their places back in rāmāvatāram are all side effects only. āḷavandār knowing well this inner thought of emperumān, had divined “yadarṭhamamBhoḍhiramanṭhyabhanḍhi \[[stōtra ratnam – 37](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-37/)\]” (Only to get Her did emperumān churn the ocean; only to get Her did He construct bridge across the ocean).

**ammān tannai** – If You do not come here and show yourself to me who is longing for You, not only would You lose your name as a big lover of ones who love you, you would also lose the supremacy attributed to you for giving, as a side-effect, the nectar to and removing the poverty of dēvas who became poor due to the curse of sage durvāsa and came to you asking for help, and giving back their places when brahmā et al lost their places of living due to rāvaṇan – she says.

**kunṟāda vali** – If it is just the strength of shoulders, then that could diminish over time; since this is strength based on boons for penances, it does not diminish over time (the for rākṣasas).

**arakkar kōnai māḷa –** For the death of rāvaṇan the head of rākShasas whose living, using the boons they obtained, was troubling for others.

**kodunjilaivāyc caram turandu** – Shooting arrows from His śrī-SArngam bow such that the enemies lose upon just seeing it.

**kodunjilai** – using the arrows from bow that is having greatness as said in “*deepta pāvaka sankāśai:* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudḍha Kāṇḍam – 16-23\]” (bows are like burning fire), and “*svarga mārga praBhāvanam* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – kiśkindā Kāṇḍam – 17-8\]” (bow that would send one to paramapadam).

**kulam kaḷaindu** – Making the clan of rākśasas to be destroyed. What about viBheeśaṇāḻvān? Is he not a rākṣasa?

Based on viBheeśaṇa’s own thoughts, and based on rāvaṇan calling him as a destroyer of own family (and so does not belong), and based on the thoughts of śrī rāma, śrī viBheeśaṇāḻvān belongs to the clan of īkṣvāhu, that is śrī rāma’s clan.

That is, since rāvaṇan did not listen to his good words, viBheeśaṇa said “*niśācara*! \[śrī rāmāyaṇama – yudḍha Kāṇḍam – 6-26\]” (Oh, rākśasa! (thus separating himself from that clan)). rāvaṇan said “*tvām tu dik kulapāmsanam* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudḍha Kāṇḍam – 16-16\]” (You who has come to destroy the clan of rākśasas is to be hated). perumāḷ asked śrī viBheeśaṇāḻvān, “*rākṣasānām balābalam* \[śrī rāmāyaṇām – yudḍha Kāṇḍam – 19-7\]” (tell me the strength and weakness of rākṣasas); vibheeśaṇāḻvān said that he would help in destroying the rākṣasas – “*rākṣasānām vaḍhēsāhyam* \[śrī rāmāyaṇam – yudḍha Kāṇḍam – 19-23\]” (I will help destroy the rākṣasa army of rāvaṇan); so he does not belong to the clan of rākṣasas.

While only rāvaṇan did the mistake, it was such a heavy one that his whole clan got destroyed. Like how if one is loving towards emperumān, then its benefit reaches all series of people in his chain of relationship, if one performs bad deeds towards Him, then that also would affect all the people in the line of relationship with such person. Anger towards rāvaṇan ended up in destruction of his clan of rākṣasas. Due to the friendship towards mahārājar (sugreevan), the clan of monkeys flourished.

**venṟānai** – Like how He destroyed the clan of rākṣasas without missing anyone, He won the war while making sure not even one from the clan of divine monkeys had any trouble.

**kunṟedutta tōḷinānai** – Making the girls of five lakhs families get the experience by creating desperation in them that they cannot survive without seeing kriṣṇan, by holding the gōvardana mountain as umbrella using the strength of His arm. By this, she asks – Should You who created the longing that does not look for anything other than You, not make me enjoy You?

**viri tirai neer viṇṇagaram maruvi** –  For helping – those who are living after that incarnation, those who send messengers, and those who get involved in quarrel out of love, is why He lives in tiruviṇṇagar.

**viri tirai neer viṇṇagaram** – place that is suitably cool having plenty of water to match the delicateness of ermperumān.

**maruvi** – Leaving paramapadam and living in arcā (statue) form in this material world that causes sorrows, is due to excessive love towards His devotees. For a farmer, it would be apt for him to sleep in the place where water flows to the fields, instead of sleeping inside his house. emperumān is “*patti uḻavan* \[[nānmukan tiruvantādi – 23](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/07/nanmugan-thiruvandhadhi-23/)\]”.

**nāḷum ninṟānai** – Him who is standing till the end of period of kalpa.

**taṇ kudandaik kidanda mālai** – If He is in standing position, one might think that there is some place else He might be going to. Preventing that thought, Him who is lying down in a resting position in tirukkudandai that removes difficulties, like saying that He would not leave until all the people of the material world is fully taken with Him to paramapadam.

What is the reason for His being in this world in the positions of standing, sitting, and lying down?

**mālai** – It is because He is having excessive love towards His devotees.

**nediyānai** – Him who is long; Is it possible for me to talk fully about how much He gives Himself to His devotees? I can only say that He is limitless.

ōr,

**nediyānai** – Him who is easily attainable for others, is being a hard-to-reach-flower for me, she says.

**adi nāyēn ninaindittēnē** – Like how there is no limit to His greatness, there is no limit to my lowliness.

**nāyēn** – Born in this birth (like a dog) which roams into any house having doors open, and being hit and thrown away from each such house entered, owner having to take bath even if he touches out of happy friendliness;

**adināyēn** – Me not having any other place to go than You, such that it is only You who would see me or go away from me, based on your considering of our relationship.  Wheter it is the time of surrendering or of union, for this āḻvār there is only ākincanyam (not having any other source of strength), and sva-dōśa-kyāpanam (presenting own mistakes). That is his greatness.

At the time of surrendering to Him he said, “*nāyēn vandadaindēn* \[[periya tirumoḻi – 1.9.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/06/periya-thirumozhi-1-9-1/)\]. Here when enjoying/experiencing Him, he is saying, “adi-nāyēn”. When this āḻvār is at the stage of getting his blemishes removed and is going to reach Him, why say like this about himself? It is because this āḻvār is such, that he thinks that all the good that happened to him are done by emperumān as instruments for enjoying emperumān.

[nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) finished his divyaprabandam by saying, “adanil periya en avā \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 10.10.11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/03/thiruvaimozhi-10-10-11-avavarachchuzh-ariyai/)\]” , thus saying that his devotion is bigger that the tatvams of chith, acith, and eeśvaran. This āḻvār is finishing his last divya prabandam saying “*adi nāyēn*”.

What is the reason? –

[nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) was free from worldly attachment from the time of birth itself, so getting him to gain more and more devotion towards Him is what emperumān had to do for that āḻvār. So He made that āḻvār to be able to say “*adanil periya en avā* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 10.10.11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/03/thiruvaimozhi-10-10-11-avavarachchuzh-ariyai/)\]”.

[tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) was in the state as he himself said in “*venṟiyē vēṇḍi veeḻ porutkirangi vēṟkaṇār kalaviyē karudi, ninṟavā nillā nenjinai udaiyēn* \[[periya tirumoḻi – 1.1.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/11/periya-thirumozhi-1-1-4/)\], that is, having many blemishes in earlier state. So emperumān grew naicyānusandānam in this āḻvār (thinking about own blemishes and considering self as lowly), and in the end of made him say “adi–nāyēn ninaindittēn”.

**ninaindittēnē** – Earlier in tirumaṇangollai, there is one acceptance He did as He accepted without expecting any qualification from āḻvār. I thought that that grace should be there till the time ātmā lived. In the state of being [parakāla nāyaki](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) the lover, that voluntary grace of emperumān is said as gāndarva type of wedding, that is, natural union. I thought that such natural union with You should exist till the time ātmā exists, is the thought here.

—–

Translation by raghurām śrīnivāsa dāsan.

## 30. 30

*paḻavinaiyai mudalariya vallār tāmē 30*

**Пословный перевод:**

ammān tannai – About sarvēśvaran; veḷippadutta – who brought out; aru maṟaiyai – the vēdas that are difficult to obtain; annam āy – incarnating in the form of bird hamsam (swan); munivarōdu amarar ētta – as saints and dēvas praised; kaliyan – tirumangai āḻvār; mannu mā maṇi māda mangai vēndan – who is the head of tirumangai that has got strong and best buildings; mānam vēl – and who is having the army that is spear; parakālan – and who is like yaman for other philosophies; minnumā maḻai tavaḻum mēga vaṇṇā – Oh One having form like a cloud that is cool and crawling and is with lightning!; viṇṇavar tam perumānē – Oh! the head of nityasūris!; aruḷāy – You should show your mercy (on me)!; enṟu sonna – praying so and divined; panniya – very detailed; tamiḻ nūl – tamiḻ śāstram; mālai – in the form of garland of words; vallār tām – those who can recite it; ariya vallār – they would be able to remove; paḻa vinaiyai – earlier karmas; tollai – of eternal past; mudal – completely along with any scent of it

**Перевод:**

**minnu mā maḻai etc. – Oh One having the form like a rainy cloud that pours beautifully with lightning, thunder, and throwing rainbow! It talks about how like a cloud pouring rain on a dry paddy field, upon hearing the pleading call of āḻvār as “adi nāyēn ninaindittēnē”, emperumān, looking with comforting eyes, came to him and showed Himself, to remove āḻvār’s sorrows.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

In previous pāsuram, the words like “mālai adi nāyēn ninaindittēnE” implied that emperumān was not present then, and now as he addresses Him, “mēgavaṇṇā!”, it could imply that emperumān has come and showed Himself. It is how the anger of love did not help (pāsuram 28), but his appealing with mentioning his blemishes (pāsuram 29), has helped.

[nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) started his divya prabandam with mentioning emperumān’s supremacy, jagatkāraṇatvam (having nature of being creator of the worlds), and finished with jagatkāraṇatvam in “*avāvarac cūḻ ariyai ayanai aranai* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 10.10.11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/03/thiruvaimozhi-10-10-11-avavarachchuzh-ariyai/)\].

This āḻvār started this divya prabandam with emperumān’s form in “*maṇi uruvil bhūdam aindāy*  \[[tiruneduntāṇḍakam – 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thirunedunthandakam-1/)\]”, and is ending the divya prabandam by mentioning His form, as “*minnu mā maḻai tavaḻum mēga vaṇṇā*”.

***viṇṇavar tam perumānē*** – It is talking about nityasūris who live based on the food that is His divine form, fed by emperumān. Oh! You who are giving Yourself for ayarvarum amarar (nityasūris) to enjoy! Is it a greatness for You to give Yourself to them, since it is like a rain pouring on the ocean; please show mercy for me also to get rid of association with the body and see you at all times (in paramapadam), like how nityasūris always see your presence.

**aruḷāy** – Even though āḻvār is at the stage of paramabhakti thus creating highest form of longing for Him, he is not considering that devotion as the means to attain Him like how upāsakas do; he thinks that it is emperumān’s mercy that is the means for attaining him. So he is saying aruḷāy. He is saying, that for getting rid of interest in worldly matters also, His mercy is the means – as he said “*āḻiyān aruḷē* \[[periya tirumoḻi – 1.1.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/11/periya-thirumozhi-1-1-4/)\]“, and for getting the experience of emperumān also, His mercy is the means.

annamāy etc. – āḻvār is giving an example for explaining how emperumān came and gave Himself to āḻvār. Like how He brought out pramāṇam (vēdas) to brahmā, He brought out Himself, the pramēya-Bhūta:, that is, One who is known from that pramāṇam.

*munivarōdu amarar ētta* – Along with riśis, brahmā and others came to emperumān and praised, while saying, “Oh! We have lost the eyes that are the vēdas”. riśis need pramāṇam for their ātmā to attain vellness. For brahmā et al too there is need for pramāṇam, for the activities of creation etc.

**annamāy** – incarnating in the form of swan so that there is no doubt that vēdam is apauruśēyam (not authored by anyone). (If He had directly said the vēdas to them, then a doubt might arise against apauruśēyatvam. Hence He came in this form and told them the vēdas).

**arumaṟai** – vēdam that is difficult to obtain. It is not the pramēyam (emperumān) which was difficult to be attained, but was pramāṇam (vēdas). (For me it was pramēyam that was difficult to obtain; for them it was the pramāṇam).

**veḷippadutta** – Creation and destruction of vēdam is about it being brought out and not brought out.

**ammān tannai** – about sarvēśvaran; He brought out vēdam to them because He is their owner – is the meaning here.

**mannu mā maṇimāda mangai vēndan** – As he sustained himself, the place also became strong, is the thought. tirumangai became a place having buildings with best gems, and which would not be destroyed till end of time of kalpam (a day of brahmā).

**mangai vēndan** – He who fell at birds’ feet, became the ruler of tirumangai.

**mānavēl parakālan** – He became able to hold the spear. Also was able to do kainkaryam (of destroying the enemies of emperumān and devotees). He holds spear due to compassion towards Him.

**māna-vēl** – Even if a commoner holds that spear, it would make him similar to tirumangai āḻvār. So this is about the greatness of the spear. ōr, it could talk about the greatness of āḻvār as ‘māna vēl-para-kālan’ (using spear He is great like yama for enemies).

**parakālan** – Using sword in hand, and by explaining the meanings of vēdas using divya prabandams, he won the opposers, and established the limits instructed by vēdam.

**kaliyan** – Having strength to do these both (sword/divya prabandam). kali – midukku – ~strength. Another meaning is – He who destroyed the problems of kali yugam. He calls himself too as ‘kali-kanṟi’.

**panniya nūl tamiḻ mālai vallār** – panniya – spanned. That is, emperumān’s true nature, form, qualities/attributes, wealth – this divya prabandam spanning all of these, and having all these in it – such divya prabandam. nūl – divya prabandam satisfying all the definitions required of a literature. In the future when someone tries to show the definitions, then they could use this divya prabandam as the reference for that.  tamiḻ – being eligible to be learned by everyone.

**mālai** – For empermān to wear it in His head. This divya prabandam being so.

**tollai etc**. – avidyai that is ignorance, karmas that are good and bad ones, and vāsanai that arises out of habit of these karmas, ruci that is interest in these matters, and association with body which is the cause of the above, – all of these are there in us from eternal past, following us in the form of cause and effect for each other; He says that those who learn this divya prabandam would be able to remove association with them on their own.

**vinai** – effects on self for two deeds in the form of good and bad. These two also show the happiness and anger of emperumān for the respective deeds. These are in the form of those deeds done in previous births, and the ones done in current birth. The ones done in previous births is called pūrvāGam. Ones that are going to be done are called “uttarāGam”. Out of pūrvāGam whatever part has started yielding its effects is called “prārabḍha karmam”.

The karma here also includes “avidyai” – lack of / blemish in true knowledge; it is of three types. They are: “jñāna-anudayam” – not having knowledge, “anyatā-jñānam” – understanding the nature/attribute of something differently, and “vipareeta-jñānam” – understanding something as totally something else.

karma also includes relationship with insentient (body, etc.) that are in the forms of stūla śareeram (gross body), and sūkśma śareeram (subtle body).

karma also includes, vāsanai which is the smell left out of habits that involved these relationships with insentient, and includes the interest/taste of involvement in these insentient matters.

All these are hurdles for a jeevātma to reach paramātmā.

**tollaip paḻavinai** – These karmas are being very old, and not being possible to determine the starting point of its accumulation. These are really very old, is the meaning.

**tāmē** – They would be able to remove completely these hurdles all on their own; they will not even require emperumān’s help (eeśvaranudaiya muḻangait taṇṇeerum vēṇḍā). (This is considering the importance of Him showing grace to us out of happiness for learning this divyaprabandam). That is, for those who learned this divyaprabandam, emperumān’s grace would automatically reach them.

**aruḷāy enṟu – munivarōdu amarar ētta** – Originally the phrase “*minnu mā maḻai tavaḻum mēga vaṇṇā viṇṇavar tam perumāḷē aruḷāy*” is taken as said by [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) to perumāḷ. Instead, if it is taken as part of the phrase munivar and amarar praising emperumān, then it is shown that the original meaning is more apt and better.

[tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) does not have the strength to say openly about his greatness, as “*en avāvarac cūḻndāyē* \[[tiruvāimoḻi – 10.10.10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/03/thiruvaimozhi-10-10-10-suzhndhu-aganru/)\]” like [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), That is why, he divines such that the phrases have to be aligned differently. Also, unlike previous pāsurams, āḻvār is not expressing his longing. He is saying that emperumān’s divine body is like rainy clouds. He is saying that emperumān gave Himself to the enjoyment of nityasūris. He says that emperumān would give him strength to hold the spear. He says he can destroy the enemies. All these talks would be suitable for this āḻvār’s talks only and will not match even the words of nityasūris and munis. So, in this pāsuram, it is clear that āḻvār got emperumān as he wished and got satisfaction in his mind.

So the apt ordering of phrases is – *munivarōdu amarar ētta – annamāy arumaṟaiyai veḷippadutta ammān tannai, mannu mā maṇimāda mangai vēndan mānavēḷ parakālan kaliyan – “minnu mā maḻai tavaḻum mēga vaṇṇa viṇṇāvar tam perumānē aruḷāy” – enṟu sonna – panniya nūl tamiḻ mālai vallār tollaip paḻavinaiyai mudalairiya vallār tāmē* (This is as listed in the word-by-word meaning here).

**——**

Translator’s note – This completes the translation of this divya SAstram. It had been an immersive experience to learn the divine words of āḻvārs and ācaryas, puttūr swami’s vivaraṇam, and upanyāsams of learned swamis, in trying to translate this with proper meanings as much as possible. If there are any corrections needed, kindly feel free to send them.

adiyēn realized that this vyākhyānam grew sweeter and more beautiful as it progressed from pāsuram to pāsuram. This is an experience that everyone could get by the grace of tāyar and emperumān, and ācAryas.

—–

Translation by raghurām śrīnivāsa dāsan.
